# 16 · Same product, our own build: what stays and what changes

This page fixes what "same" and "different" mean for Indus Agent, so no builder has to guess. It is
written from the ten research specs in `research/` and the decisions in `README.md` (P1–P8 and the
lead defaults). Names follow `17-naming-and-glossary.md`; feature areas follow
`03-feature-inventory.md` (cited by area name here, because ids are assigned in parallel).

Working name: **Indus Agent**. CLI `indus`, env prefix `INDUS_`, home `~/.indus`, Python package
`indus`, npm scope `@indus/*`. The name is confirmed for the build but may still change before
launch after a trademark check (D-16.40, owner).

D-ids: `D-16.n` for this doc, `D-04.n` for stack decisions in `04-architecture.md`, `D-15.n` for
roadmap decisions. Every intended difference found while building or in the parity pass gets a row
in §2.7 (or §2.8 if it is a gap) and is cited by the parity ledger and the acceptance map. A
difference with no id is a bug or an open question. Agents propose, the owner or lead decides, and
the date is recorded in §3.

---

## 1. What stays the same (we deliberately match the reference)

| Area | Stays the same |
|---|---|
| **Product idea** | A self-improving personal agent: one runtime that chats, runs tools on a chosen machine, remembers, writes its own skills, schedules work, and is reachable from a terminal, an editor, a browser, a desktop app and messaging apps. |
| **Architecture** | Python owns all agent behaviour (turn loop, providers, tools, state, learning, cron, gateway). TypeScript front ends (TUI, web, desktop) own only presentation and talk to Python over one declared JSON-RPC contract. One SQLite store per profile is the source of truth for conversations. A per-profile gateway process is, in the end, the single owner of execution for every surface (P1 says when). See `04-architecture.md`. |
| **Information architecture** | Terminal: transcript above, live work dock, status rule, multiline composer, modal cards for approvals and pickers. Desktop: left sidebar (sessions, projects, profile rail), central tabbed panes, optional right panes (preview, files, git, terminal), bottom status bar, route overlays. Web: left navigation of admin pages, a chat page that embeds the real TUI in a browser terminal with a narrow side rail. Messaging: one conversation slot per chat, slash commands, inline approve/deny. |
| **Core flows** | Install → `indus setup` (provider, key, model, backend, tools) → `indus` opens a chat → the agent streams text, calls tools, asks before risky commands → the session is saved and can be resumed, searched, titled, branched, compressed → memory and skills grow between sessions → cron jobs and messaging bots reuse the same agent. |
| **Feel** | Keyboard-first and dense in the terminal; streaming from the first token; interrupt, steer and queue always available; nothing lost on crash (user message persisted before any model call, replies ledgered before delivery); cheap long sessions (byte-stable system prompt for provider caches). |
| **Behavioural contracts** | Strict role alternation and one tool result per tool call on every failure path; persist-before-effect; approval floors that no mode can bypass; default-deny messaging authorization; at-least-once delivery with ambiguity labelled; at-most-once cron firing; curator archives and never deletes; frozen memory snapshot per session. |
| **Third-party protocols** | Kept exact where they are someone else's standard: OpenAI Chat Completions and Responses, Anthropic Messages, Bedrock Converse, Gemini, MCP, ACP, JSON-RPC 2.0, SSE, OAuth device code (RFC 8628) and PKCE, `SKILL.md` frontmatter as published by agentskills.io, platform bot APIs. |
| **Cron grammar** | The reference grammar is kept on purpose (P6): a bare duration repeats, `in <dur>` runs once, cron expressions and ISO timestamps also accepted. |

## 2. What changes (on purpose)

### 2.1 Identity: required, non-negotiable

| Reference | Indus Agent |
|---|---|
| Product name, CLI, Python package, console scripts, plugin entry-point group, user agent string | Our own: `indus`, package `indus`, entry-point group `indus.plugins`, UA `indus/<version>` (full map in `17-naming-and-glossary.md`) |
| Home dir, env prefix, file names (persona file, project context file, store file, auth file) | `~/.indus`, `INDUS_*`; persona file `PERSONA.md`, project file `INDUS.md` / `.indus.md`; our own store and auth file names. Third-party convention files (`AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, cursor rules) are still read because they belong to other tools, not to the reference. |
| Toolset names, metric names, deep-link scheme, IPC prefix, bridge global, HTTP headers | Our own (`indus-cli` style toolsets, `indus://` scheme, our own channel prefix and header names) |
| Logo, icons, favicons, sprites, sounds, desktop pet art | Made or licensed by us |
| Palette, theme tokens, theme and skin names, fonts, UI kit | Our own design system (`packages/ui`); OFL fonts; no vendor UI-kit package, no vendor fonts or theme preset names (R09.22) |
| All prompt text the model sees (identity, guidance blocks, nudges, summaries, cron hint) | Written fresh; same purpose, our words (lead default) |
| All UI copy, onboarding, help, errors, playful strings (spinner verbs, faces, fortunes), pairing replies | Written fresh in our own voice (lead default) |
| Bundled and optional skills, plugin examples, locale catalogues | Authored by us from scratch (D-16.33); none copied or translated from the reference |
| Contributor attribution data | Not copied; starts empty (R10.29) |

### 2.2 Protocol and storage: own names, not wire- or DB-compatible

**Decision D-16.1:** Indus Agent is **not wire-compatible** with the reference. We name our own
JSON-RPC methods, events, server-request kinds, error codes, REST routes, WebSocket paths,
control-socket verbs, IPC channels, ticket formats and ID prefixes. A reference client cannot talk
to our backend and the reverse. Why: matching would force us to mirror the reference's concrete
schemas (its expression, not its ideas), would import its known flaws (error codes that mean
different things per method, R05.15), and would tie our release cadence to theirs.

**Decision D-16.2:** Indus Agent is **not storage-compatible** with the reference. Our SQLite store
has its own table, column, index and trigger names, its own message-content codec and its own
FTS projection; our config keys, `.env` names (except vendor-standard platform credentials,
D-16.22), auth file layout, cron job store, skill usage sidecars, delivery ledger and spill layout
are our own. We do not open, read or migrate a reference home in 1.0. Why: compatibility would
pin us to the reference's column order and 30+ schema versions, and the clean-room rule is easier
to keep when no file format is shared.

Optional later (post-1.0, owner's call, D-16.41): a one-way importer that reads a *copy* of a
reference home (sessions JSONL export, memory files, skills) and writes our formats, built from a
field-mapping spec, never from reference code.

Our protocol rules (own design, recorded so builders do not drift):
- One contract, declared once in Python, with generated TypeScript types and a JSON Schema
  document; CI fails when generated files are stale (D-04.3).
- Error codes have one meaning across all methods, listed in one table (D-16.27).
- The same method names serve every transport (stdio NDJSON for the TUI child and ACP-internal use,
  WebSocket for the daemon, desktop and dashboard).

### 2.3 Fresh start, so no legacy baggage

We start on the reference's *final* behaviour and skip every path that exists only to carry old
installs forward:

- **Config migrations.** The reference stamps `config.yaml` with a version (current 51, support
  floor 12) and runs ordered migration steps. We start our stamp at 1 with an empty migration list;
  the hook exists so our own later changes can use it. Legacy key moves (root-level turn cap,
  single-fallback key, list-shaped custom providers, old "systems" shape) are not read.
- **Retired-model and exporter migrations** (`migrate` subcommands for retired vendor models, a
  relay exporter env family, a runtime switch for one vendor) are not built.
- **Store schema history.** The reference's schema has ~32 versions, a storage-layout upgrade path
  and chunked data migrations. Our store starts at schema 1 on the final layout, with declarative
  column reconcile from day one (new columns need no version bump) and chunked, time-boxed
  migrations only for our own future changes (R03.15 still applies to us).
- **Old updater surface.** The reference keeps a stable import surface so an old updater can
  replace itself (R04.24) and an interpreter-bridge marker so installs on older Pythons can reach
  their updater (R10.12). We require one interpreter line (D-04 stack) and run the updater from the
  installed release, so neither trick is needed. Post-pull syntax check and rollback stay.
- **Legacy entry points.** No second "legacy single-query runner" console script; one `indus` CLI
  plus `indus acp` as a subcommand (a thin `indus-acp` shim only if editor registries need a
  separate binary).
- **Two-generation transports.** The reference TUI carries a legacy stdio backend next to a newer
  shared-daemon WebSocket path (R05.1), and ACP carries a legacy in-process class next to a gateway
  viewer (R09.13). We build each mode once, explicitly, behind one interface (D-16.10, D-16.29).
- **Imports from other products.** The OpenClaw import (`claw migrate`) is not built (D-16.50).
  Importing another coding agent's instruction and permission files stays in scope as a feature
  (P24), not as a migration.
- **Small compat paths skipped:** legacy checkpoint store cleanup, legacy comment-only config
  scaffold upgrade, legacy sibling-backup sweeps, label-based lookup of old Daytona sandboxes,
  profile identity migration, the empty compatibility extras in packaging, "nightly" channel naming
  (we say canary everywhere, R10.27), one-time desktop secret-store migration.
- **Capability negotiation** (`client.capabilities`, server feature flags) exists from P04 but
  starts empty; every flag added later is ours.

### 2.4 Scope: full parity, in phase order

The lead set **full parity** as the 1.0 target. "v1" below means "in 1.0", with the phase that
delivers it (`15-roadmap.md`). "Later" means after 1.0 or still open with the owner.

| Feature area | Reference | Indus 1.0 | Later |
|---|---|---|---|
| Agent turn loop, prompt assembly, steer/redirect/interrupt | Full | **Full** (P04, P08) | — |
| Model providers | 42 bundled profiles, 5 API modes, OAuth routes incl. impersonating ones, vendor portal | **API-key and documented OAuth/device-code providers**, Chat Completions + Anthropic Messages (P03), Responses + Bedrock + Gemini native (P08), credential pools, fallback chain (P08). Bundled list per D-16.42. | Subprocess "external agent" transport (P24 if owner agrees) |
| Subscription OAuth that impersonates another client | Yes | **No** (D-16.11) | — |
| First-party hosted portal provider, free tier, top-up, subscription commands | Yes | **No** (D-16.12) | — |
| Context compression, prompt caching, usage/cost | Full | **Full** (P08) | — |
| Mixture-of-agents, side questions, fast mode, goals/loops/heartbeat | Yes | **Yes** (P08 fast mode; P10 MoA, side questions, goals/loops) | — |
| Core tools: file, terminal, process, web, todo, clarify | Yes | **Yes** (P05) | — |
| Delegation, code execution, browser, vision, image/video gen, TTS/STT, voice mode | Yes | **Yes** (P10) | — |
| Computer use, LSP, worktrees, verify | Yes | **Yes** (P10 computer use; P11 worktrees; P24 LSP, verify) | — |
| Terminal backends | 7 + managed Modal gateway | **Local, Docker, SSH** (P05/P11); **Singularity, Modal (direct), Daytona, Vercel Sandbox** (P21) | Managed Modal gateway: never (D-16.16) |
| Background processes, checkpoints/rollback | Yes | **Yes** (P11) | — |
| Approvals (manual, smart, yolo, allowlists, floors) | Yes | **Yes** (P05 manual + floors; P08 smart pre-judge) | — |
| Sessions store, FTS search, titles, usage, export/import, prune, repair | Yes | **Yes** (P02, repair/recover tools P07) | Reference-store importer (D-16.41) |
| Classic CLI REPL, slash commands, one-shot modes | Yes | **Yes** (P06) | — |
| CLI management (setup, model, auth, config, sessions, doctor, backup, logs, uninstall, update) | ~70 commands | **Yes** (P07; update and uninstall finished in P23) | — |
| Profiles | Yes, plus multiplexed serving | **Yes** (P01); multiplexed serving P24 | — |
| Memory, skills, curator, background review, learn/refine, learning journey | Yes | **Yes** (P09) | — |
| External memory providers | Plugin-shaped | **Yes** (P13, via plugins) | — |
| MCP client, plugins, hooks, skills hub | Yes | **Yes** (P13) | Curated plugin catalog, out-of-process plugin host (D-16.45, D-16.46) |
| Cron | Yes | **Yes** (P14; ticker moves into gateway in P15) | — |
| TUI | Ink fork, shared-daemon WS default | **Upstream Ink** (D-16.28), stdio child first (P12), daemon attach (P17) | — |
| Messaging gateway | ~30 adapters, API server, webhooks, relay, hosted rooms, multiplexing | **Telegram, Discord, Slack + API server + webhook** (P15); **all other chat adapters** (P16); multiplexing, hosted rooms, relay, A2A (P24) | — |
| Kanban board, workers, projects | Yes | **Yes** (P24) | — |
| Shared daemon as backend for every surface, turn lease, canonical wire | Yes (default) | **Yes** (P17, parity path) | — |
| Web dashboard | ~20 pages + embedded TUI chat | **Yes** (P18); login providers per D-16.44 | Dashboard plugins beyond the SDK basics (owner) |
| ACP adapter | Gateway viewer (+ legacy in-process class) | **Yes** (P19), one implementation per mode (D-16.29) | — |
| MCP server mode | 10 tools, two hollow | **8 working tools**; approval tools only once wired to the daemon (D-16.30) (P19) | — |

| Research tooling (batch, trajectories, compression, evals) | Yes | **Yes** (P22) | — |

### 2.5 Visual differences: siblings, not twins

**Superseded for look and layout by D-16.1190 (owner, 2026-10-24):** the web dashboard and the
desktop app follow the reference's layout, components, spacing, type style and light/dark colours;
the clean room for code and assets below still holds (own wordmark, open fonts, no reference assets).

Keep the **layout, density and interaction model** of every surface: the same regions in the same
places, the same keys for the same actions where the action is a convention (Ctrl+C order,
Esc-Esc, Enter vs Shift+Enter), the same card-based approvals.

Change the **surface**:
- **Palette.** Our own tokens on `:root` and in the TUI theme file: a warm clay/terracotta accent
  with deep teal secondary on neutral slate, not the reference's colours. Light and dark variants
  of each, contrast-checked (WCAG AA) in CI for web and desktop.
- **Type.** OFL fonts only (for example Inter for UI text and JetBrains Mono for code), bundled with
  the licence file.
- **Shape and motion.** Our own radius scale, elevation, focus rings and motion timings.
- **Iconography and art.** A licensed open icon set (for example Lucide, ISC) plus our own logo,
  empty-state art, tray icons, sprites. No reference images.
- **TUI.** Our own default theme and theme names, our own prompt glyph, banner and spinner set;
  same status-rule information at the same width breakpoints.
- **Words.** Every label longer than a word or two is rewritten; short conventional labels
  ("Save", "Cancel", "Settings") may coincide.

A parity screenshot comparison must show the same regions in the same places with clearly
different branding.

### 2.6 Small improvements we may add (owner's call)

1. Stable, documented error-code table across the protocol (adopted as D-16.27).
2. Batch runner checkpoints bound to a dataset hash (adopted as D-16.31).
3. Dashboard "effective config" view that prints where each value came from (default, file, env,
   admin overlay) — helps the reference's own docs-vs-defaults confusion (R10.4). Proposed for P18.

### 2.7 Intended differences

Seeded from the product decisions (P1–P8), the lead defaults and the security items the specs
raised. Builders add rows as task notes decide new ones; the "Decided in" column then names the
note (`docs/notes/Pnn/<slug>.md`).

| ID | Area | Reference | Indus 1.0 | Decided in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D-16.10 | Runtime / surfaces | Classic chat and TUI attach to a per-profile shared daemon by default; the REPL never runs the agent itself (R04.1, R05.1) | **In-process first** (P1): the classic CLI runs the agent in its own process; the TUI spawns a Python child over stdio. Both talk to one `SessionService` interface. The per-profile gateway daemon arrives in P17 and then becomes the default (parity path) for every surface, with in-process kept as `--local` | README P1 |
| D-16.11 | Providers / auth | Subscription OAuth routes that present another vendor's client identity, headers and renamed tools | **API keys and officially documented OAuth/device-code flows only** (P2). No identity lines, headers or tool renames claiming another product | README P2 |
| D-16.12 | Providers | First-party hosted "portal" inference provider with free tier, top-up and subscription commands | **Dropped** (P3); no portal provider, no account commands, no portal login on the dashboard | README P3 |
| D-16.13 | Secrets | `.env` and auth file on disk; desktop secrets plain 0600 by default, keychain opt-in | **OS keychain by default**, falling back to owner-only 0600 files under 0700 dirs (P4). Availability is probed without triggering interactive prompts; a keychain that errors or prompts is marked unusable for that run and we fall back (guards R08.10) | README P4 |
| D-16.14 | Distribution | Installer, Docker, Nix, Termux, MSIX; wheel builds blocked | **Same channels, no PyPI** (P5). Installer scripts, Docker, Nix in 1.0; Termux and MSIX later | README P5 |
| D-16.15 | Distribution | Termux APT and MSIX/Store in the main release flow | **Later**: built in P24 only if capacity allows, else post-1.0 | README P5 |
| D-16.16 | Terminal backends | Seven backends plus a vendor-hosted managed Modal gateway | **Local, Docker, SSH first; the other four later behind the same contract** (P7). Managed Modal gateway dropped; Modal runs in direct mode with the user's own credentials | README P7 |
| D-16.17 | Gateway | Multiplexed profiles on by default; hosted rooms | **Multiplex available, off by default**: one profile per gateway process unless `gateway.multiplex_profiles: true`, then `gateway run` claims the user's host lock and serves every live profile of the root in one process (P24-01..04, wired in wave 42); hosted rooms are driven by the gateway's room loop (P24-06) | README P8 |
| D-16.18 | Desktop security | A few IPC handlers check the calling frame | **Every IPC handler checks its sender** (app origin, main frame, known window) and rejects otherwise | Lead default |
| D-16.19 | Desktop security | No CSP found for the chat renderer; fuses not configured in source | **Strict CSP** on every renderer page; **Electron fuses set** (no RunAsNode, no NODE_OPTIONS or inspect args, ASAR integrity, load only from ASAR, cookie encryption on) | Lead default |
| D-16.20 | Desktop / web security | Guest content mostly guarded; some paths reach the window-open handler (R08.8) | **Untrusted content can never open URLs**: deny-all window-open in guest frames, external opens only from a trusted user click, http/https only | Lead default |
| D-16.21 | Research tooling | Trajectory compressor loads its tokenizer with remote code enabled (R10.10) | **Remote code off by default**; an explicit flag opts in and prints a warning | Lead default |
| D-16.22 | Config / env | Platform credentials unprefixed (`TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN`) | **Same vendor-standard names** for platform credentials; every product variable uses `INDUS_` | Lead default |
| D-16.23 | Copy | Reference prompt text, UI copy, playful strings, bundled skills | **All written fresh**; nothing carried over | Lead default |
| D-16.24 | Docs vs code | The reference's docs and code disagree in many places | **We follow the code.** Known cases: project-context AGENTS chain walks git root → cwd; compression threshold arithmetic per code (R01.7); curator stale 14 d / archive 30 d / interval 168 h (R07.7); Docker PID limit 2048 and persistent containers left running for reuse (R02.23); delegation defaults 10 children / depth 1; process tool is one tool with actions; MCP tool names use the double-underscore prefix (R07.21); TUI transport per D-16.10 not per docs; approval quick-picks are digits (R05.11). Each builder records further cases here | Lead default |
| D-16.25 | Web / ACP auth | Non-loopback dashboard fails closed; an "insecure" flag lingers as a no-op; ACP viewer trusts the local socket | **Fail closed everywhere**: the dashboard refuses to start on a non-loopback bind or public URL without a login provider; there is no insecure flag at all; loopback tokens are rejected in gated mode; every WebSocket upgrade goes through one auth helper; ACP and every other local client attach to the daemon only with a single-use ticket over an owner-only socket | Specs R09.2, R09.3, R08.4 |
| D-16.26 | Gateway API server | Loopback API server may run without a key (open question 06 Q13) | The OpenAI-compatible API server **always requires a bearer key**, loopback included; `indus gateway api-key` creates one | Lead, accepted 2026-10-18 (§3.3) |
| D-16.27 | Protocol | Error codes reused with different meanings per method (R05.15) | **One error-code table** with stable meanings, generated into TS | Spec R05.15 |
| D-16.28 | TUI | Heavily forked Ink renderer and a TS port of Yoga | **Upstream Ink** as a dependency; missing capabilities (synchronized output gating, kitty keyboard, mouse, selection) added as our own small modules around it. Trial and fallback in D-04.2 | Task brief / spec 05 |
| D-16.29 | ACP | One constructor silently returns either a gateway viewer or a legacy in-process agent; mode advertising inconsistent (R09.13) | **Two explicit modes**: `indus acp` attaches to the profile daemon (default once P17 lands) or runs `--local` in-process; both advertise the same session modes | Spec R09.13; P19-03/04 (`docs/notes/P19/acp-local-host.md`, `acp-attached-host.md`) |
| D-16.30 | MCP server mode | Two approval tools advertised but never populated (R09.10) | **Not advertised** until they are wired to the daemon's approval queue; then real | Spec R09.10; built in `docs/notes/P19/mcp-serve-approvals.md` |
| D-16.31 | Research tooling | Checkpoint reuse by run name even when the dataset changed; tool names by position; two trajectory writers; timed-out samples silently dropped (R10.3, R10.6–R10.8) | Checkpoint **bound to a dataset hash**; tool responses matched **by call id** (position as fallback); **one shared converter**; a **rejects file** for dropped samples | Spec 10 |
| D-16.32 | Config | Three config loaders with different defaults (R06.25, 04 §3.11.1) | **One loader, one defaults table**, with a presence query ("did the user set this key?") for presence-sensitive readers | Spec R06.25 |
| D-16.33 | Skills | 58 bundled skills plus optional catalogue | **Our own starter set**, written fresh (size per D-16.43) | Lead default |
| D-16.34 | Packaging | Core deps carry an interpreter marker so old Pythons install with no deps (R10.12); lint config shadowed and versions mismatched (R10.13) | **One interpreter line, one lint/type config, one target version**; pinning policy enforced literally by CI (D-16.52) | Spec 10 |
| D-16.35 | Approvals | Reader falls back to manual while shipped config writes smart (02 Q13) | **One default from one table** (value per D-16.53) | Spec R02.23 |
| D-16.36 | Cron grammar | Bare duration repeats; `in <dur>` once (kept, P6). Doc example "every 1d at 09:00" may not parse | Kept; **"every Nd at HH:MM" is accepted** and means daily at that time (proposed, D-16.54) | README P6 |
| D-16.37 | Messaging tools | Delivery is a library, not a model tool (R02.26) | Same; recorded so no builder "adds" a send tool | Spec R02.26 |
| D-16.38 | Dashboard chat | Chat tab embeds the real TUI over PTY; on native Windows tries ConPTY then falls back to a banner (R09.19) | Embed the TUI the same way (R09.1). **Windows: ConPTY via the PTY library; if it cannot start, show one clear "use WSL2" notice**; documented once | Spec R09.19 |
| D-16.39 | Plugins | Hook names generic; entry-point group branded | Generic hook names kept (they describe behaviour); entry-point group `indus.plugins` | Spec 01 §2 |
| D-16.70 | Desktop distribution (Windows) | Windows desktop app ships as MSIX/Store packages in the main release flow | **Signed NSIS installer** built by electron-builder at 1.0 (Authenticode); MSIX/Store deferred (D-16.15) | Lead (reconciliation, 2026-10-18); P23-16 |
| D-16.71 | Voice / TTS | Default text-to-speech goes to a keyless third-party service | **Default local engine is a permissively licensed engine from the licence allow-list**, named in the task note; hosted engines are opt-in with the user's key | Lead; P10-17 |
| D-16.72 | Prompt caching | Byte-stable prompt and tool array on every turn (R01.1) | Same, with **one documented exception**: mixture-of-agents turns get no byte-stable cache sidecar | Lead; P10-21 |
| D-16.73 | Tools | Built-in tools found by scanning modules (AST discovery) | **Explicit list of built-in tool modules** in `tools/builtin/__init__.py`; an optional tool's import error is logged and skipped | P04-08, `docs/notes/P04/tool-registry.md` |
| D-16.74 | Toolsets | Platform bundles are static lists | **Bundles also include registry-registered members** of a static set (R02.25) | P04-11, `docs/notes/P04/toolsets.md` |
| D-16.75 | Terminal config | `TERMINAL_*` environment variables bridge config into backends | **No env bridge**: backend selection and terminal settings are read from the call's `Scope` config (supersedes the earlier `INDUS_TERMINAL_*` proposal) | P05-04 (`docs/notes/P05/env-registry.md`) |
| D-16.76 | Approvals | Dangerous-command patterns as a hand-kept regex list | **Patterns authored from our own command corpus**, with verdict tests; nothing transcribed (R02.23) | P05-07 (`docs/notes/P05/approval-detect.md`: token-level rules on a parsed command, 590+ corpus rows) |
| D-16.77 | Bang shell | `!cmd` runs even when the approval guard fails to load | **Refuses to run** when the guard cannot load (fail closed, R04.27) | P06-11 |
| D-16.78 | Auth store | Auth file on disk holds tokens and keys | **`auth.json` holds metadata only**; tokens and keys live in the `SecretStore` (keychain first, D-16.13) | P07-06 |
| D-16.79 | Backups | Backup zip includes the secrets the home holds | **Keychain secrets are not exported**; the command warns and lists the entries to re-enter; `.env` secrets stay in the zip at 0600 | P07-22 |
| D-16.80 | CLI | A `login` command for the hosted portal | **No `indus login`**; the unknown-command hint points to `indus auth add` | P07-08 |
| D-16.81 | Desktop | GPU child crash can lead to running without the renderer sandbox | **The sandbox is never disabled automatically**; one bounded relaunch with GPU off | P20 (resolver/lifecycle task notes) |
| D-16.82 | Desktop backend resolver | Resolver can fall back to a payload bundled inside the app | **No bundled-payload rung** in 1.0: one variant, installer-driven | P20 (resolver task note) |
| D-16.83 | ACP | Adapter falls back to an in-process agent when no daemon is reachable | **`acp.allow_local_fallback` defaults to false**: without a daemon, `session/new` fails with a clear message unless `--local` or the flag is set | P19-03 (`docs/notes/P19/acp-local-host.md`) |
| D-16.84 | Desktop builds | Windows test builds packaged per the release flow | **Unsigned NSIS installer plus portable ZIP** during the build (P20-38); signing at 1.0 per D-16.70 | P20-38 |
| D-16.89 | Desktop transport | Desktop mints a one-use grant per HTTP attempt against the daemon (R08.4) | Desktop speaks JSON-RPC over the daemon WebSocket (single-use tickets per dial) and sends REST to the loopback `indus serve` it starts or finds with `indus serve ensure` (P18), using a launch-scoped session token held in the main process; **no per-request HTTP grant** | Lead ruling; P18-01, P20-10 |
| D-16.93 | Import | A backup member whose path leaves the home is skipped and the rest restored | **The whole archive is refused before anything is written**; restored files are 0600, dirs 0700 | P07-23 (`docs/notes/P07/import.md`) |
| D-16.94 | Device-code sign-in | `slow_down` adds 1 s to the polling interval | **Adds 5 s, as RFC 8628 requires** (a flow may document another step); waits run on the injected clock | P07-09 (`docs/notes/P07/device-code.md`) |
| D-16.95 | Config commands | `config get/show` skip masking when log redaction is switched off; `config check` always exits 0; `config migrate` writes at once | **Masking always applies unless `--raw`**; `check` exits 1 on errors (a file newer than the build is one); `migrate` is a dry run unless `--apply` | P07-11, P07-13 (`docs/notes/P07/config-read.md`, `config-check.md`) |
| D-16.96 | Session export | Redaction only on request | **Exports mask secrets by default** (`--no-redact` keeps them) | P07-14 (`docs/notes/P07/sessions-read.md`) |
| D-16.97 | Shell completion | Hand-kept per-shell scripts with static command lists | **Click's dynamic completion protocol**: commands, profiles, session titles and toolsets complete from live data | P07-25 (`docs/notes/P07/completion.md`) |
| D-16.98 | Interactive prompts | Without a terminal, prompts fall back to `getpass` or return the default; EOF keeps the default; setup cancel exits 0 | **No terminal → guidance and exit 1, never a prompt** (R04.19); EOF counts as cancel; a cancelled `indus setup` exits 1; first-run guidance goes to stderr | P07-01, P07-02, P07-03 (`docs/notes/P07/prompts.md`, `setup-wizard.md`, `first-run.md`) |
| D-16.99 | Profiles | A clone copies the credential file; `default` can be renamed (display name) | **Keychain entries are copied by name, credential-pool secrets and `auth.json` never** (sign-ins stay with their profile); `default` cannot be created, renamed or deleted | P07-16 (`docs/notes/P07/profile-cmds.md`), lead at P07 landing |
| D-16.100 | Logs | `logs -f` keeps reading the old handle after rotation | **Follow reopens the file after rotation or truncation**; bad options are usage errors (exit 2) | P07-20 (`docs/notes/P07/logs.md`) |
| D-16.101 | Prompt caching | Cache markers on Claude routes the reference recognises, including some it reaches through other wires | **Markers only for Claude models on the Anthropic wire or OpenRouter, or a route that declares or configures an opt-in**; `prompt_caching.cache_ttl: auto` is 1 h when a person drives the session, 5 m for automated sources | P08-07 (`docs/notes/P08/cache-plan.md`), lead at P08 landing |
| D-16.102 | Providers | Official OpenAI hosts use the Responses API | **The bundled `openai` profile stays on chat completions until live Responses captures exist** (D-04.5); custom providers on official OpenAI hosts and `api_mode: responses` use the Responses transport | P08-09 (`docs/notes/P08/responses-transport.md`) |
| D-16.103 | Providers / keys | A generic OpenAI key can reach other vendors' native endpoints through shared resolution | **Gemini and Bedrock never send a key read from `OPENAI_API_KEY`**; the bundled `gemini` profile reads `GEMINI_API_KEY` / `GOOGLE_API_KEY` only; Bedrock uses its bearer token or the AWS credential chain | P08-10, P08-11 (`docs/notes/P08/gemini-transport.md`, `bedrock-transport.md`) |
| D-16.104 | Cost | Billing route chosen from provider names plus host checks, live price catalogues | **Price by request host only**: a vendor's list price applies only on that vendor's host over HTTPS/443, an aggregator's reported charge is `actual`, a loopback server is `included`, everything else is `unknown` — never shown as `$0.00`; prices come from our own table (`pricing_table.yaml`), never fetched at run time | P08-08 (`docs/notes/P08/usage-cost.md`) |
| D-16.105 | Auxiliary models | `auto` side-task routing walks every provider with a key | **`auto` is the session's own route and model**; only configured lists are walked (`auxiliary.<task>.fallback`, then `fallback_providers`); HTTP 402 falls through | P08-17 (`docs/notes/P08/aux-client.md`) |
| D-16.106 | Context references | — (no `@file:`-style expansion) | **`@file:` / `@folder:` / `@diff` / `@git:` / `@url:` expand before the turn is stored**; credential files are refused with a visible note; the expansion fits in the room left under the compression trigger (an item that does not fit becomes a pointer) | P08-19 (`docs/notes/P08/context-references.md`), lead at P08 landing |
| D-16.107 | Turn outcome | A response the provider reports as cancelled surfaces as a provider failure | **It ends the turn `interrupted_by_system`**, with no failure attached | P08-09, lead at P08 landing |
| D-16.108 | `search_files` | Content results in ripgrep's parallel discovery order | **Content results in path order** (`--sort=path`), so `offset` paging never repeats or skips a hit; file-name search keeps the fast discovery order | Lead at P08 landing (flake root cause, `docs/notes/P05/search-files.md`) |
| D-16.109 | Credential pool | A credential is looked up from the pool as requests need one; subagent tasks take their own leases | **Selection is sticky per session**: the chosen credential serves every request until it fails; `request_count` counts selections, not requests; subagents share the parent's live `PoolSet` | P08-12 (`docs/notes/P08/pool-runtime.md`), lead at P08 wave 2 landing |
| D-16.110 | Credential pool | The "same key once more" step for a plain 429 sits in the agent's recovery code around the call | **It runs inside P03's retry loop**: a classifier wrapper makes the second 429 in a row (or the first plan-limit / billing answer) non-retryable only when another credential is usable; with one key P03's normal retries run | P08-12 (`docs/notes/P08/pool-runtime.md`), lead at P08 wave 2 landing |
| D-16.111 | Fallback chain | Falls back early on several fallback-worthy verdicts when the pool cannot recover | **Eager fallback only for `upstream_rate_limit`** (an aggregator's 429 no key can fix); every other trigger lets P03's retries and the pool run first | P08-13 (`docs/notes/P08/fallback-chain.md`), lead at P08 wave 2 landing |
| D-16.112 | Credential pool | A sole credential's cooldown is cut to a short window for non-billing failures | **No short cap**: the cooldown stays as the policy table says (1 h unless the provider names a reset), but a request is never refused while every key cools down: it goes out on the bound key and a failure moves on to the fallback chain | P08-12 (`docs/notes/P08/pool-runtime.md`), lead at P08 wave 2 landing |
| D-16.113 | Fast mode | Fast-mode support decided by model-name rules in code; extra modes and a learned "no fast capacity" switch | **The priority tier comes only from the provider profile's declaration** (`fast_body` / `fast_headers` / `fast_models`, exact model ids from the vendor's docs) and is sent only to that profile's own host; modes `normal` / `fast` / `auto`; the `/fast` choice lives in the process (a resumed session starts from `agent.fast_mode`) | P08-15 (`docs/notes/P08/fast-mode.md`), lead at P08 wave 2 landing |
| D-16.114 | Smart approvals | A judge DENY with nobody to ask blocks the command; only the bare verdict word is accepted | **A judge DENY always reaches the person with once / deny only** (contexts with nobody to ask are settled before the judge runs); one trailing full stop is forgiven, anything else escalates; comments are stripped shell-aware (`#` inside a word or after `\` stays) | P08-14 (`docs/notes/P08/smart-approvals.md`), lead at P08 wave 2 landing |
| D-16.115 | Smart approvals | The denial breaker text can be added to a person-timeout message even when the judge never denied | **The breaker addendum follows a judge denial only**, the tally lives with the session's `ApprovalState` (at most 256 sessions), and a person's timeout or an undeliverable prompt never counts | P08-14 (`docs/notes/P08/smart-approvals.md`), lead at P08 wave 2 landing |
| D-16.120 | Docker env | Forwarded variable names are taken as configured | **`terminal.docker_forward_env` can never re-allow a credential Indus manages** (provider keys, `INDUS_*`); values ride the client's environment only, never `-e NAME=value` (R02.7, R02.22) | P11-04 (`docs/notes/P11/docker-env.md`) |
| D-16.121 | Docker probes | Resource and storage checks may start a throwaway container (and so pull an image) | **Probes never pull**: cgroup and storage facts come from `docker info`, every run/inspect uses `--pull=never`; only definite answers are cached | P11-01 (`docs/notes/P11/docker-probe.md`) |
| D-16.122 | Docker exec | `docker exec -w <cwd>`; container home `/root` as the default cwd; stop ends the local client | **No `-w` on exec** (the wrapper changes directory, so a removed dir cannot fail the start); **default cwd `/workspace`**; a stop signals the command **inside the container** (TERM, ordered client kill, KILL) | P11-05 (`docs/notes/P11/docker-environment.md`) |
| D-16.123 | Docker recovery | Without persistence a vanished container keeps failing until the idle reap; recovery matches its patterns anywhere | **Degraded result + eviction** when the container is gone and persist-across-processes is off; recovery patterns must open the output, so a command that prints them is not mistaken for a lost container | P11-06 (`docs/notes/P11/docker-recovery.md`) |
| D-16.124 | Docker reaper | Stopped containers swept after minutes; running ones left alone | **Stopped longer than 24 h** are removed; **running session containers** whose session is over (host-answered, fail-safe toward keeping) and that run no job are removed too; the profile-wide container is never removed while it runs | P11-07 (`docs/notes/P11/docker-reaper.md`), host wiring at P11 landing |
| D-16.125 | Docker images | A changed default image keeps using the old sandbox | **A new image means a new container** (the image is in the label fingerprint); the old one stays until the reaper's 24 h rule | P11-05 (`docs/notes/P11/docker-environment.md`) |
| D-16.126 | Docker isolation | Any host bind turns approval prompts back on | **Only writable host binds and the bound host cwd count**: read-only volumes and Indus's own read-only skill/credential mounts keep the container isolated (deny rules and the hardline floor apply either way, R02.4, R02.6) | P11-03 (`docs/notes/P11/docker-mounts.md`) |
| D-16.127 | SSH sockets | Control sockets named from a short target prefix in the shared temp dir, globbed at cleanup | **`<sha256(user@host:port)[:16]>[-<sha8 of SendEnv names>]`** in `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`, the profile socket dir or `/tmp`; a socket dir owned by someone else is refused, a loose one tightened; sockets are remembered, never globbed | P11-08 (`docs/notes/P11/ssh-client.md`) |
| D-16.128 | SSH home | Remote home detected once, then guessed | **Detection retried before a later command, never sooner than 30 s after a failure** | P11-09 (`docs/notes/P11/ssh-environment.md`) |
| D-16.129 | SSH sync | Remote files synced back at cleanup | **SSH never syncs back** (upload-oriented, R02.31; sync-back for SDK backends is P21-10); the remote snapshot file is removed at cleanup | P11-09, P11-10 (`docs/notes/P11/ssh-environment.md`, `remote-sync.md`) |
| D-16.130 | Remote jobs | One `kill <pid>` to a background job in a sandbox; any poll error means lost | **Ordered group kill** (the job leads its own process group: TERM, grace, KILL); transient poll failures retried, five in a row mean `lost`; a job of a replaced sandbox is never signalled | P11-12 (`docs/notes/P11/remote-processes.md`), lead at P11 landing |
| D-16.131 | Crash recovery | A live pid is adopted even when its start time cannot be read; sandbox entries dropped silently | **A job is adopted only when its pid exists and its start time matches**; an unreadable start time (no `/proc`) means `lost`; sandbox entries become `lost` records; entries of another live Indus process are never adopted | P11-14 (`docs/notes/P11/process-checkpoint.md`) |
| D-16.132 | Credential files | Only paths inside the home, by containment rule | **Any file the user owns**, minus Indus's secret stores and control files (the `read_file` rule, links resolved first); mounted read-only in Docker, pushed upload-only over SSH | P11-11 (`docs/notes/P11/credential-files.md`) |
| D-16.133 | Process receipts | Retained results only for jobs that asked for a completion message | **A receipt for every exit**, written before any completion event; command and tail force-redacted; readable only by the owner conversation or its compression continuation | P11-13 (`docs/notes/P11/process-receipts.md`), lead at P11 landing |
| D-16.134 | Sudo on remote backends | Sudo handling also runs on remote backends | **This machine's sudo password never leaves it**: the hook is keyed by backend environment; Docker and SSH get the rewrite only when `docker_sudo_password` / `ssh_sudo_password` is stored, read that secret alone and never prompt | Lead at P11 landing (security), `docs/notes/P11/ssh-environment.md` |
| D-16.135 | Checkpoints | Automatic prune off by default | **`checkpoints.auto_prune` defaults to true** (at most once per 24 h); unattended prune never deletes orphan projects; the size cap applies only in the daily prune | P11-18 (`docs/notes/P11/checkpoint-prune.md`), lead at P11 landing |
| D-16.136 | `memory` tool | `target` defaults to the notes store; every failed call counts toward the retry cap, and a later success lifts the stop | **`target` required on every call** (one store per call, batches too); no read action; `replace` refused with a pointer to `patch`; argument mistakes (ambiguous, invalid) do not count toward the 3-failure cap; once capped, the rest of the turn gets the terminal result | P09-02 (`docs/notes/P09/memory-tool.md`) |
| D-16.137 | Memory store files | Time-stamped drift backups; a batch that empties a store is refused | **One `<file>.bak`** (replaced each time); shrinking changes allowed on an over-limit store; a batch may empty a store (each removal is explicit); one process per home assumed for the frozen snapshot | P09-01 (`docs/notes/P09/memory-store.md`) |
| D-16.138 | Frozen memory block | Snapshot re-read from disk when an agent is rebuilt | **Block recovered verbatim from the session's stored prompt** on resume and compaction rebuild (one source of truth); our tags `<saved-notes>` / `<user-profile>` double as cut markers; threat hits shown as `[blocked: …]` in the block only | P09-04 (`docs/notes/P09/memory-prompt-block.md`) |
| D-16.139 | Prompt volatile tier | Skills index leads the volatile tier, then memory and user profile | **Memory notes, user profile, then the skills index** (the P09-04 phase row); the frozen memory blocks change only between sessions | Lead at P09 wave 1 landing (`tests/unit/core/prompt/test_builder.py::test_volatile_tier_order`) |
| D-16.140 | Pending writes | Ordered by time only; a `/learn`-style foreground turn asks inline | `seq` gives a total creation order (fake clocks tie); ids are 10 hex chars; the skill diff renderer takes before/after texts; any turn a person runs (foreground or `/learn`) asks inline on the interactive CLI, the review fork always stages | P09-03 (`docs/notes/P09/pending-writes.md`), lead at Compare |
| D-16.141 | Write provenance | Separate provenance variables for skills and memory; usage records say `created_by` | **One write-origin context** (`learning.pending.bind_origin`: `foreground`, `background_review`, `learn`, plus `attended`) shared by memory and skills; usage records carry `origin: agent\|learn\|null` | P09-12 (`docs/notes/P09/skill-usage.md`), lead at P09 wave 1 landing |
| D-16.142 | Skill format | Broken YAML recovered by splitting lines on `:`; flat `<name>.md` skills accepted; incident lint at 4 refs / 0.5 per 1k | **Broken frontmatter refused on writes and listed as broken on discovery**; only `SKILL.md` folders; incident lint at ≥ 5 refs and ≥ 5 per 1,000 prose chars; names YAML 1.1 reads as non-text (`on/off/yes/no/y/n/true/false/null`, numbers) refused, and such a frontmatter name is a clear error | P09-05 (`docs/notes/P09/skill-format.md`), lead at landing (YAML guard) |
| D-16.143 | Skill discovery | One union-typed disabled setting; plugin skills resolved on a separate path | `skills.disabled` (flat) plus `skills.platform_disabled` (per surface); plugin skills (`plugin:skill`) go through the one catalog; a broken `SKILL.md` keeps its name slot only when nothing else owns the name | P09-06, P09-08 (`docs/notes/P09/skill-resolver.md`, `skill-view-tools.md`) |
| D-16.144 | Skills index delta | The last delta note is read back from the transcript (agents rebuilt per turn) | **Tracker kept in memory on the session's agent** (a resumed session may repeat one note); index block delimited by `<skills_index>`; the note rides the next user message through a prologue note source (`AgentOptions.note_sources`) | P09-07 (`docs/notes/P09/skills-prompt-index.md`), lead at landing |
| D-16.145 | Skill slash commands | Invocation markers parsed back out of stored messages | Our frames are never parsed back; skill and bundle commands are registered when the REPL starts (refresh arrives with P09-24 `/reload-skills`); preload uses the existing `-s/--skills` flag (the phase text says `--skill`) | P09-09, P09-10 (`docs/notes/P09/skill-invoke.md`), lead at landing |
| D-16.146 | Bundled skills | MD5 stream manifest; essential skills seeded even under opt-out | SHA-256 per-file framing; opt-out seeds nothing; restore copies one skill directly; the wheel ships `skills/` as `indus/_bundled_skills` | P09-21 (`docs/notes/P09/bundled-sync.md`), lead at landing (wheel) |
| D-16.147 | Learning tool wiring | Tools register themselves on import | **The session host registers the learning tools** (`learning.host`: memory, session_search, skills_list, skill_view), not `tools.builtin` (tools sit below learning); inline executors only for selected agent-level tools, so an unselected `memory` cannot run and adds no prompt guidance | Lead at P09 wave 1 landing |
| D-16.148 | `session_search` | (research spec only) | discover 10 / 25 max, browse 10 / 25, read window 20 / 40 max, tool output clipped to 1,000 chars, 12,000-char answers with a continuation hint; `cron` and `subagent` sessions and the current lineage hidden unless asked | P09-25 (`docs/notes/P09/session-search.md`) |
| D-16.149 | `skill_manage` | Folder snapshot and rollback after a failed op; delete only alone; fuzzy patch matching; separate archive helper | **All-or-nothing on an in-memory overlay** checked before any write (delete may sit in a batch, applied last); exact substring `patch` (no quote folding for skills); origin from the one write-origin context; writes gated by `skills.write_approval` (staged as one pending write, `indus skills pending/approve/reject`, `/skills pending`); review-fork deletes go through the curator's archive (`manage_tool.ARCHIVER` → `curator.archive.manage_archiver`, one ledger entry, written by the tool) | P09-11 (`docs/notes/P09/skill-manage.md`), lead at P09 wave 2 landing (archive) |
| D-16.150 | Skill ledger | Pre-rollback plus rollback entries; ledger on/off switch | **One rollback entry** carrying the current bytes (the undo of the rollback); actor default `agent` (the CLI binds `user`, the curator `curator`, including its consolidation pass); no on/off switch (`ledger_max_bytes: 0` only stops trimming); trim to three quarters of the cap; the curator's archive/restore/purge/journey edits reach the same ledger through `curator.ledger_port`, which defaults to the profile's `SkillLedger` (`set_ledger` only overrides, for tests) | P09-13 (`docs/notes/P09/skill-ledger.md`), lead at P09 wave 2 landing (port default) |
| D-16.151 | Learning CLI and slash | Curses checklist to toggle skills; confirmations exit 1 without a terminal | No checklist (`skills.disabled` via `indus config`); a needed confirmation without a terminal exits **2** (the P09-24 row); `/memory` alone shows pending; `indus memory edit` edits a temp copy and saves only a changed text; `/reload-skills` has its own registry entry and rebuilds `ctx.extensions.skill_commands` from **one builder** (`cli/slash/skill_cmds.skill_commands`, also used at REPL start); `indus skills` reuses the memory command's pending helpers | P09-24 (`docs/notes/P09/learning-cli.md`), lead at P09 wave 2 landing (one builder) |
| D-16.152 | Review counters | Counters tick in three modules; every model call counts toward skills | Counters worked out once from the finished turn's rows on a per-session `ReviewTrigger` (a `SessionRuntime.after_turn` hook); the skill counter counts **tool-calling iterations**; a turn that called `memory` is not memory-due; the host also resets a counter the moment the agent's own write succeeds (`MemoryTool.on_success`, `manage_tool.SUCCESS_LISTENERS`, foreground only); one-shot (`single_query`) and cron sessions never review | P09-14 (`docs/notes/P09/review-counters.md`), lead at P09 wave 2 landing (listeners) |
| D-16.153 | Review fork | A second agent object; the crossing request finishes before the budget stops; local-server slot probe | The fork is **our loop's turn over a forked runtime** (in-memory store, own session id); the budget stops *before* a request would cross it; the digest tail extends back to an assistant row; idle = no live turn in this process for 60 s; route/effort warnings are log lines; a live turn cancels the fork through the agent's `before_turn` hooks (core seam: `Agent.start_turn` awaits them, a failing hook is logged); `AuxClient.call` takes `tools=` / `tool_choice=` (one implementation for review and curator) | P09-15 (`docs/notes/P09/review-fork.md`), lead at P09 wave 2 landing (`before_turn`) |
| D-16.154 | Review prompts and notices | Notices echo the tool's message, per-op preview limits, emoji glyphs | Our prompt text (golden file); `on` lines are generic per store/skill; one 200-char preview limit in `verbose`; no glyphs; the summary reads `skill_manage`'s per-action `results` rows (a row is counted unless it says it failed or was staged) | P09-16 (`docs/notes/P09/review-prompts-summary.md`), lead at P09 wave 2 landing (results rows) |
| D-16.155 | `/learn`, `/refine` | Author line; product field names | Our standards block (`license: MIT`, `metadata.indus`, our section order); the classic CLI submits `/learn` as a plain turn (the built prompt is the stored user message; `learn_turn` with origin `learn` is the host API); `/refine` reaches the session's runner through `HostPort.refine` → `learning.host.review_runner` and covers memory only when the `memory` tool is on | P09-17 (`docs/notes/P09/learn-refine.md`), lead at P09 wave 2 landing |
| D-16.156 | Curator lifecycle | A failing cron read counts as "nothing referenced"; run claim in a separate lock file; bundled telemetry | A failing reference source **stops archiving for that run**; the claim lives in `.curator_state`; with `prune_builtins` bundled skills age from first sight (archive only); evaluated on interactive CLI start (daemon thread, never blocks or raises) and by the interactive service's maintenance timer (90 s, then hourly; `service.maintenance.learning_timer`, idle = time since the newest stored message) | P09-18 (`docs/notes/P09/curator-lifecycle.md`), lead at P09 wave 2 landing (hosts) |
| D-16.157 | Curator consolidation and backups | A forked full agent; YAML block in the final answer; cron jobs file in the snapshot | A **small tool loop on the auxiliary client** (`curator` task) with a three-tool registry (the real `skill_manage`, origin `background_review`, ledger actor `curator`); classification by the `absorbed_into` declaration, then the tool-call audit; snapshots leave out archive, ledger, hub state, backups and a skill's own `.git`/caches/virtualenvs (carried over on rollback); the cron file is P14's to snapshot | P09-19 (`docs/notes/P09/curator-consolidate.md`) |
| D-16.158 | Curator CLI and journey | `purge` with TTL 0 exits 1; pins recorded for any skill; a `usage` verb; map edges and clusters | `purge` with `archive_ttl_days: 0` says purge is off and exits 0; `pin`/`unpin` refused for unmanaged skills (with an `adopt` hint); no `usage` verb (`indus skills list`); `adopt` one name at a time; `/curator` offers status/run/pause/resume only; `indus learning` is a second name for `indus journey`; the journey has nodes only (no edges/clusters) and its skill edits are ledgered as `user` | P09-20, P09-23 (`docs/notes/P09/curator-cli.md`, `journey.md`) |
| D-16.159 | Starter skill seeding | Seeded by the installer and on update | Also on the **first chat or `-z` start of a profile with no `.bundled_manifest`** (`bundled_sync.seed_first_run`, honours `.no-bundled-skills`, never raises; a failed source leaves no manifest so the next start retries); after that only `indus skills sync` refreshes | Lead at P09 wave 2 landing (wave 1 open seam) |
| D-16.160 | `execute_code` approval (E9) | An interactive CLI never asks before running a script | The script is approved **as a whole, before anything is written or started** (`tools/builtin/code/gate.py`): an isolated backend (container, no host mount) → allowed; auto-approve / `approvals.mode: off` → allowed; **interactive: asked once per session** (once / session / deny — never "always", a script is not an allowlist entry; the smart judge first in `smart` mode); **unattended (no broker, cron, one-shot `-z`, unattended): the matching `approvals.*_mode` policy, deny by default**, so `indus -z` refuses `execute_code` unless e.g. `approvals.single_query_mode: approve`. A subagent asks through the parent's surface (labelled), once per child session | P10-08 (`docs/notes/P10/code-runner-rpc.md`), lead at P10 G+H landing |
| D-16.161 | `execute_code` runner and module | Output cut before masking; spill readable by others; refusals silently dropped; an empty tool intersection offers every tool | Output **masked before it is cut and spilled** (the 0600 spill in a private dir never holds a secret the inline text hides); a refused in-script call raises `ToolCallRefused`; forbidden `terminal` arguments refused, not dropped; RPC server thread-based and loop-independent; an **empty intersection offers only the helpers**; the description follows `code_execution.mode`/`kernel` | P10-08, P10-09 (`code-runner-rpc.md`, `code-schema-module.md`) |
| D-16.162 | Session kernels | Child kernels reaped when idle; no lost-state report after a timeout; the runner spills long cells | `code_execution.kernel: auto\|off` (auto: a session kernel on the local backend, a remote kernel where one starts, a script otherwise); **pinned child kernels are exempt from the idle sweep and the LRU cap** (R02.32) and end at the child's teardown; the next call after a timeout/kill reports `state_lost`; the host spills long cells; replies on their own pipe; hosts dispose a session's kernels on close (`/new`, `/clear`, switch) and close both pools at exit; only an interactive host runs the idle reaper (`service/code_host.py`) | P10-10 (`code-kernel.md`), lead at landing |

| D-16.229 | Cron prompt assembly | One scan of the assembled prompt | Our hint wording; **two-tier scan**: what the job's author wrote with the strict `cron` scope, data loaded at run time with `context` after invisible characters are stripped; upstream and notepad data framed as data; skill bundles in a job's skills are not expanded (open) | P14-10 (`cron-prompt.md`) |
