| D-16.231 | Cron delivery | Unresolvable platforms skipped and logged; media paths read from the reply | Local delivery only before P15; an unknown platform is reported as `unknown_platform`, never routed to local; **attachments only as `Path` objects inside the job's output folder** (never from reply or MCP text); our wrapper wording; the delivery status is stored on the ledger attempt | P14-13 (`cron-delivery.md`) |
| D-16.232 | Cron bookkeeping | Quota hold anchored on one provider's error type; the record mutated under the lock | The P03 classifier (`connection` with no HTTP status = unreachable; `rate_limit`/`billing` with `reset_at`/`retry_after` and zero answered calls = quota hold); a change set applied in **one locked store write** after delivery, with the store's planner (D-16.221); the review nudge and the hold line go only on a notice that is sent | P14-14 (`cron-bookkeeping.md`), lead at landing |
| D-16.233 | Cron incidents and the after-run order | Separate failure-type keywords | An `occurrences` counter; the incident table in `executions.db`; after a run: a failure first asks the incident whether to alert (a repeat inside the reminder window — and its nudge — is held) → delivery → bookkeeping (locked) → a success resolves the job's open incidents | P14-15 (`cron-incidents.md`), lead at landing |
| D-16.234 | Cron monitor mode | A body over 256 KiB truncated; plain URL opener | Over 256 KiB is an error; the fetch goes through `safe_client` (every redirect hop checked); the monitor script runs through the cron script runner; the new hash is stored when the source changed, before the run | P14-16 (`cron-monitor.md`), lead at landing |
| D-16.235 | `cronjob_manage` | Inference fields dropped; a restart guard with profile-flag exceptions | Inference pins (`model`, `provider`, `base_url`, `reasoning_effort`) **refused**; any `indus … gateway restart` or `… gateway stop` refused; prompts scanned with the `cron` scope; deferred by default; `run` returns a handle and the result arrives through a per-session sink that **follows a rotating compaction to the new session id** | P14-18 (`cron-tool.md`), lead at landing |
| D-16.236 | `indus cron` and `/cron` | Bare `cron` lists; notepad `delete` | Bare `indus cron` prints the group help; notepad verb `unset`; missing arguments exit 2; `remove` without a terminal needs `-y`; `tick` prints one `<job> <status>` line per run; one backend per process (`ports.BACKEND_FACTORY` = `service.cron_host.CronHost`, set when the host module is imported) | P14-19 (`cron-cli.md`), lead at landing |
| D-16.237 | Cron notepad | Keys free-form | Keys may not hold control characters (the prompt block cannot be forged by a key); values indented under their key | P14-17 (`cron-notepad.md`) |
| D-16.238 | Gateway send results and retry | Plain-text fallback on several failure readings; timeouts retried like connection errors | Every platform call answers with a classified `SendResult` (seven kinds, first rule wins, nothing matched = `unknown`); **read/write timeouts are never retried** (the platform may have the message); connect-level failures and rate limits retried at most twice with clamped jitter; a server wait above the platform's inline cap is handed to the delivery ledger, no "delivery failed" notice into the penalty; plain-text resend only for `bad_format` / `unknown` | P15-04 (`send-retry.md`) |
| D-16.239 | Adapter contract | Per-adapter behaviour for unsupported operations and private notices; reference callback prefixes | One template base: idempotent connect/disconnect, credential-scrubbed errors, optional operations refused before the wire; **private notices and media sends refuse unless the adapter implements them** (a plain send into a group would show a notice to everyone); callback data `apv:` / `clr:` (≤ 64 bytes); a `markup` capability picks the P15-15 formatter; the outbound `interim` / `final` marks are taken off before any hook (never on the wire) and an interim edit dropped by the busy send slot is `ok` with `skipped` (lead at integration) | P15-05 (`adapter-base.md`); lead at landing |
| D-16.240 | Gateway config and enablement | Gateway-only defaults; `enabled: true` starts without credentials and fails later; env home channel beats config | One loader and defaults table for every `gateway.*`, `platforms.<name>.*` and gateway `display.*` row (G1–G4 rows included: `trust_env`, session-key, unknown-DM, bot-loop, concurrency, media, streaming, tool progress, heartbeat, ephemeral TTL); `enabled: true` without credentials does not start and is listed as a problem; **config home channel beats env**; keys written straight under `platforms.<name>` are merged into `extra` (explicit `extra` wins); `message_timestamps` under `gateway`; busy-text rows `display.busy_ack` / `busy_text_quiet_s` / `busy_text_cap_s` | P15-02 (`gateway-config.md`); lead at landing |
| D-16.241 | Inbound events | A bare `/` treated as a command; sent-message cache persisted | A bare `/` (or a path such as `/etc/hosts`) is plain text; the sent-message index lives in memory only (bounded per chat and overall); replied-to quotes cut to 4,096 UTF-8 bytes | P15-03 (`inbound-events.md`) |
| D-16.242 | Platform registry and token locks | Fixed adapter table; per-token lock files; plugin platforms through their own hook | Registry entries carry `group_users_env`, `group_chats_env`, `trusted_inbound` (`api_server`, `webhook`; **a plugin can never mark a built-in trusted**); plugin platforms arrive through P13's provider kind **`platform`** (`PlatformRegistry.load_plugin_entries(host.providers)`); token locks via one cross-process guard lock plus atomic replace; `INDUS_GATEWAY_LOCK_DIR` honoured only when absolute | P15-06 (`registry-locks.md`); lead at landing |
| D-16.243 | Session keys and the routing index | Thread chats keyed like DMs in places; a deleted session can stay routed | Thread chat type counts as a group; per-user group keys on by default, shared threads (`gateway.group_sessions_per_user` / `thread_sessions_per_user`); **deleting a session drops every gateway route pointing at it and clears it as a previous session** (`state/deletion.py`), so the chat's next message starts a fresh one | P15-07 (`session-keys.md`); lead at landing |
| D-16.244 | Authorization and unknown senders | Unknown DMs get a pairing code unless the platform allowlist env is set | **Any configured allowlist (env, `platforms.<name>.extra.allow_from` / `group_allow_from` / `group_allowed_chats`) flips unknown DMs to `ignore`** unless `unauthorized_dm_behavior` says otherwise; `gateway.unauthorized_dm_behavior` (`pair` default, `ignore`, `decline` once per sender per 24 h); codes never go where a DM is not private (R06.24); the chain fails closed | P15-08 (`authz.md`) |
| D-16.245 | Bot-loop guard and slash tiers | Edits/repeats of one message counted again; one admin list for DM and group | A repeated message id counts once; DM and group slash-tier lists are independent (`allow_admin_from` / `group_allow_admin_from`, `user_allowed_commands` / `group_user_allowed_commands`), floor `help` + `whoami`; defaults on, 20 bot messages per 300 s, 600 s cooldown (`gateway.bot_loop_guard`) | P15-10 (`bot-loop-tiers.md`) |
| D-16.246 | Pairing store and the layer rule | Pairing files owned by the gateway, read by the CLI directly | The pairing store lives in **`indus.state.pairing`**, below both the gateway and the CLI; `indus pairing` and the gateway share it; **no import-linter exemptions** — what two top-layer siblings share lives in `state/` or `service/` (P15-28's `indus gateway` commands follow the same rule) | lead at landing (P15-09, `pairing.md`) |
| D-16.247 | Outbound finals | Redaction optional per platform; chunks always marked | Chat finals are **always** redacted before formatting and chunking; silence tokens `[SILENT]`, `SILENT`, `NO_REPLY`, `NO REPLY` (whole reply only, never on a failed turn; a person expecting a reply gets a short notice); limits below 32 units are cut bare (no markers or fence repair); Slack tables ≤ 20,000 characters, one native table per message | P15-14 / P15-17 (`chunker.md`, `sender-filters.md`) |
| D-16.248 | Streaming and progress | Approval placeholder on every transport; partial words shown | Interim frames show text only up to the last whitespace (a half-streamed secret is never shown); the approval placeholder only on native streams (P2); tool progress edits throttled to 1.5 s; heartbeat every `display.heartbeat_seconds` (180) | P15-16 (`stream-consumer.md`) |
| D-16.249 | Media path trust by recency | Files created in the last 600 s trusted by default | **`gateway.media_trust_recent` is off by default**: Linux keeps no birth time, so "recent" is the modification time and any process can bump it with `touch`; trusted places are the profile's gateway caches and `gateway.media_allow_dirs` (strict mode: the allow-list only); turning it on keeps the 600 s window and the deny-list | lead decision (P15-18, `media-policy.md`) |
| D-16.250 | Intake replies | Each reply path formats and sends on its own | Every intake reply (turn finals, notices, busy acks, command answers) goes through **one reply port** (`gateway.outbound.replies.PlatformReplies` over the P15-17 `OutboundSender`): `notice` with nothing to reply to counts as unsolicited (dead targets skipped), `ack` is an ephemeral notice (`display.ephemeral_system_ttl`, 0 = kept) | lead at landing (P15-11 / P15-17) |
| D-16.251 | Admission and mention gating | Mention gating before authorization; a bare Telegram `/start` answered | Mention gating runs **after** authorization (commands and replies to the bot pass); a bare Telegram `/start` ping is not answered | P15-11 (`admission.md`) |
| D-16.252 | Busy input | A refused redirect drops the text | In `interrupt` mode a message whose redirect is refused is queued (media still interrupts) | P15-12 (`busy-guards.md`) |
| D-16.253 | Chat slash commands | Cross-origin session listing for everyone; `/approve all` = session-wide | Cross-origin `/sessions all` and `/resume` need slash tiers on and an admin; `/whoami` answers only while idle; `/approve all` approves each pending request once; destructive commands ask only while `approvals.destructive_slash_confirm` is on (default off) | P15-13 (`chat-commands.md`) |
| D-16.260 | Sandbox records (P21-01) | Snapshot ids kept per backend in one JSON file written without a lock; legacy un-prefixed keys migrated | One file per kind under `<home>/sandboxes/records/`, keys always `<profile id>:<task key>` (R02.8); **every read and write under an exclusive lock** (10 s, then `backend.sandbox_records_busy` — never an unlocked write), atomic 0600 write in a 0700 dir; an unreadable file is moved aside (`.corrupt-<stamp>`); no legacy keys (D-16.3). A busy store fails the sandbox open (retry later) rather than silently starting from the base image | P21-01 (`sandbox-records.md`) |
| D-16.261 | Vendor call deadlines (R02.30) | SDK timeouts trusted; retries only around some Vercel calls | **Every vendor call** runs on its own SDK pool under our deadline (create 300 s, snapshot 60 s, stop/terminate/delete 15 s, get 30 s, file 30 s, bulk 120 s, exec = command timeout + 10 s); a missed deadline abandons the call, fires one vendor cancel and raises `backend.deadline`; up to 3 tries with jittered backoff (0.5 s ×2, cap 4 s, jitter 0.5–1.0) **only for idempotent calls** (never exec, never a missed deadline); every command also runs under `timeout -k 2 <N>` inside the sandbox | P21-02 (`sdk-call.md`) |
| D-16.262 | Stdin for SDK sandboxes (P21-03) | Payload passed per backend, unverified | Pipe-less sandboxes (Daytona, Vercel) get stdin as an uploaded file **checked by byte count** and opened with `exec 0<file \|\| exit 1; rm -f file` before user code runs (an incomplete upload is removed); Modal streams 64 KiB chunks then EOF; text that cannot be encoded runs the command with empty stdin and reports `stdin_error`; an empty string stages nothing | P21-03 (`stdin-payload.md`) |
| D-16.263 | Singularity/Apptainer (P21-04) | A failed SIF build falls back to running the `docker://` URL | A failed build is an error (`backend.singularity.build`, stderr preview ≤ 500 chars), no fallback; cgroup limits used when root or cgroup v2 (one retry without limits on a cgroup failure); a persistent sandbox (overlay) serves the whole profile like Docker | P21-04 (`singularity.md`) |
| D-16.264 | Vendor credentials and forwarded variables (P21-11) | Token names stripped per call site; forwarded values in some command lines | Modal (token pair, OAuth names), Daytona (`DAYTONA_API_KEY`, `DAYTONA_JWT_TOKEN`) and Vercel names are in the child-env floor and registered at CLI start (R02.22); forwarded variables reach an SDK sandbox **only through the SDK's env parameter** (a value-less name is unset in the script) and never enter the shell snapshot (R02.1, R02.7) | P21-11 (`vendor-env.md`); wiring at landing |
| D-16.265 | SDK extras (P21-15) | `vercel` pinned as the umbrella package; an `all` extra | Extras `modal = modal==1.6.1`, `daytona = daytona==0.220.0`, **`vercel = vercel-sandbox==0.7.0`** (the package with the sandbox API); no `all` extra; the fakes' method lists come from the pinned SDKs (`sdk_surfaces.json`) | P21-15 (`fake-sdks.md`, `sdk-versions.md`) |
| D-16.266 | Vercel Sandbox SDK shape (P21-08/09) | Older SDK calls with a runtime argument | Against vercel-sandbox 0.7.0: `create_sandbox` / `create_process` / `fs.write_bytes`; the configured runtime (`node24`, `node22`, `python3.13`) is sent in the **image** slot (to verify in the live lane); the OIDC-only path relies on `VERCEL_OIDC_TOKEN` being in the process env; running-wait polls **every 500 ms** for ≤ 30 s (reference 250 ms; lead ruling) | P21-08 (`vercel-lifecycle.md`) |
| D-16.267 | Interrupt and timeout in SDK sandboxes (P21-06/09) | Interrupt stops or recreates the whole sandbox | Interrupt and timeout **signal the command's process group** through a second exec (pid file per command; TERM, then KILL), Vercel also calls the vendor's process kill; the in-sandbox guard is `ceil(timeout) + 2` s and the vendor-side exec bound 2 s later; results stay rc 130 + `interrupted` / rc 124 + `timed_out` | P21-06 (`modal-exec.md`), P21-09 |
| D-16.268 | SDK sandbox images and lifecycles (P21-05/07) | One image key; Daytona looked up by labels; a leftover reused | Rows `terminal.modal_image`, `terminal.daytona_image` (empty = `terminal.docker_image`), `terminal.vercel_runtime` (enum); `modal_mode` accepts `auto`/`direct` only (`managed` refused, D-16.16); Daytona sandboxes are found by name `indus-<task key>` only, and a non-persistent profile **deletes** a same-name leftover before creating | P21-05 (`modal-lifecycle.md`), P21-07 (`daytona.md`) |
| D-16.269 | Sync-back (P21-10) | Stale temp entries removed by age alone | Hash-diff apply under a profile lock, credential files upload-only, escaping/symlink members refused, archive cap `terminal.sync_back_max_bytes` (2 GiB); stale `cache/sync-back` temp entries removed only when **the owner process is dead and the entry is older than 30 min** (lead ruling) | P21-10 (`sync-back.md`) |
| D-16.270 | SDK backend registration (P21-12 wiring, lead) | Backends created by name with SDK imports in the probe path | Registry rows `singularity`, `modal`, `daytona`, `vercel_sandbox` (seven built-ins); the SDK probes read only package metadata (a missing extra is named) and then the settings and credentials, never importing an SDK or touching the network; SDK rows have a **195 s cleanup bound** (bulk sync-back 120 + snapshot 60 + stop 15) instead of 15 s; a persistent SDK sandbox serves the whole profile (task key `shared`); the records store runs off the event loop | Landing (LEAD-NOTES, P21 B1+B2) |
| D-16.271 | Batch resume (P22-05/06/08) | Completed indices skipped even without `--resume`; content scan separate | Checkpoint indices are honoured **only for the same dataset hash**; `--resume` adds the content scan and records the prompts it finds as completed; batch numbers restart at 0 on each run (files appended); `--batch-size` default 10 | P22-08 (`batch-orchestration.md`), P22-09 |
| D-16.272 | Batch workers (P22-07/08) | Workers re-read the environment; partial runs mixed with failures | Workers get the parent's env view through the pool pipe (never argv or disk) and rebuild the scope from it; runs that stop early with a usable trajectory (`max_iterations`, `budget_exhausted`, `invalid_tool_strikes`, `guardrail_halt`) are written with `partial: true`; provider failures and interruptions write nothing and are retried next run; a missing `docker` CLI under the Docker backend is a failure | P22-07 (`worker.md`), P22-08 |
| D-16.273 | Image rows before P21-14 (P22-07) | Per-task container images through the reference's override table | Rows naming an image fail with **no model call** (the parent warns up front) until P21-14 plugs a registrar into `worker.ImagePrep.register` — **superseded by D-16.322** (P21-14 landed: image rows run in their own sandbox) | P22-07 (`worker.md`) |
| D-16.274 | Trajectory converter and routing (P22-03/09) | The reference's own system text; routing only for one provider | System turn in our own words; a tool response whose id matches no call is `unknown` (position only when ids are missing); a leading `<think>` counts as reasoning when the native field is empty; provider routing sent in `extra_body.provider` whenever any routing flag is given | P22-03 (`trajectory-converter.md`), P22-09 |
| D-16.275 | Research layer (P22, lead) | — | `indus.research` sits **one layer below the user surfaces** (above `indus.service`) in the import contract, so the CLI verbs and the interactive saver import it directly | Landing (LEAD-NOTES, P22 R1) |
| D-16.276 | Delivery ledger (P15-19) | Final replies written to a ledger and resent after a crash | Table `delivery_ledger` (id = sha256 of session key, `<session id>:<turn id>` and content); states pending → attempting → delivered / failed → abandoned; owner = pid **plus process start time** (a reused pid is dead); **only turn finals** are ledgered (command answers, notices, acks, cron output and `indus send` are not; cron keeps P14's attempt ledger); the stored text is the **masked** text; a partly sent split reply stores only its undelivered pieces and a retry sends only those; three labels of our own (duplicate, reconnected, flood) and a refused send is retried **without** a label; no "delivery failed" notice while the ledger will still retry; in-process retries after 30 s and 120 s, the last attempt kept for the next boot; the active-turn marker is cleared only after the final passed the sender (≤ 300 s wait, R06.5); `gateway.delivery_ledger` (default true) | P15-19 (`delivery-ledger.md`) |
| D-16.277 | Platform deliverers and `indus send` (P15-20) | `send` reads its body from a file; deliverers per platform | Deliverers register into P14's registry when a platform connects (and `all` while any is attached) and go when it goes down; `indus send --file` **attaches** a file through the P15-18 media policy (the body is the argument or stdin; `MEDIA:` tags are sent as text); mirror notes ride the conversation's next user message (note channel), never a fake assistant row; the channel directory comes from the adapter's `list_channels()` or the routes index only (no alias file), refreshed every 5 min and saved in the home; `indus send` builds and connects its own adapters (no running gateway) and, when TARGET names no built-in platform, loads the profile's plugins to find a plugin platform; no model tool wraps the router (R02.26) | P15-20 (`platform-deliverers.md`), lead at landing |
| D-16.278 | Gateway daemon process (P15-24) | Separate lifecycle sentinel; one exit code for most failures | The runtime status file `gateway_state.json` **doubles as the lifecycle marker** (left live by a dead owner = unclean death); `degraded` = a parked fatal platform or the loop-watchdog exit (`exit_reason` tells them apart); exit 78 only when nothing connected, at least one connect-time conflict and nothing retrying (a missing library or unbuildable adapter parks the platform but never forces 78); a failed required part or an unexpected startup error exits 75; liveness = pid + start time + pid namespace; supervisors are recognised only by `INDUS_GATEWAY_SUPERVISED`; `_INDUS_GATEWAY` reaches tool subprocesses through the scope env and child_env's force entry (never the process env); the concurrency cap is read at start; adapter factories take `AdapterConfig(name, settings, scope, clock, proxy)` (in `gateway.config`, shared with `indus send`) | P15-24 (`daemon-process.md`) |
| D-16.279 | Daemon parts (P15-24 integration, lead) | One startup function wiring every feature | Features plug in as `DaemonPart`s listed once in `PART_FACTORIES` (`"module:attr"`), started by stage — CONTROL (control socket, **required**), RECOVERY (ledger sweep, inside the startup-restore window, before boot recovery), BACKGROUND (deliverers + channel directory; cron, chat prompts and media intake) — and stopped in reverse (CONTROL last, so the socket answers during the drain); `on_platform_up` / `on_platform_down` / `on_ready` listeners; the reconnect watcher runs with the control part (it queues failures from the first connect) | Landing (LEAD-NOTES, P15 waves 2–3) |
| D-16.280 | Runtime records (P15-24/28, lead ruling) | Status, pid and stop records in the gateway package | **One** module `indus.state.gateway_runtime` for the daemon, the CLI, cron and the updater: pid record, status file + `runtime_view` (running / stale after 120 s or a gone process / stopped / missing), the planned-stop record (`pid`, `start_time`, `reason`, `by`; a record for another process means nothing), `alive(pid, start_time)`, exit codes; the status file is `gateway_state.json` (already excluded by `indus import`) | Landing (LEAD-NOTES, P15 waves 2–3) |
| D-16.281 | Drain, restart, recovery (P15-25/26) | Interrupted turns resumed once even after a crash | A crash-interrupted turn is **paused**, not resumed (R06.19: `/reset` or an operator `resolve_unknown` with the generation ends it); a stop- or restart-interrupted turn is resumed once with a heads-up; an owed reply the transcript holds goes out once through the ledger with the recovered label **unless the ledger already holds a row for that turn**; drain reasons: external marker = maintenance, control `drain` = operator (maintenance notice), `pause-for-update` = update (restarting notice); the external marker never clears a drain it did not start | P15-25 (`daemon-lifecycle.md`), P15-26 (`boot-recovery.md`), lead at landing |
| D-16.282 | Control socket verbs (P15-27, lead) | Verbs answered by the daemon's internals | `identify` = the daemon's identity (pid, start time, pid namespace, epoch, supervisor, code version), `status` = its status snapshot; `drain {on}` and `pause-for-update {resume}` act on the lifecycle and answer `{draining, reason, stopping, active_turns}`; `reload-plugins` rereads the gateway hook folders only and says `plugins_reloaded: false` (plugin code loads once per process; a restart picks up new plugins); handlers run on the daemon's loop | P15-27 (`control-socket.md`), lead at landing |
| D-16.283 | Service units and `indus gateway` (P15-28) | Shared helpers in the gateway package; unconditional kill on stop | Unit, plist, socket-client and service-manager code in `indus.service` (layer rule D-16.246); `Restart=on-failure` + `RestartPreventExitStatus=78`; the launchd agent runs a `/bin/sh` wrapper that maps 78 → 0; hashed unit names for non-default homes; `TimeoutStopSec = max(60, max(restart_drain, cron_drain + 10) + 30)`; `install --system` only prints a unit; `stop` records the planned stop before signalling and kills only with `--force`; `stop` / `restart` **refuse inside the gateway's own process tree** (`_INDUS_GATEWAY`); `run` is registered by string (`indus.gateway.daemon.command:run_command`) | P15-28 (`service-units.md`), lead at landing |
| D-16.284 | Reconnect and gateway hooks (P15-29) | — | Backoff 30, 60, 120, 240, then 300 s forever for retryable failures; `needs_attention` after `agent.reconnect_attention_after` (1,800 s) is a signal only; a platform lost while running is retried at once, one that failed at startup after the first backoff; `/platform pause/resume` is manual; gateway hooks are folders under `<home>/hooks/` (sync handlers on the bounded pool, 10 s); `gateway:startup` fires once the daemon is ready | P15-29 (`reconnect-hooks.md`), lead at landing |
| D-16.285 | Cron in the daemon (P15-21) | Ticker ownership by asking the gateway | Ticker ownership is a **lease file** `<home>/cron/ticker_owner.json` (0600, refreshed each pass, stale after 180 s or a dead/reused pid); a deferred `indus cron tick` exits 0; the daemon's stop drains running jobs for `agent.cron_drain_timeout` (30 s, new row); the narration-of-silence filter settles as silent | P15-21 (`cron-host.md`), lead at landing |
| D-16.286 | Approvals and clarify over chat (P15-22) | Anyone in the chat may answer | Answers only from the person whose message started the turn, or an admin of the chat's tier list (only when an admin list is configured); **bots never answer**; the same rule in the prompts' front door and in `/approve` / `/deny` themselves (a command handed straight to the handler cannot skip it); the gateway denies on its own only when the request has no deadline (else the broker's own timeout, R02.5); shared approval wording in `service/approval_copy.py` | P15-22 (`chat-prompts.md`), lead at landing |
| D-16.287 | Plugins in cron runs (D-16.230 follow-up, P15-21) | — | **No `PluginUpkeep` for cron runs**, in the daemon as in `indus cron tick`: a job behaves the same whichever process fires it (no plugin tools, sections or hooks); `CronAgentRunner` is the one place to add it later | P15-21 (`cron-host.md`) |
| D-16.288 | Media intake (P15-23) | — | Caches under the profile home per kind (0700); `gateway.stt_enabled` (default true); the default image describer is P10's vision tool with the service's side model and the "native" image mode is a note pointing at `vision_analyze` (submit is text-only until a P04 seam); albums gathered for 0.8 s of quiet, at most 3 s; adapters register download functions per platform (none: a "could not be downloaded" note) | P15-23 (`intake.md`) |
| D-16.289 | API server (P15-30) | One shared token, `/health` open | Config `gateway.api_server` (off by default; 127.0.0.1:8642); a bearer key is required on **every** route, `/health` included, compared in constant time and never logged; no key = the server refuses to start; keys live in the secret store and are managed with `indus gateway api-key`; a request without `X-Indus-Session-Id` makes a new session whose first message folds in the request's system and earlier messages; data-URL images cached under `<home>/cache/api_server/images`; a failure keeps the OpenAI `finish_reason` plus an `indus` error object | P15-30 (`api-server-openai.md`) |
| D-16.290 | Runs, sessions and jobs API (P15-31) | — | Runs, idempotency keys and stored responses are in memory (a restart forgets them); background completions are kept in `<home>/gateway/api_completions.jsonl`, **not** the delivery ledger (they are not chat deliveries: no platform, no chat; lead at landing); `/api/cron/fire` needs the API key; cron runs started over the API are cancelled and awaited when the server closes | P15-31 (`api-server-runs.md`), lead at landing |

| D-16.292 | HTTP surfaces in the daemon (P15-30..32 integration, lead) | — | `HttpPart` (BACKGROUND, present only when a surface is enabled) starts the surfaces after recovery and closes them before their session services; one service per surface (`build_surface_service`): MCP servers for the API server only, plugins never (one plugin host per profile), the webhook surface neither; a surface that cannot start is a `surfaces.<name>.problem` row of `gateway_state.json` and **never fails the daemon** (platforms and cron keep running), the API server's counters are rows of the same map | Landing (LEAD-NOTES, P15 waves 2–3) |
| D-16.320 | Flapping cloud probes (P21-12, R02.13, R02.29) | One probe answer per call; a probe at boot caches "unavailable" | The `singularity`, `modal`, `daytona`, `vercel_sandbox` probes sit behind a per-row guard: a failure within **120 s** of a success serves last-good until the failure streak is **30 s** old (first to latest failure), then it is honoured and last-good dropped; a probe with no scope (or that loses it) answers "not checked yet", records nothing and is run for real on the first scoped call; kept separate from the tool registry's 60 s grace and Docker's own availability cache | P21-12 (`remote-probes.md`) |
| D-16.321 | Doctor and setup for cloud backends (P21-13) | Setup installs the SDK extra; Vercel defaults read from the nearest link file | Setup never installs an extra (it names the one to install), asks plainly instead of reading `.vercel/project.json`, writes credentials only to the secret store (never `.env`, never shown), never stores the short-lived Vercel OIDC token, and offers Singularity on every OS; `--non-interactive --terminal-backend NAME` takes credentials from the environment and leaves `terminal.backend` unchanged while a required name is missing (exit 1); doctor prints credential **names only**, no SDK import, no network; the selected backend's summary row is `info` (its detail rows carry the error) | P21-13 (`doctor-setup.md`) |
| D-16.322 | Per-task overrides (P21-14) | Override table keyed by a routed task id; cwd-only overrides share the default sandbox | `backends.task_overrides.TASK_OVERRIDES` keyed by (profile home, task id), sessions bound to a task resolve to it; **every** override, cwd-only included, gets its own `task:<id>` environment; image mapped per backend (Docker config overlay, Singularity `docker://<image>`, Modal/Daytona call override; Vercel, local and SSH ignore it with one warning); explicit call overrides win; **a pinned Docker image (`terminal.docker_image_pinned`) is never replaced by a task's image** (ignored with one warning; integrator ruling); batch rows and `indus research swe` both go through it and clear it when the task's environment is released; supersedes D-16.273 | P21-14 (`task-overrides.md`), landing |

| D-16.324 | Interactive trajectory saver (P22-10) | Raw text written; local timestamps | Every turn value passes the P05 redactor in **force** mode before it is written; UTC timestamps from the scope clock; files in the session's working directory; the listener runs synchronously in the end-of-turn bookkeeping (a one-shot run cannot lose its sample) and a failed write is a log warning; whole-file lock (`flock` / one-byte `msvcrt` range); wired for the CLI only (TUI bridge and gateway not yet) | P22-10 (`saver.md`) |

| D-16.326 | Compression algorithm (P22-12) | Empty summaries accepted; region may start on a tool turn | Our prompt, marker `[SUMMARY OF EARLIER TURNS]:`, fallback and elision texts; a blank reply is a failure (retried); a start that cannot move off a `tool` turn aborts; inner repeats of the marker removed; summariser through the provider layer only; property tests are seeded random generators (no Hypothesis dependency) | P22-12 (`compressor.md`) |

| D-16.330 | Telegram webhook mode (P15-33, lead ruling) | Webhook listener on all interfaces (IPv6 included) | Webhook mode only with `TELEGRAM_WEBHOOK_SECRET` (secret store, then env; none = fatal connect error); the `X-Telegram-Bot-Api-Secret-Token` header is compared in constant time **before** the body is read (401 otherwise; bodies ≤1 MiB); the listener binds **127.0.0.1** unless `platforms.telegram.webhook_host` is set explicitly (a public bind is an opt-in, as for the P16-01 listener kit and the API server, D-16.289); port 8443 by default; our own small app, not the P15-32 receiver | P15-33 (`telegram.md`), integrator |
| D-16.331 | Telegram client (P15-33) | `python-telegram-bot` (LGPL-3.0) | Our own async Bot API client over httpx (`adapters/telegram/bot_api.py`), **no `telegram` extra**; the token is put into a copy of the request path by our transport only (placeholder `/bot****/` in every log line, repr and error), transport errors re-raised `from None` and scrubbed; the drift check compares our client with the documented Bot API method names (`TELEGRAM_DOCUMENTED_METHODS`, kept by hand) | P15-33 (`telegram.md`) |
| D-16.332 | Telegram behaviour (P15-33) | Short floods and network errors retried inside the adapter; a refused press gets a toast; direct-IP fallback, DoH and a polling-stall watchdog | A cold boot drops the server-side backlog unless `drop_pending_on_cold_boot: false` (a reconnect keeps it); update receipts ≤4,096 for 24 h saved per bot under `<home>/gateway/`; inbound edits ignored; reactions and the topic-root reminder opt-in; command menu cut at a 3,500-character text budget (forum chats get a chat-scoped menu); every button press is answered at once with an empty answer and authorized by the pipeline (no adapter allowlist copy); retries through the shared P15-04 policy; no DoH, direct-IP fallback or polling-stall watchdog (the reconnect watcher owns recovery) | P15-33 (`telegram.md`) |

Live: `tests/e2e/gateway/live_demo3.py`, `network` marker, `INDUS_LIVE=1` and `INDUS_HOME`, outside normal collection; credentials only through the profile's settings, never printed | integrator (P15-36, `demo3.md`) |
| D-16.350 | Inbound HTTP listener kit (P16-01) | One listener per adapter; bind address taken as configured | Shared kit `gateway/adapters/_http.py`: loopback by default, a non-loopback host also needs `webhook_public: true`; body cap checked on the declared length and while streaming (ceiling 16 MiB whatever the config says); `/health`, 404/405/503 answered by the kit before any handler; listener keys are free `platforms.<name>.webhook_*` keys (no schema rows); constant-time signature helpers; stop within the 5 s disconnect budget | P16-01 (`http-kit.md`) |
| D-16.351 | Outbound HTTP failure classes (P16-01, R06.4) | Failures classified from error text | Classified by exception class and status: connect failures retryable; read/write timeouts and dropped responses are "maybe delivered" and never retried; 502/504 maybe delivered; 503 transient (rate limit when Retry-After is sent); 500 unknown; 401 never read as "forbidden", so a wrong token cannot mark a chat dead | P16-01 (`http-kit.md`) |

| D-16.353 | Nostr `npub` decoding (P16-01) | Mixed case lower-cased before decoding | Mixed-case bech32 refused, per BIP-173 | P16-01 (`http-kit.md`) |
| D-16.354 | Signal sender ids (P16-01) | Number and UUID taken as given | Numbers (E.164) and UUIDs validated; a bad number falls back to the UUID | P16-01 (`http-kit.md`) |
| D-16.360 | Runtime ledger (P17-01, R03.21, R03.22) | One admission table with a separate stored-result envelope, worker executions and receipts; a session row must exist | Our tables `runtime_owner`, `runtime_admissions`, `runtime_generations` and retirement markers (D-16.2); no session-row requirement; `claim_head` answers a kind (`claimed`/`idle`/`running`/`blocked`/`moved`); the final text lives on the row; at most **32** queued per session; refusals are bounded `runtime.<reason>` codes in `ERROR_CODES` (P17-25 maps them onto the protocol table); retirement markers are never pruned; a deleted session's admissions are retired inside `deletion.delete_rows_in` | P17-01 (`runtime-ledger.md`) |
| D-16.361 | Unknown turns (P17-01/02, R06.19) | An operator's resolution settles the lost turn `interrupted` | `discard_unknown(id, expected_generation)` settles outcome **`discarded`**; a stale generation is refused `stale_generation`; the FIFO stays paused until then and the blocked callback runs once per unknown admission | P17-02 (`admissions.md`) |
| D-16.362 | Lease wait timeout (P17-02/03) | "Session busy, send again" answer | The admission settles `failed` with detail `lease_timeout` (the person may send again); the turn never runs unserialised | P17-02 (`admissions.md`) |
| D-16.363 | Durable turn lease (P17-03, R03.20, R06.2) | Lease row without a generation; settings from config | The lease row carries the generation (refresh and release are keyed by holder **and** generation); the holder's pid namespace falls back to `host:<name>` where `/proc/self/ns/pid` is missing; holder liveness needs same host and namespace before any pid probe; timings are constants (TTL 300 s, refresh 60 s, write patience 20 s, margin 2 s, wait 1,800 s, in-process slots 512), no config rows; the P02 per-session file lock is superseded for lease-taking hosts (D-16.69) | P17-03 (`turn-lease.md`) |
| D-16.364 | Turn liveness watchdog (P17-04) | Sleep time discounted; stack dump on abort | Person waits (approval, clarify) count as activity explicitly; no stack dump; no sleep discount (Linux monotonic time stops in suspend; macOS recorded as a follow-up); `stalled` travels as the failure reason beside `interrupted_by_system` until P17-25 decides on an `ExitReason` row | P17-04 (`turn-liveness.md`) |
| D-16.365 | Generation fences (P17-05, R01.4, R06.2) | An interrupt naming the session's last generation succeeds even after that turn ended; generation checked before the request lookup; stop by admission only | Interrupt of a finished turn is `stale_generation` with `current: None`; an answer for a withdrawn request is a quiet no-op even when its turn ended; stale is a returned value (`StaleGeneration`, error code `runtime.stale_generation` shared with the ledger); `stop(session_id, admission_id)` and an admission of **another session is `not_found`** (integrator); redirect is not fenced (P04 path) | P17-05 (`generation-fences.md`) |
| D-16.366 | Fence wiring in the admission worker (P17-02 + P17-05 integration) | — | `AdmissionService(fences=)`: `begin` at each claim, `adopt` at once with the claim's ticket (it keeps an early stop itself, so a fenced stop also ends a lease wait; a steer before the runner binds its hook is `rejected`, never latched), `release` when the turn has ended, before the settle; soft interrupt → `interrupted`, hard interrupt and `stop` → `cancelled`, system → `interrupted_by_system`; `GenerationFences.bind_admissions` breaks the construction cycle | Integration (LEAD-NOTES "P17 wave 1") |
| D-16.367 | Event log and replay (P17-06) | Frames batched on the socket; process cap from the oldest inserted session; no epoch → no check | Held `message.delta`/`reasoning.delta` pieces are joined into one event before numbering (33 ms); the process cap takes from the least recently active session; `since` without an epoch or with a cursor past the newest seq is a `session.replay_gap` (`epoch_changed` / `evicted` / `ahead`); `forget` drops a closed session's buffer; per-session counters and eviction watermarks are kept for the daemon's life | P17-06 (`event-log.md`) |
| D-16.368 | Server-request re-delivery (P17-07, R05.3, R02.5) | `-32601` ends the request at once; all-declined becomes a canned "not shown" tool result; `request.cancel{reason}`; one process-wide table; ids `sr_…` | `-32601` (or any other error) leaves the request to the other viewers and it settles `undeliverable` only when none is left (the asker gets `RequestUnsupported`, distinct from timeout or deny); 4404 counts only from a viewer that advertised the decline capability; `request.settled{outcome: answered/withdrawn/undeliverable}` (P04's `server_request.cancelled` kept beside it for in-process surfaces); one `RequestBook` per session; ids `srq-<12 hex>` | P17-07 (`request-redelivery.md`) |
| D-16.369 | Refusal and failure details (P17-01/02 integration, security) | Exception text stored as is | Every ledger refusal detail and every settle detail is one printable line of at most 160 characters with credential shapes masked (`foundation.redact`), absolute paths cut to `…/<last part>`, and — for turn failures — the scope's own secret values masked by the P05 redactor in force mode | Integration (LEAD-NOTES "P17 wave 1") |
| D-16.380 | Signal adapter (P16-05) | Python wrapper of signal-cli; markdown turned into Signal text styles; attachments downloaded and cached for every envelope; a process-wide attachment token bucket; the stream wait reset on every open; mention offsets read as code points; every message in an allowed group answered | Our own client over httpx for a signal-cli daemon the user runs (GPL-3.0, never bundled, imported or started by us); plain text; message id = the send timestamp; attachments fetched lazily by the intake for admitted messages only (≤100 MiB, type sniffed from magic bytes); no bucket model: one retry after the server's stated refill (default 4 s), a longer wait handed back without sleeping (R06.3); the stream wait (2 → 60 s) resets only after data or 30 s up; mention offsets in UTF-16 units; groups need a mention unless `require_mention: false`; send errors classified (unregistered → chat gone, safety number → forbidden, network → transient) | P16-05 (`signal.md`) |
| D-16.381 | Email mailbox (P16-09) | A fresh IMAP login every 15 s; "(empty email)" turned into a prompt; `X-Auto-Response-Suppress` dropped | One persistent IMAP session (IDLE where Python and the server offer it, else a 60 s check) on the bounded `email` pool; mail already in the inbox at first start is never answered; the seen list survives the fresh adapter built per reconnect (per-process snapshot keyed by address and IMAP host); an empty mail is ignored; `X-Auto-Response-Suppress` alone drops nothing (Exchange sets it on ordinary mail); replies carry `Auto-Submitted: auto-replied` (RFC 3834) and thread by `In-Reply-To` / `References`; a reply over 50,000 characters goes out as threaded `(i/n)` mails (the plan's "attachment" form needs a core switch: follow-up) | P16-09 (`email.md`) |
| D-16.382 | Email sender trust (P16-09, security) | Allowlist compared on the From address alone | A From address counts only when the **topmost** `Authentication-Results` header carries the pinned `authserv_id` (`platforms.email.extra.authserv_id` / `EMAIL_AUTHSERV_ID`) and DMARC pass, or one aligned SPF pass, or an aligned DKIM pass (comments and quotes cannot smuggle verdicts); no pin → nothing is trusted (logged once with the fix; `require_authenticated_sender: false` switches the check off); screening runs on a header-only fetch capped at 64 KiB (From ≤2,048 characters) before any body download; strangers are dropped before they become events (email's unknown-sender default is `ignore`, R06.8); HTML bodies become text with no remote content fetched; allowlist entries compared lower-cased | P16-09 (`email.md`) |
| D-16.383 | Email sending (P16-09) | One generic failure; IPv4 fallback; a standalone sender | SMTP failures classified (recipient refused → chat gone, 5.7.x → forbidden, 4xx → retry or rate limit with a stated wait, 552 → too long, a break after hand-over → maybe delivered); a refused SMTP login stops the retry loop; no IPv4 fallback; `indus send` uses the adapter's send-only open (D-16.387) | P16-09 (`email.md`) |
| D-16.384 | Matrix adapter, plain (P16-06) | mautrix (MPL-2.0) client; `auto_thread` on; `m.notice` dropped; sync errors retried every 5 s; the working reaction removed after 5 s; raw HTML passed through | Our own Client-Server API client over httpx (no SDK; mautrix is left for P16-07 E2EE); the token only in the `Authorization` header, no redirects followed, media only from the configured homeserver (authenticated route first, legacy fallback); sync backoff 2 → 60 s; `m.notice` passed on marked `is_bot` (the core's allow-bots rule decides); `auto_thread` off by default; a thread the bot takes part in needs no new mention (`thread_require_mention`); HTML generated escaped from the Markdown (no sanitiser needed); `e2ee: off / optional / required` seam — `required` fails fatal (`e2ee_unavailable`) until P16-07 | P16-06 (`matrix.md`) |
| D-16.385 | Matrix outbound limits and mentions (P16-06, R06.11) | Characters only; `@room` as written | Pieces of ≤15,000 UTF-8 bytes, event content ≤45,000 bytes (HTML left out when it would burst the event); every message carries `m.mentions` with the users the reply names, `@room` only with `allow_room_mentions`; a reply relation only on the first piece | P16-06 (`matrix.md`) |
| D-16.386 | Mattermost adapter (P16-08) | Token sent in an `authentication_challenge` frame; mass mentions left to a post property; every file downloaded at once | REST v4 over httpx, the event socket over httpx-ws (`mattermost` extra, MIT; already locked via `daytona`); the token only in the `Authorization` header, the socket upgrade included; `@channel` / `@all` / `@here` outside code defused in the text; `reply_mode: thread` (default off); files downloaded on demand by the intake; video files are video events; socket backoff 2 → 60 s with up to 20 % jitter | P16-08 (`mattermost.md`) |
| D-16.387 | Send-only open on the P16 adapters (lead ruling, extends D-16.334) | — | All four read `AdapterConfig.send_only` when built (E3's `inbound_enabled` attribute removed): Matrix calls `whoami` and never syncs, Mattermost calls `users/me` and never opens the socket — even when a sink is installed (their sink-gated start is kept for the daemon); Signal checks the daemon and opens no event stream or health monitor; Email probes SMTP and never logs in to IMAP. Each adapter has an `indus send` test through the real builder | integrator (P16 E2+E3 landing) |
| D-16.388 | Media fetchers on the P16 adapters (lead ruling, extends D-16.335) | — | `register_fetchers(seat)` on all four (E2's `register_fetchers(intake)` typed to the seat, E3's `fetch_attachment` registered by it): Matrix `mxc://` through the homeserver, Mattermost files through the server (both with a size cap), Signal `getAttachment` (base64, sniffed, 100 MiB), Email parts held in memory (32 per message, 64 MiB, 30 min) | integrator (P16 E2+E3 landing) |
| D-16.389 | Fatal platform state on the P16 adapters (lead ruling, extends D-16.333) | Logged; the listener stops | E2's adapter-private `lost_handler(reason, fatal)` and E3's proposed `report_failure` replaced by `Adapter.report_fatal`: `matrix_auth` (sync answers 401), `mattermost_auth` (socket upgrade refused 401/403), `missing_dependency` (httpx-ws missing when the socket opens), `email_auth` (IMAP login refused after connect; the mailbox loop stops, no retry that could lock the account). Signal has none (the daemon holds no credential of ours); its stream failures retry | integrator (P16 E2+E3 landing) |
| D-16.390 | Registry rows and credentials for Signal, Matrix, Mattermost, Email (lead ruling) | — | Built-in rows (order telegram, discord, slack, signal, matrix, mattermost, email, api_server, webhook): Signal probe `httpx`, `required_env` `SIGNAL_HTTP_URL` + `SIGNAL_ACCOUNT`, cap 8,000, `group_chats_env` `INDUS_SIGNAL_GROUP_ALLOWED_CHATS`, install hint names signal-cli; Matrix probe `httpx` (no extra), `MATRIX_ACCESS_TOKEN` + `MATRIX_HOMESERVER`, 15,000; Mattermost probe `httpx_ws` (extra `mattermost`), `MATTERMOST_TOKEN` + `MATTERMOST_URL`, 4,000; Email probe `imaplib`/`smtplib` (no extra), `EMAIL_ADDRESS`, `EMAIL_PASSWORD`, `EMAIL_IMAP_HOST`, `EMAIL_SMTP_HOST`, 50,000. `BUILTIN_CREDENTIALS` (enablement, masking, token lock): signal `SIGNAL_ACCOUNT`, matrix `MATRIX_ACCESS_TOKEN`, mattermost `MATTERMOST_TOKEN`, email `EMAIL_ADDRESS` + `EMAIL_PASSWORD`; server URLs and hosts are never in the table (read by the adapters, not masked). Config rows `platforms.signal` / `matrix` / `mattermost` / `email` | integrator (P16 E2+E3 landing) |
| D-16.391 | Token-lock keys (lead ruling) | Signal locked on the phone number | The daemon locks on the platform's credential values joined, so Signal locks on `SIGNAL_ACCOUNT` alone (two gateways on one number conflict whatever their daemon URLs), Matrix and Mattermost on the token, Email on address + password. The ruled email key (address + IMAP host) needs a per-platform lock key the registry does not have: open seam | integrator (P16 E2+E3 landing) |
| D-16.392 | Matrix send/cron addresses (P16-06 → P16-32) | — | `matrix.split_target` (`!room:server:$event` → room + thread) is **not** registered with `cron.targets` (P16-32's per-platform target seam does not exist yet), so `indus send matrix:!room:server` is split at the first `:` until P16-32 lands: open seam | integrator (P16 E2+E3 landing) |
| D-16.393 | Adapter contract suite (P16 shared change) | — | `AdapterCase.lacks` names inbound shapes a platform cannot express (Signal and Email: no threads or bot flags); the inbound check then expects no event for them; `_long_text` builds linearly (same output; the quadratic loop took 6.7 s at a 50,000 cap); one case module per platform in `tests/contract/gateway/` (`<platform>_case.py`, imported once by the conftest; E2's case builders moved out of the wire fakes, E3's `case_*` modules renamed) | integrator (P16 E2+E3 landing) |
| D-16.394 | Signal account in request logs (security, integrator) | — | The event-stream request gets `account=<number>` inside the transport (`signal.adapter._AccountQuery`), so httpx's request log line, `response.request` and error texts never carry the phone number (the log setup pins httpx at WARNING; the test turns it on); every other log, repr and error masks the number (`mask_number`, credential scrubbing) | integrator (P16 E2+E3 landing) |
| D-16.400 | WhatsApp Business Cloud adapter (P16-04) | Binds every interface; starts without the app secret and answers 503; Graph v20.0; raw error text; approve / deny only; long prompt bodies cut; the first ten clarify choices kept; taps resolved in the adapter with a text fallback; non-voice audio, stickers and locations mapped to voice / photo / text; an adapter-owned DM policy | Our own Graph API client over httpx on the P16-01 kit: loopback bind by default; Graph `v23.0` (`adapter.DEFAULT_API_VERSION`); a send outside the 24-hour window, a refused token and throughput limits come back typed (the window: `forbidden`, final, tried once); every approval choice offered (≤3 reply buttons, else a list), prompts over the 1,024 body and more than ten clarify choices go as the text card; taps are core button events checked against the prompt's own ids; `audio`, `sticker`, `location` kinds kept; the DM gate is the core authorization chain (`INDUS_WHATSAPP_CLOUD_ALLOWED_USERS`); user id = the `wa_id` digits (alt `+digits`); `preview_url` only when the piece holds a URL; wamid dedupe 5,000, interactive state 1,000 (stored only after a successful send) | P16-04 (`whatsapp-cloud.md`) |
| D-16.401 | WhatsApp formatting (P16-04 → P15-15) | — | `Capabilities.markup = "markdown"` (the sender's Discord-style pass: tables to code blocks) and the adapter turns each piece into WhatsApp syntax (`whatsapp_cloud.markup.to_whatsapp`: bold `*`, italic `_`, strike `~`, headers bold, links `label (url)`). Proposed follow-up: a `"whatsapp"` `Markup` style in `outbound/formatting.py` and drop the adapter-side pass | P16-04 (`whatsapp-cloud.md`) |
| D-16.402 | WhatsApp webhook (P16-04, security) | Unsigned bodies accepted when no app secret is set | `WHATSAPP_CLOUD_APP_SECRET` missing = connect refused (`whatsapp_cloud_app_secret_missing`, fatal; send-only needs none); body ≤3 MiB before it is read; `X-Hub-Signature-256` checked on the raw body in constant time before any JSON (401); the GET handshake compares the verify token in constant time (no token 503, wrong 403, unsafe challenge 400); only our phone-number id and WABA count; media ids checked against a safe charset; the bearer token only on Meta hosts; numbers masked (`mask_number`) and the token, app secret and verify token scrubbed everywhere | P16-04 (`whatsapp-cloud.md`) |
| D-16.403 | SMS adapter (P16-10) | `SMS_WEBHOOK_*` env names; an insecure no-signature mode; media ignored and caption-less MMS dropped; keyword messages and `OptOutType` start turns; the inbound text logged | Our own Twilio REST client over httpx on the P16-01 kit; the webhook URL is `platforms.sms.webhook_public_url` (config, no env bridge); MMS inbound kept as attachments (fetched from Twilio only), outbound MMS refused (needs a public media URL); keywords / opt-outs and repeated `MessageSid`s never start a turn; a message to another number of the account dropped; Twilio codes typed (unsubscribed and region: final + forbidden; invalid number: chat gone; 20429 rate limited; 21617 too long; 20003 final + fatal `sms_auth`); UTF-16 lengths; the inbound text never logged; user id = E.164 (alt digits); markdown removed by the P15 plain formatter | P16-10 (`sms.md`) |
| D-16.404 | SMS webhook (P16-10, security, D-16.25) | Signature skipped without a configured URL | No public URL = connect refused (`sms_public_url_missing`, fatal; send-only needs none); body ≤64 KiB before it is read; `X-Twilio-Signature` = HMAC-SHA1 over the public URL (query included, tried with and without the default port) + the sorted form fields, constant time, 403 on a missing or wrong header; an empty auth token never verifies; the Account SID, auth token and our number are credentials (masked) | P16-10 (`sms.md`) |
| D-16.405 | LINE adapter (P16-23) | Stops at five bubbles and ellipsises the rest; pushes after any reply failure; three adapter allowlists; "[unsupported message type]" text; holds a slow answer until a tap forever; binds every interface | Our own Messaging API client over httpx on the P16-01 kit (port 8646); every bubble delivered in calls of five; reply token used while younger than 50 s, else push; push only when the token is refused or the reply never reached LINE (a timed-out reply may have landed, R06.4); cap 20,000 UTF-16 units in 4,500-unit bubbles; a long server wait handed back and the monthly quota never retried (R06.3); "still working" postback after 45 s (0 disables), the held answer pushed after `slow_reply_hold_seconds` (600); the core authorization chain; unknown message types dropped; loopback bind; a token revoked while running reports `line_auth` fatal (integrator) | P16-23 (`line.md`) |
| D-16.406 | LINE webhook and served media (P16-23, security) | Media served with an allowed-roots check at serve time | `X-Line-Signature` (HMAC-SHA256 base64) on the raw body in constant time before parsing (401); media LINE fetches is served from `/line/media?t=<token_urlsafe(32)>` below the public **https** origin of `webhook_public_url`, valid 1,800 s (410 after, 404 unknown), only paths the sender's media policy already validated (R06.22), ≤50 MiB; files refused; media refused in send-only (nobody would serve it); unknown audio duration 60 s | P16-23 (`line.md`) |
| D-16.407 | ntfy adapter (P16-26) | Loses what was published during a reconnect gap; truncates long text; a fixed backoff ladder; 401 only is fatal | JSON stream per topic set (several topics), reconnect with `since=<last id>`, silence watchdog 90 s, backoff 2 → 60 s; text over 4,096 bytes refused (the sender splits first); 401 and 403 fatal (`ntfy_auth`, at connect and while running); topic names validated; replies to `reply_topic` (else the topic); `NTFY_TOKEN` (Bearer, or `user:pass` Basic) sent only to an https server or one on this machine; every topic reports "dm" but `dm_is_private=false`, so pairing codes never go there (R06.24); the topic is the user id | P16-26 (`ntfy.md`) |
| D-16.408 | IRC adapter (P16-27) | Fire-and-forget lines; unaddressed channel lines dropped in the adapter; unbounded nick retries; a global 0.3 s sleep; 510-byte lines; no SASL; passwords over plain connections | asyncio streams with TLS by default; each line confirmed (echo or PING); unaddressed channel lines go to the core's mention policy; nick collision retried three times (`_` appended); 0.5 s per line per target; 450-byte payloads cut on UTF-8 boundaries within the 512 bytes with CRLF (R06.11); SASL PLAIN (`IRC_SASL_PASSWORD`, a new env name), `PASS`, NickServ `IDENTIFY`; any password over a plain connection refused (`irc_plaintext_auth`, fatal) unless `allow_plaintext_auth: true`; `user_id_alt` = the lower-case nick; `indus send` connects as `<nick>-send` and joins only the target; a ban is fatal (`irc_banned`); FAIL codes best-effort | P16-27 (`irc.md`) |
| D-16.409 | Send-only, fetchers and fatal state on the E4/E5 adapters (lead rulings D-16.333 … 335, 387 … 389) | — | All five read `AdapterConfig.send_only` (WhatsApp and SMS start no listener and need no app secret / public URL; LINE starts no listener and refuses media; ntfy opens no subscription; IRC connects as `<nick>-send`), each with an `indus send` test through the real builder; `register_fetchers` on WhatsApp (two-step Graph download), SMS (Twilio media) and LINE (content endpoint); ntfy and IRC download nothing; `report_fatal`: `whatsapp_cloud_auth`, `sms_auth`, `line_auth`, `ntfy_auth`, `irc_banned` / `irc_auth` | integrator (P16 E4+E5 landing) |
| D-16.410 | Registry rows and credentials for WhatsApp Cloud, SMS, LINE, ntfy, IRC (lead ruling) | — | Built-in rows after email (order … email, whatsapp_cloud, sms, line, ntfy, irc, api_server, webhook), all with no extra: WhatsApp Cloud probe `httpx`, `WHATSAPP_CLOUD_ACCESS_TOKEN` + `WHATSAPP_CLOUD_PHONE_NUMBER_ID`, 4,096; SMS `httpx`, `TWILIO_ACCOUNT_SID`, `TWILIO_AUTH_TOKEN`, `TWILIO_PHONE_NUMBER`, 1,600; LINE `httpx`, `LINE_CHANNEL_ACCESS_TOKEN` + `LINE_CHANNEL_SECRET`, 20,000; ntfy `httpx`, `NTFY_TOPIC`, 4,096, `dm_is_private=false`; IRC `ssl`, `IRC_SERVER` + `IRC_NICKNAME`, 450. `BUILTIN_CREDENTIALS` (enablement, masking): whatsapp_cloud token + phone-number id, sms SID + token + number, line token + secret. Extra secrets read by the adapters: `WHATSAPP_CLOUD_APP_SECRET` / `_VERIFY_TOKEN` / `_WABA_ID`, `NTFY_TOKEN`, `IRC_SERVER_PASSWORD` / `IRC_SASL_PASSWORD` / `IRC_NICKSERV_PASSWORD`. Config rows `platforms.whatsapp_cloud` / `platforms.sms`; LINE, ntfy and IRC use free `platforms.<name>` keys | integrator (P16 E4+E5 landing) |
| D-16.411 | Public env names (integrator, follows from D-16.412) | — | `gateway.config.BUILTIN_PUBLIC_ENV`: ntfy `NTFY_TOPIC`, irc `IRC_SERVER` + `IRC_NICKNAME`. Like credentials, all present turn the platform on from the environment or secret store alone, but they are not in `PlatformSettings.credentials`, so `Adapter.scrub` never masks a nick, server or topic (IRC and ntfy passwords/tokens are still scrubbed by their adapters). `load_gateway_settings(public=)` takes another table as `credentials=` does | integrator (P16 E4+E5 landing) |
| D-16.412 | Per-platform token-lock key (lead ruling; closes the D-16.391 seam) | Scoped locks per platform (the IRC server + nick is the canonical example) | `PlatformEntry.lock_env`: when set, the daemon's token lock is keyed on those values (read like credentials, joined in order; `gateway.config.lock_key`) instead of the credential values; the daemon remembers the key it locked on so the release matches even if the secret store changed. IRC = `IRC_SERVER` + `IRC_NICKNAME`, ntfy = `NTFY_TOPIC` (neither in `BUILTIN_CREDENTIALS`), email = `EMAIL_ADDRESS` + `EMAIL_IMAP_HOST` (the password is no longer part of the key: a new password is the same mailbox); Signal keeps `SIGNAL_ACCOUNT`; every other platform its credentials. Lock file names stay `<platform>-<sha256[:16]>` (R06.16) | integrator (P16 E4+E5 landing) |
| D-16.413 | Phone-number masking helper (integrator) | — | `mask_number` / `mask_numbers` (every digit but the last four hidden, separators kept) moved from the WhatsApp Cloud and SMS adapters into `gateway/adapters/_identity.py`; both adapters import it (Signal keeps its own E.164 form, D-16.394) | integrator (P16 E4+E5 landing) |
| D-16.414 | Adapter contract suite (P16 shared change, extends D-16.393) | — | `AdapterCase.lacks` gains `media` (no attachment of any kind may arrive: ntfy, IRC) and `caption` (a photo arrives without its caption: LINE); three new case modules (`irc_case`, `line_case`, `ntfy_case`) and two (`sms_case`, `whatsapp_cloud_case`), one import each in the conftest. Acceptance ids: ACC-K9-2 (WhatsApp Cloud), ACC-K10-4 (SMS), ACC-K12-5 (LINE), ACC-K12-7 (ntfy), ACC-K12-8 (IRC), numbered by the platform's place in the inventory row (K12: BlueBubbles 1 … Raft 11) | integrator (P16 E4+E5 landing) |
| D-16.420 | Daemon session host (P17-08) | Live-session record per subscription id; the session object is the TUI registry's | `DaemonSessionHost` composes the P04 `SessionService` (no subclass) and adds the daemon's door, fences, event log and request books; viewers (`Viewer`: connection id, surface `cli`/`tui`/`gui`/`acp`/`web`/`messaging`, capabilities) are keyed by connection id, one per connection and session; a session is keyed by the id it was first attached under across rotating compactions (events carry the current id); the host owns the service (`aclose` closes it) | P17-08 (`daemon-host.md`) |
| D-16.421 | ACP session lifetime (P17-08) | Live entry dropped on the last detach | An `acp`-source session ends after its last viewer leaves only when nothing runs or waits in its queue (row ended `acp_close`); with work left it ends when the queue drains unless a viewer comes back; unviewed ACP sessions found only in the store at daemon stop are unloaded, not ended | P17-08 (`daemon-host.md`) |
| D-16.422 | Busy input per surface (P17-08, R05.18) | Per-client busy mode | Input sent during a running turn is admitted durably first; with no explicit mode the TUI queues and every other surface interrupts (soft, only the turn running when the input was admitted) | P17-08 (`daemon-host.md`) |
| D-16.423 | Host turn runner (P17-08 + wave-1 wiring) | — | A stop that came before the runner bound its hooks means the turn never starts; a lease won after waiting reloads the session (the old agent is closed, its cleanups run; plugins' `session_started` not re-run); a hard interrupt or fenced stop also stops subagents; turns the service would start itself (host injections, subagent results, unconsumed steer text) are admitted through the ledger as `internal:<origin>` | P17-08 (`daemon-host.md`) |
| D-16.424 | Frozen creation policy (P17-09, R04.1) | Frozen fields compared on resume; bypass launch must name a model | Same fields plus `pass_session_id`, kept in the session's `model_config["launch"]` (shared with `--local`); a bypass launch (safe mode / ignore user config) must name model **and** provider; unknown launch fields are refused (`invalid_field`); a field never frozen counts as the default, so naming it on resume conflicts; `policy_conflict` lists every differing field; aliases compared as typed (P17-18 resolves) | P17-09 (`creation-policy.md`) |
| D-16.425 | Secrets in the launch policy (P17-09, security) | Key reference forwarded | An `api_key` in a launch is dropped (never stored nor compared); a credential-shaped `key_ref` or a base URL with user info is refused `runtime.policy_refused` (`secret_in_policy`) without echoing the value; codes `runtime.policy_conflict` / `runtime.policy_refused` in `ERROR_CODES` (P17-25 maps them) | P17-09 (`creation-policy.md`) |
| D-16.426 | Session row on create (P17-08/09) | — | `create` writes the session row at once only when a policy is given (to freeze it); otherwise the first turn writes it as in-process; the P02 per-session file lock stays held beside the durable lease until P17-18 | P17-08 (`daemon-host.md`) |
| D-16.427 | WebSocket tickets (P17-10, R08.4) | Ticket kept after a wrong-purpose redeem; dict lookup only | SHA-256 digest stored with purpose, profile id, instance id and a 30 s expiry; the stored and computed digests are compared with `hmac.compare_digest`; **any** failed redemption of a known ticket spends it, and every ticket offered in a refused upgrade is spent; spent markers answer `used` until expiry; at most 1,024 live tickets and (integrator) 4 spent markers per live slot (`capacity`) | P17-10 (`tickets.md`) |
| D-16.428 | Upgrade check (P17-10, R09.2) | Forwarded subject and host scope; query tickets read | `authenticate_upgrade` is the one helper (service layer): non-loopback or address-less peer → 4403, Origin present and not allow-listed → 4403, missing/doubled/bad ticket or no `indus-gateway-v1` → 4401; a ticket in the query string is never used, it is spent and logged without its value; no forwarded subject or `host` scope (P24); `profile_id` = 16 hex of SHA-256 of the resolved home, `instance_id` random per daemon start | P17-10 (`tickets.md`) |
| D-16.429 | Ticket redaction (P17-10, lead ruling) | — | `indus-gateway-ticket.<t>` and a `ticket` query value are masked **whole** (`[redacted]`, no leading or trailing characters kept), unlike other credential shapes (`abcd…wxyz`) | integrator (P17 wave 2) |
| D-16.430 | Attach verbs (P17-11) | Caller must name profile and instance; symlinked home refused | `session-ticket {purpose, profile_id?, instance_id?}` and `describe` (adds `protocol_version`); the control server enforces owner-only by verb name and answers `forbidden` where the OS cannot confirm the peer; the server refuses to mint from a home writable by others; a symlinked home is allowed (the target is checked); refusal codes are plain strings on the control wire (`bad_params`, `bad_purpose`, `profile_mismatch`, `stale_instance`, `unsafe_home`, `ws_unavailable`, `capacity`) | P17-11 (`attach-verbs.md`) |
| D-16.431 | Ticket client checks (P17-11, security) | — | `gateway_control.session_ticket` accepts an answer only with a ticket of the minted shape, a credential-free loopback `ws://`/`wss://` URL with a port and no query or fragment, and the ids it expected (`bad_response` / `profile_mismatch` / `stale_instance` otherwise, ticket dropped); `TicketReply` hides the ticket from `repr` | P17-11 (`attach-verbs.md`) |
| D-16.432 | Shared RPC dispatcher (P17-12) | Per-handler `4000` for an unknown key; types left to handlers; long handlers on a thread pool; retirement gate | One dispatcher in `service/rpc` (P12's `tui_bridge/dispatch.py` a thin layer): only declared names register (import-time refusal); full params validation before the handler (`UNKNOWN_PARAM_KEY` with the key path); async handlers with per-call deadlines (120 s, `prompt.submit`/`prompt.background` 1,800 s, `session.compress` 600 s) answered `INTERNAL_ERROR` with `retryable`; crashes logged by method name only, never params; no retirement gate (drain refuses at admission) | P17-12 (`rpc-dispatch.md`) |
| D-16.433 | Host attach descriptor (P17-08 + P17-11 integration) | — | `DaemonSessionHost.creatable_sources()` = `cli tui gui acp web` (never `messaging`: the gateway creates those) and `capabilities()` = `events.since`, `server_requests`, `server_requests.<kind>` for each kind, `request.decline` (client feature vocabulary; P17-25 owns the table); both plain reads, so `describe` answers while turns run | integrator (P17 wave 2) |
| D-16.434 | Attach verbs in the daemon (P17-11 wiring) | — | The control part registers `describe` / `session-ticket` over a `TicketBook` made per daemon start and revokes it at stop; until P17-13 mounts the WebSocket endpoint `ws_url` is null, `session-ticket` answers `ws_unavailable` without minting, and `describe` lists no sources or capabilities (the daemon builds no session host yet) | integrator (P17 wave 2) |
| D-16.435 | Replay is for viewers (P17-08, security, integrator) | — | `events_since(..., connection_id=)` from a connection not attached to that session answers `SESSION_NOT_FOUND` (a viewer only replays sessions it attached; requests are re-offered only to it) | integrator (P17 wave 2) |
| D-16.440 | Microsoft Teams adapter (P16-11) | The vendor SDK (`microsoft-teams-apps`, which also loads a `.env` at import); binds every interface; send-only (no edits); card presses checked against `TEAMS_ALLOWED_USERS` in the adapter; replies through the reply endpoint with a flat fallback; images from any public host; documents as data URIs | Our own Bot Connector client over httpx on the P16-01 kit (loopback, port 3978, `/api/messages`, 1 MiB): Adaptive Card 1.4 approvals with a text fallback, card presses (`invoke` `adaptiveCard/action`) answered at once and handed to the core's requester/admin rule; edits (`PUT` activity); conversation references in `<home>/gateway/teams_conversations.json` (0600, 2,000) so cron and `indus send` reach a conversation after a restart; replies posted with `replyToId` (no fallback needed); attachments only from Microsoft hosts (the bot token to Bot Connector / `*.asm.skype.com` only, SharePoint links without it); images inline, other files refused (file consent not built); the tenant id is the session scope; no `teams` extra. Not in 1.0: the meeting pipeline (D-16.87) | P16-11 (`teams.md`) |
| D-16.441 | Teams inbound token and service URL (P16-11, security; lead ruling) | Token validation left to the SDK | Bearer JWT checked before the body is read: three strict base64url segments ≤8 KiB, `alg` RS256 only (never `none`, HMAC or another padding), `kid` required, no `crit`; header URLs (`jku`, `x5u`, `jwk`) never fetched; keys only from the Bot Framework OpenID metadata (`jwks_uri` https on the metadata host), 24 h cache, an unknown `kid` refreshes at most every 5 minutes, a failed refresh keeps the held keys, keys of 2,048–8,192 bits; signature by `cryptography` (RSASSA-PKCS1-v1_5 / SHA-256, D-16.451 — the builder's own RSA arithmetic was replaced at landing); issuer `https://api.botframework.com`, audience our app id (string or list), `exp` / `nbf` with 300 s skew; key endorsements must name the channel and a `serviceUrl` claim must equal the activity's; service URLs https, port 443, no userinfo/query, exact hosts (`smba.trafficmanager.net` and the two US-government hosts, plus `extra_service_hosts`); refused URLs are never stored or called (SSRF); client secret and tokens scrubbed everywhere | P16-11 (`teams.md`), integrator |
| D-16.442 | Microsoft Graph change-notification receiver (P16-12) | Platform `msgraph_webhook`; binds every interface (CIDRs required); resource checked before clientState; the raw notification dumped into the prompt; subscription upkeep in a separate CLI renewing within 24 h of expiry; counters in `/health` | Platform `msgraph`, a core-style ingress (`trusted_inbound`, `dm_is_private=false`, never a chat): loopback by default, a public bind needs `allowed_source_cidrs`; validation echo `text/plain`, ≤1,024 printable characters (else 400, no echo); clientState (16–128 printable, `MSGRAPH_WEBHOOK_CLIENT_STATE`) compared in constant time first, an all-forged batch 403; receipts by id or content digest for 1 h; events internal with `allow_control` false and a sanitised six-field summary framed as outside data (template placeholders limited to those fields); subscriptions created and renewed at 80 % of their lifetime inside the receiver (404 recreates; `reauthorizationRequired` / `subscriptionRemoved` handled), ids in `<home>/gateway/msgraph_subscriptions.json` (0600) | P16-12 (`msgraph.md`) |
| D-16.443 | Graph replies (P16-12; lead ruling + integrator wiring) | Replies logged | A reply goes to `platforms.msgraph.deliver_to` (one `platform:chat` on another platform; `msgraph:` refused at config) through the daemon's delivery router: `RouterPart` gives every `GraphReceiver` that comes up a forward (`delivery.parts.graph_forward`) and never attaches the receiver as a target (`msgraph` also in `send_command._NOT_TARGETS` and the channel directory's). No route (no `deliver_to`, or not wired) → `msgraph.adapter.not_delivered()`: `ok` false, `unsupported` (no retry, no failure notice), `extra["undelivered"] = "not_configured"`, counted in `counts["undelivered"]`, one warning per receiver naming the setting — never reported delivered. A failed forward is final (`kind` unknown / bad format, not retried by the Graph side) | integrator (P16 E6+E7+E8 landing) |
| D-16.444 | Google Chat adapter (P16-13) | Google SDKs (google-auth, Pub/Sub gRPC); ADC and key-file paths; any 403 on a send is fatal; 429/5xx retried three times inline; card clicks only acknowledged; per-thread counter file; a "thinking…" card; per-user OAuth for native uploads | Our own Chat and Pub/Sub REST clients over httpx with `cryptography` (extra `google-chat`, lazily imported): HTTP callbacks on the kit (loopback, 8647, `/google-chat`, 1 MiB) or Pub/Sub pull (ack after hand-on; backoff 2 s doubling to 120 s; 401/403/404 or ten failures in a row fatal); 503 + `Retry-After` while draining (the base drain probe, D-16.452) and no pulls; dedupe by message name; DM/group/thread from `spaceType` and `threadReply`; card clicks become core button events; a 403 is a forbidden chat (others keep working); retries left to the core (R06.4); a 401 refreshes the token once then is final; edits patch text and drop cards; media sends refused cleanly (no per-user OAuth); no typing marker | P16-13 (`google-chat.md`) |
| D-16.445 | Google Chat credentials and callback tokens (P16-13, security) | Expected service-account email required for HTTP mode; ID-token check through google-auth | `GOOGLE_CHAT_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_JSON` holds the key JSON itself (secret store, then env; never a path, no metadata-server lookups); a `token_uri` other than Google's three https token endpoints is refused before anything is signed; assertion RS256, token cached until a minute before expiry, one fetch at a time; key and token never in errors/logs/reprs.
