| D-16.607 | `admission.cancel` on the daemon (integrator, lead decision 3) | — | New attach method `admission.cancel {session_id, admission_id}` → `{cancelled, state, generation?, outcome?}`: drops a queued input of a session this connection watches (`AdmissionService.cancel_queued`; the ledger refuses a claimed row in the same transaction); a started input is answered `cancelled: false, state: started` with its generation and is never stopped by admission id — the ACP attached host then interrupts that generation; another session's admission is `ADMISSION_NOT_FOUND` | integrator |
| D-16.608 | `session.usage` on the daemon (integrator, lead decision 3) | — | The daemon's WebSocket serves `session.usage` for a watched session from `usage_numbers` (D-16.556), the shape of the turn-end `session.usage` event; the ACP attached host's `usage_update` after `session/new` reads it | integrator |
| D-16.609 | ACP attach descriptor (integrator, lead decision 3) | The adapter describes the daemon before dialling | The attached host keeps no `describe` of its own: the dial checks the profile as every surface does (control-socket `describe` before the ticket, `gateway.ready` after, P17-24), then the host reads `runtime.describe` over the connection, on every reconnection, and checks its profile id again; `Admission.generation` is optional in the backend (an unclaimed daemon input has none, D-16.555) | integrator |
| D-16.610 | Sessions REST (P18-11) | Stored counters; reference snapshot names; 400 for bad bodies | Mutation snapshot `revision` = 31-bit CRC of the row's visible fields (0 = absent), `generation` = the runtime ledger counter, a stale one → 409 `stale_revision`; single delete with a live daemon runs through the maintenance gate's `delete` op (fence read-side; a daemon-side fenced verb would close a small race — follow-up); prune `older_than_days` ≥ 1 and bulk lists ≤ 500 ids, else 422; a dry-run prune needs `?profile=` on a multi-profile host (every write depends on `write_scope`); the "recent" order uses the row's own activity stamp (`state/listing.py` `order="activity"`, `count_by_source`); export is one JSON body of our portability objects | P18-11 (`rest-sessions.md`) |
| D-16.611 | Config and keys REST (P18-12) | Config GET expands values; keys in `.env`; DELETE of an unset key 404 | Config GET never expands `${NAME}` and hides credential-shaped literals (PUT puts them back); effective source `env` = from a `${NAME}` reference; `config/schema` and `config/defaults` are unscoped; credentials go to the secret store (name rule restated in `web/routes/config_view.py` beside the CLI's — move to `foundation/config/keys.py`, follow-up); keys signup URL null; DELETE of an unset key → 200 `{removed: false}` | P18-12 (`rest-config-env.md`) |
| D-16.612 | Status probe shape (P18-13) | The reference's long gateway payload | Our own public shape `{version, daemon{state, heartbeat_age_s}, platforms[{name, state}], active_sessions, recent_sessions, auth_required, auth_providers, components{store, daemon, platforms}, overall, memory{pressure, available_percent}, disk{pressure, used_percent, free_gb}}` — no paths, pids or host details; "recent 20" = active count over the 20 most recently active sessions; MiB/GiB thresholds; a status record naming a gone process reads `stopped`; memory from `/proc/meminfo` (psutil when installed, else `unknown`); takes `?profile=` | P18-13 (`rest-status-logs.md`) |
| D-16.613 | Logs and analytics REST (P18-13) | Logs `search`; per-model analytics | Log lines redacted on the way out; no `search` parameter; analytics `days` 1..365 (422 outside), calendar days in the clock's zone; only `/api/analytics/usage` | P18-13 (`rest-status-logs.md`) |
| D-16.614 | Cron fire webhook token (P18-14) | — | Our own HMAC-SHA256 token over `{profile, job_id, expires_at}`, minted by an authenticated `POST /api/cron/jobs/{id}/fire-token {ttl_s?}` (TTL 300 s, at most 3,600) under a per-process 32-byte key (a restart revokes every token); `POST /api/cron/fire` (public path) checks it in constant time before reading the body, one 401 for every token problem, a deleted job → 200 `gone`; the token is never logged | P18-14 (`rest-cron-skills-mcp.md`) |
| D-16.615 | Cron trigger / fire runs (P18-14) | A daemon "run job" verb | Trigger and fire run the job in a dashboard background thread (no daemon verb; P15 API server precedent), 409 while that job runs; delivery targets from the profile config only; blueprints listed, not instantiated; no run history | P18-14 (`rest-cron-skills-mcp.md`) |
| D-16.616 | Skills hub and plugins REST (P18-14) | Search results carry a scan verdict; install enables | A hub search downloads nothing, so `verdict` is null there (inspect and install scan); a `dangerous` skill is never installed (409 `scan_blocked` with the verdict); skills toggle is `POST`; plugin install never enables (a second call does), and enable is refused for plugins that need Python packages or provide a memory backend | P18-14 (`rest-cron-skills-mcp.md`) |
| D-16.617 | MCP REST (P18-14) | `bearer_token` provisioning; per-server redirect override | Adding a server refuses literal secrets (`${NAME}` references only; the value goes on the Keys page); `…/test` uses `write_scope` (it starts a program); remote OAuth sign-in through the dashboard reuses P13 `mcp.oauth` internals (a public `redirect_login` there is a follow-up); delete also removes the stored sign-in | P18-14 (`rest-cron-skills-mcp.md`) |
| D-16.618 | Daemon routes (P18-15) | Spawning the gateway CLI; pid signals | `GET /api/daemon` (state for the restart banner) added; start = the attach ensure; stop / restart = background actions over the control socket (`stop` verb, D-16.597), never a pid signal; `POST /api/daemon/drain {action}` is the service-bearer route and the one unscoped write (the backend's own profile unless `?profile=`) | P18-15 (`rest-channels.md`) |
| D-16.619 | Channels, pairing and webhooks REST (P18-15) | Credentials in `.env`; webhook create needs the receiver | Platform credentials go to the secret store, the whole body is validated before any write, a masked preview sent back is refused; explicit disable beats an env credential (R06.15); pairing routes live in `messaging.py`; a webhook may be created while the receiver is off (`PUT /api/webhooks/receiver` turns it on and answers `restart_needed`, no restart), its secret is ours and shown once; gateway registry and settings reached by module name (layer rule) | P18-15 (`rest-channels.md`) |
| D-16.620 | Profiles REST (P18-16) | In-process profile ops; full export with history | Create / rename / delete run `indus profile …` as a child process (`web` may not import `cli`; moving them into `service` is a follow-up); no alias script unless `alias: true`; export / import = our own zip of the profile's settings (config, persona, skills, memories, `.env` names), no `state.db`, logs, cache or secrets; a deleted profile is never recreated by a read (R04.25) | P18-16 (`rest-profiles-system.md`) |
| D-16.621 | Files REST (P18-16) | — | Machine-level (no `?profile=`); `~` against the backend scope's `HOME`, browsing from `/` without one; a locked root confines every path (links out are hidden and refused); secret-holding names refused for writes and deletes too, not only reads; uploads streamed to a hidden temp file (256 MiB) and renamed when complete; text preview instead of a data URL; downloads by ticket (D-16.596) | P18-16 (`rest-profiles-system.md`) |
| D-16.622 | System operations REST (P18-16) | sudo paths; prompt-size breakdown | Operations run as background actions (`web/actions.py`, logs masked with the profile's known secrets, status tail 1..2,000 lines); update check (24 h cache) loads `indus.cli.version` by module name (belongs in `foundation`, follow-up), apply for git installs only; no sudo case (per-user service units); shell hooks need `consent: true`; the prompt-size breakdown is not built (needs a P04 helper below `cli`; follow-up, lead decision 6) | P18-16 (`rest-profiles-system.md`) |

| D-16.627 | MCP `attachments_fetch` answer (P19-14) | Paths of a message's media | Paths, never file bytes; paths the media policy refuses are listed under `refused` with the policy's reason; an inline `data:` image is summarised (`{type: image, inline: true, media_type, bytes}`), not echoed | P19-14 (`mcp-serve-read.md`) |

| D-16.629 | MCP approval tools (P19-17, D-16.30) | Two tools advertised, never populated (R09.10) | Registered only when the daemon answers `approvals-open` with capability `approvals.v1` at start; decisions `allow-once`/`allow-session`/`allow-always`/`deny` → `once`/`session`/`always`/`deny`; the watcher reads the queue at most once a second and announces prompts already open at start; an approval in a session no viewer can show is settled `undeliverable` by the daemon (P17-07) and never reaches MCP (the server is not a viewer; follow-up if MCP-only approval is wanted) | P19-17 (`mcp-serve-approvals.md`) |
| D-16.630 | MCP `messages_send` (P19-16) | — | Owner-only control verbs `deliver-message`, `approvals-open`, `approvals-respond` on the daemon (wire constants in `indus.service.messaging_bridge`); text over 16,000 characters refused, not cut; a target the platform grammar refuses → `bad_target: …`; ledger key `mcp-send:<platform>:<chat_id>`, message ref `mcp-send:<random>`, one row per call (a client retry is a new message, never the chat conversation's key) | P19-16 (`mcp-serve-send.md`) |
| D-16.631 | ACP prompt content (P19-08) | Images passed to the agent; too many → `acp_content_invalid` | Images are staged as owner-only files under `<home>/cache/images` and named in the prompt text (neither host's submit carries attachments); 512 KiB per resource; at most 10 images, extra ones become a note instead of a refused prompt; remote images through the SSRF-guarded client | P19-08 (`acp-content.md`) |
| D-16.632 | ACP backend additions for prompts (P19-09) | — | `AcpBackend.wait` / `lost` on both hosts; the local host sends `message.complete` and `session.usage` after every turn (as the daemon host does) so one projection settles replies in both modes; notice events show as agent text segments (P19-10) | P19-09 (`acp-prompt-cancel.md`), P19-10 (`acp-projection.md`) |
| D-16.633 | ACP cards and clarify (P19-11) | A dismissed card or timeout denies | Local: a dismissed card is a denial (the editor is the only viewer); attached: dismissal, transport loss or an unknown option is not an answer, the card key is released (R09.12); multi-select clarify answers stay lists (our `ClarifyAnswer`); sudo and secret prompts are never shown in editors | P19-11 (`acp-permissions.md`) |
| D-16.634 | ACP commands and model picker (P19-12, D-16.601) | `session/set_model` with a model catalogue | Advertised commands = the desktop surface's set plus `tools`, minus `branch`/`quit`/`clear`/`history`/`config`; `AcpBackend.steer` / `run_command` and `MutationResult` status `confirm`; model guard: the context in use must fit the target window, a guarded pick asks first, a pick made mid-turn waits for the turn boundary and is refused there with a notice; never the profile default; the local host now serves `model`, `rewind` and `reset` (supersedes the first clause of D-16.606); attached `/queue` waits in the adapter (remote submit has no `when_busy`) | P19-12 (`acp-commands-model.md`) |
| D-16.635 | ACP toolset and editor MCP (P19-13) | `hermes-acp` toolset; editor servers by their own names | `indus-acp` platform toolset; `no_mcp` drops config MCP servers; editor servers registered as `editor-<name>`; the "global disabled list" is the launch's disabled toolsets (no config key for a global one); a tool call's status is the executor's ok verdict only | P19-13 (`acp-toolset.md`) |
| D-16.636 | ACP conformance fixtures (P19-18) | — | Snapshot = the vendor's four files at tag `schema-v1.19.0` (stable + unstable schema and method tables, Apache-2.0, `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES`); the names the adapter uses are collected from its code (handler table, vendor-shaped literals, the real `initialize` answer) and their expected tier lives in the drift test (`UNSTABLE`: `session/fork`, `elicitation/create`, `sessionCapabilities.fork`, `clientCapabilities.elicitation`), since our dispatcher does not gate unstable methods; the replay golden is a separate fixture (`zed_session.golden.jsonl`) and every replayed frame validates against the snapshot; the `live` editor lane skips with its reason (no headless Zed); MCP `tools/list` snapshot with and without the approval queue | P19-18 (`protocol-drift.md`) |
| D-16.637 | ACP card order and the attached model picker (P19-18 integration) | — | A permission or clarify card is sent only after the updates queued before it (the tool call it is about) are on the wire; the attached host takes the profile's `(model, provider)` through a route seam and fills the provider of a resumed session (row's `billing_provider`, else the profile's when the row runs the profile's model), so `configOptions` shows `provider:model` on both hosts — found by the transcript replay (both hosts give one golden) | P19-18 (`protocol-drift.md`) |
| D-16.638 | `session.mutate` on the daemon (D-16.602 built, w31) | — | The daemon advertises `acp.editor_policy` and serves `session.mutate {session_id, operation, payload, request_id, generation?}` for `editor_mcp`, `branch`, `workspace`, `model` (`confirm` / `deferred`), `rewind`, `reset`, `rename`, `compress`, `command` (`tools`, `status`, `usage`, `version`), only on sessions this connection attached; idle-only operations answer `SESSION_BUSY`; a foreign generation → refused `stale_generation`; idempotent by `request_id` (`IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT` when reused for another change); deadline 600 s on the daemon, 660 s in the client channel (compression is a model call) | P19 D1 (`acp-daemon-policy.md`) |
| D-16.639 | Editor MCP servers on the daemon (D-16.602, w31) | Editor servers per canonical session, fingerprints in the durable policy | The record is frozen by the first `editor_mcp` of a session (create carries no specs; the attached host sends it right after create); a changed spec refuses the whole call (`editor_mcp_changed`, `refused_servers`), also after a restart; servers not in the record join it; a repeated name keeps the later spec; at most 16 servers / 64 KiB; ACP sessions only; specs in memory, the row keeps fingerprints (env and header values hashed) and the frozen schemas; closed at session end; stdio servers restarted on a workspace move | P19 D1 (`acp-daemon-policy.md`) |
| D-16.640 | Per-session tools on the daemon (w31) | — | Editor tools live in a per-session `SessionRegistry` over the shared one (`editor-<name>`, as on `--local`), invisible to every other session; `SessionService.agent_shaper` (optional seam run before the agent factory on attach and reload) gives a session its registry, toolsets and folder; ACP sessions created without toolsets get `platform_toolsets.acp` or `indus-acp`; a resumed ACP session runs in its stored folder; `no_mcp` is not honoured on the daemon yet (follow-up) | P19 D1 (`acp-daemon-policy.md`) |
| D-16.641 | Deferred session changes on the daemon (w31) | — | Model picks and editor MCP changes made during a turn wait for the turn boundary behind a per-session gate around turn start; picks are kept in memory and re-bound to the agent before each turn when a rebuild (rewind, workspace, a lease-waited reload) lost them; the row's `model` column is not updated (follow-up, as on `--local`) | P19 D1 (`acp-daemon-policy.md`) |
| D-16.642 | Attached ACP against a real daemon (integration of w31 with A2) | — | The attached host's commands go to the daemon (`run_command` → `session.mutate command` → `CommandReply`; a refusal is the command's reply); `tests/e2e/acp/test_acp_daemon.py` drives prompt, model picker, a daemon-answered command and fork through ACP only (the WebSocket fallback is gone); supersedes D-16.602's "owed" | P19 D1 (`acp-daemon-policy.md`), P19-18 (`protocol-drift.md`) |
| D-16.643 – D-16.649 | unused | | | |
| D-16.650 | Shell status strip and pressure banner (P18-25) | A daemon boot id; dismissals per boot | The boot id is the daemon pid from `GET /api/daemon`; a suspected OOM restart is a pid change while the last memory reading was critical, worded "may have"; dismissals live in `sessionStorage["indus.pressure.dismissed"]` as `trigger:boot` keys (a new tab session shows live warnings again); one switch point (`shellStatus.useBootWatch`) when the heartbeat gains a boot id | `docs/notes/P18/page-system.md` |
| D-16.651 | Channels restart banner, profile builder, action logs (P18-23, P18-24, P18-25) | — | The restart banner's mark is `sessionStorage["indus.channels.restart.<profile>"] = {pid}`, cleared by a new pid or running-after-stopped; a webhook's one-time secret never enters the query cache (`gcTime: 0`, component state only); the builder's model / skills / MCP steps are separate calls after the create (a partial result is listed, never rolled back); action logs poll every 1 s, 400 lines | `docs/notes/P18/page-channels.md`, `page-profiles-models.md`, `page-system.md` |
| D-16.652 | Chat host close codes and timers (P18-26) | — | 4409 (superseded) is one muted line with "Use it here" and no automatic reclaim; a clean 1000 close means the session ended (the start-new overlay), not a reconnect; the keep-alive frame goes only after 20 s of silence in both directions; one 8 s budget covers the gated ticket fetch and the handshake | `docs/notes/P18/chat-host.md` |
| D-16.653 | Resume replay sanitiser (P18-26, R09.8; changed at integration) | Erase codes dropped for 30 s after a resume | For 30 s after an explicit or announced resume, each flood — a run of line breaks and erase codes (`ESC [ n K`, `ESC [ n X`) holding 50 or more breaks — folds into one blank line, its erase codes with it; erase codes outside a flood always pass. The builder's filter dropped every erase code in the window, and our Ink renderer repaints each line with `ESC [ 2 K`, so in the browser run a closed approval card stayed drawn and lines overlapped for the first 30 s of every resume or reload (P18-30); the trailing run held back for the next frame now includes erase codes between its breaks | `docs/notes/P18/chat-host.md`, `docs/notes/P18/e2e-m6.md` |
| D-16.654 | Chat rail feed and actions (P18-27) | Rail actions through the RPC sidecar | The feed stops on 4400 as on 4401/4403; the rail's actions type the TUI's own slash commands into the terminal (not `/api/ws`); pasted or dropped images go to `indus-chat-uploads/` under the Files root (no cleanup yet); the feed accepts a bare event or a JSON-RPC notification, whichever the TUI publisher sends | `docs/notes/P18/chat-rail.md` |
| D-16.655 | Dashboard build and bundle (P18-29) | — | Dev backend override `INDUS_WEB_DEV_BACKEND` (dev only); releases run `python -m indus.web.bundle publish` after the SPA build and before `uv build` (P23); no env override for the bundle folder; a checkout's `apps/web/dist` wins over the package's `static/`; the 600 kB chunk budget fails a build only under `CI` | `docs/notes/P18/build-serve.md` |
| D-16.656 | Login page (P18-28; changed at integration) | — | Form-post failures (wrong password, rate limited, provider outage) redirect to `/login?error=<code>`; the page is sent with `Referrer-Policy: same-origin` (and the matching meta), not `no-referrer`: under `no-referrer` Chromium sends `Origin: null` on the form's POST and the gate's cross-site check refused every sign-in (found by the P18-30 browser run; `test_the_form_post_keeps_its_origin`); the referrer still never leaves this origin | `docs/notes/P18/login.md`, `docs/notes/P18/e2e-m6.md` |
| D-16.657 | TUI input: late terminal replies (P12 renderer, P18-30 integration) | — | The renderer's input filter drops complete OSC replies (`ESC ] … BEL/ST`), also split across reads: no key types one, and xterm.js in the dashboard answers the renderer's OSC 11 background probe over the WebSocket after the probe's 150 ms window, which typed `11;rgb:…` into the prompt (and into the message the model got) | `docs/notes/P18/e2e-m6.md` |
| D-16.658 | Dashboard browser suite (P18-30) | — | Playwright (`@playwright/test` 1.56.1, the version whose Chromium revision 1194 is preinstalled where the suite is built; the plan's reference pin 1.63.0 needs a browser this machine does not have) against real `indus dashboard` + `indus gateway run` stacks in temp homes, one per test, driven through a Python harness over JSON lines (`tests/fakes/web_stack.py`; the CLI side through `pexpect`); an opt-in local lane `pnpm web:e2e` and a CI job `web-e2e` in the gate; every URL the browser asks for is checked for the session token, minted tickets and `token=`/`ticket=` queries; the gated run binds `127.0.0.2` (outside the gate's loopback set, still the machine's `lo`) | `docs/notes/P18/e2e-m6.md` |
| D-16.659 – D-16.669 | unused | | | |
| D-16.670 | Renderer CSP connect sources (P20-15; changed at integration) | — | The build-time meta's `connect-src` adds loopback on any port (`ws:`/`http:` on `127.0.0.1` and `localhost`) and `wss:`/`https:`, because the page loads before the boot finds the daemon, the serve port or a remote host. At integration main's header (`CspState`, `RUNTIME_CONNECT_SOURCES` in `electron/security/csp.ts`) carries the same sources beside the exact origins it learns: Electron 44 does apply the header to the `file://` page (the builder's note assumed it did not), and a loaded page keeps the policy it was loaded with, so the header's `connect-src 'self'` refused the first dial to the daemon. Script, frame, object and base rules unchanged | `docs/notes/P20/renderer-shell.md` |
| D-16.671 | Renderer shell, strings and transcript defaults (P20-15, P20-18) | — | `@indus/ui` has no React yet, so the shell's small primitives stay in the desktop; `src/i18n/en.ts` is one flat record and spreads the chat slice's `CHAT_STRINGS` (`src/chat/strings.en.ts`); reasoning blocks hidden until P20-32 reads `display.show_reasoning` (`/reasoning show` toggles live); no OFL font vendored yet | `docs/notes/P20/renderer-shell.md`, `transcript.md` |
| D-16.672 | Boot overlay (P20-16) | — | Reauth is read from `statusCode` 401/403 on the error snapshot; the backend notice comes from the bridge's `boot.onNotice` (`indus:backend:notice`, P20-13; the overlay's guessed bridge names removed at integration) | `docs/notes/P20/boot-overlay.md` |
| D-16.673 | Renderer connection store (P20-17) | Replay parks live frames while a replay runs; answers wait 300 s | One socket per (connection, profile) with its own ladder (full jitter 300 ms / 15 s, reset only after 5 s open, warning after 300 s); replay by `session.subscribe` from the cursor (at most 256 sessions keep a cursor, 10 s per resubscribe), the watchdog probes with `events.since`; answers wait 330 s, liveness probe 5 s; `WsTransport` also accepts the `indus-dashboard-v1` family and requires the server to echo the first protocol offered | `docs/notes/P20/connection-store.md` |
| D-16.674 | The desktop as a viewer (integration of P20-17 with P20-19..22) | — | On every new socket the store sends `client.capabilities {features: ["server_requests", "request.decline"], surface: "gui"}` before replaying its sessions. The brief put `surface: "gui"` on `session.create`, which has no such field: the daemon takes the surface (presence, busy default) from `client.capabilities`, and without the features it offers the window no approval, clarify, sudo or secret card | `docs/notes/P20/connection-store.md` |
| D-16.675 | Who starts the boot (integration of P20-12 with P20-16/17) | — | A main window starts its primary connection at launch: `connection.get` starts or joins main's boot and answers when the backend is ready (`boot.get` only reads progress, whatever the orchestrator note says); a boot failure leaves the store `failed` until the overlay reports ready. A backend announced while the first dial still waits for its descriptor is not a change (no redial, no `lastError`) | `docs/notes/P20/connection-store.md`, `boot-orchestrator.md` |
| D-16.676 | Composer attachments (P20-19) | — | Bytes travel as `POST /api/files/upload {multipart: {path, overwrite: false, file: {name, type, base64}}}` through `indus:api` (main builds the multipart body) into `<session cwd>/.indus/attachments/<id>-<name>`; picker paths go as paths only in local mode (a one-byte `fs.readText` is the path guard until P20-29 adds a check); before main described the backend the mode counts as remote (bytes) | `docs/notes/P20/composer.md` |
| D-16.677 | Prepared-submission journal (P20-20) | — | Channel shapes owned by P20-20 (`prepare {requestId, sessionId, payload}`, `retire {requestId, receipt {outcome, admissionId?}}`, entries with `state` and `updatedAt`); caps 256 KiB per payload and 200 live entries | `docs/notes/P20/journals.md` |
| D-16.678 | Busy actions and the queue (P20-21) | — | Stop with inputs waiting pauses the queue in this window: the daemon has no pause, so this window's queued inputs are withdrawn and held until Resume (a daemon `admission.hold` is a P17 follow-up); the drain gives up on an item after 4 attempts | `docs/notes/P20/busy-actions.md` |
| D-16.679 | Approval, clarify, sudo and secret cards (P20-22; integrated with P20-17) | — | Arming delay 250 ms; a request without `choices` offers once / session / deny; "always" is not confirmed twice. The cards take `OpenServerRequest` = the store's pending request with `answer` bound to `ServerRequestStore.answer(id, …)`, so first-answer-wins stays in the one store (`bindAnswers`, `useOpenServerRequests`, `GatewayConnectionProvider` / `ServerRequestStoreProvider` for tests) | `docs/notes/P20/approval-cards.md` |
| D-16.680 | Model picker and status bar (P20-23) | Sticky model keyed by connection and profile | Storage keys `indus.desktop.model.<profile>`, `…model-presets`, `…model-favourites`, `…model-default-checked.<profile>`, `…statusbar.items`; sticky by profile only until P20-26's registry; the profile default read with `config.get model.default`, written with `config.set model`; the status bar sits beside the shell's connection badge | `docs/notes/P20/model-status.md` |
| D-16.681 | Desktop slash commands (P20-24) | — | The catalogue is the registry's `desktop` surface (`slashCatalog.json`, `dump-slash-catalog --check`) plus `EXTRA_HIDDEN` (the TUI's own local commands) | `docs/notes/P20/slash.md` |
| D-16.682 | The person's message and Retry (integration of P20-18 with P20-19) | — | No daemon event carries a prompt's text, so the composer adds an optimistic transcript row before `prompt.submit` (`src/chat/localEcho.ts`): bound to its turn when the turn started, removed when queued (the queue panel shows it), steered or refused. Retry on a failed turn sends that turn's message again as a new input (new request id). One `ChatActions` per (connection, session) (`sharedActions`) serves the fresh draft's first send, the composer that then shows the session and Retry; journal recovery skips inputs that object is still sending or has settled (the first message of a new chat was offered back as "Not confirmed" while it ran). Slash output lines show above the composer (last 5) until the transcript gains local notices | `src/chat/localEcho.ts`, `src/app/chatSlots.tsx` |
| D-16.683 | Backend resolver and installer (P20-08, P20-09) | — | Ladder: source root, the unpackaged app's own checkout, `INDUS_DESKTOP_INDUS`, the managed install `<home>/indus-agent/.venv`, the POSIX launcher `~/.local/bin/indus`, else bootstrap; a checkout without `.venv` is skipped; the installer's stage exits are retried 3× (500 ms) and the orphan ledger records installer stage hosts only; the release base default is an owner decision (§3.2, D-16.690) | `docs/notes/P20/backend-resolver.md`, `installer-runner.md` |
| D-16.684 | Local attach and WS tickets (P20-10; changed at integration) | — | One serve token per launch held in main (a serve from an earlier launch answering 401 is stopped and re-ensured once); `stale_socket` restarts the daemon once, other mint failures ensure again; the ticket answer names the exact `protocols`. Inside Electron the upgrade's `Sec-WebSocket-Protocol` is not shown to `onBeforeSendHeaders` (and a rewritten one is ignored — checked in Electron 44.5.1), so a local dial's reservation is matched by window and exact URL (the oldest live one) to strip `Origin`; the daemon still redeems the single-use ticket | `docs/notes/P20/local-attach.md`, `electron/backend/tickets.ts` |
| D-16.685 | Remote connection (P20-11) | — | The HTTP leg checks `GET /api/config` (a 404 also counts as authenticated); the URL is stored in `remote-connection.json`, the token as the `remote-connection-token` secret. The WebSocket leg (main swaps a reservation id for the token in `Sec-WebSocket-Protocol`) does **not** work inside Electron: the header cannot be rewritten there (integration check). Remote chat was owed until P24-29 replaced the swap with a per-dial ticket (D-16.785; §3.2 D-16.691) | `docs/notes/P20/remote-connection.md` |
| D-16.686 | Boot orchestrator (P20-12) | — | Retry clears only the start latch (an installer failure needs Repair); at most 3 repairs per process; a stale daemon is restarted only on `stale_socket` or version skew; `INDUS_DESKTOP_SKIP_QUIT_PROMPT=1` for tests | `docs/notes/P20/boot-orchestrator.md` |
| D-16.687 | Backend supervision (P20-13) | — | Watch every 2 s; the event `indus:backend:notice` (restarted / crashing with its last output) is shown by the boot overlay | `docs/notes/P20/supervision.md` |
| D-16.688 | REST proxy in main (P20-14) | — | Startup paths `/api/status`, `/api/health`, `/api/config`, `/api/config/schema`, `/api/profiles`; the proxy refuses `/api/files/download-ticket` and `/api/auth/ws-ticket` | `docs/notes/P20/rest-proxy.md` |

The transcript store now reads `GET /api/sessions/{id}/messages` through main's REST proxy for the window's profile and flattens it like the daemon's display projection (user/assistant/tool rows; tool-call-only rows dropped; tools named by their call); `session.history` is the fallback where the route is missing; a session opened before the connection was ready loads when it becomes ready | `src/chat/restHistory.ts`, `src/chat/transcriptStore.ts` |

Per kind (finished turn, approval) the renderer gates the notice; main's master switch is on while either is. The tray toggle stays switchable; switched on without a host, the row says why | `electron/ambient/prefsBridge.ts`, `src/ambient/AmbientHost.tsx` |

| D-16.837 | Traps ledger complete (P25-11) | — | Every research R-id (261) has a ledger row; a placeholder `Review:` row warns in the everyday check and fails with `--strict`; an accepted review reads `Review: accepted by the lead <date>: <why> (<owner>)` and only the lead writes one; `--strict` runs in the release gate only | `docs/notes/P25/traps-complete.md` |
| D-16.838 | Python performance budgets (P25-12) | — | Every number in `tooling/perf/budgets.toml`; `tests/perf/` carries `perf` (the serial lane); `tooling/perf/baseline.json` ships empty and is recorded on the CI runner (`INDUS_PERF_RECORD`); the store-append budget is measured on turn-sized batches of 50 rows per commit (one commit per row costs ~2.9 ms because SQLite 3.45.1 makes the store fall back to DELETE journaling — a store finding if the budget is meant per row) | `docs/notes/P25/perf-python.md` |
| D-16.839 | TypeScript performance budgets (P25-13) | — | The TUI budgets are an asserting vitest file under its own config (`pnpm --filter @indus/tui bench`), not `vitest bench`; the web budget is checked on the production build (`apps/web/scripts/check-bundle.mjs`, gzip level 9, 1 kB = 1,000 bytes); desktop cold start is a vitest e2e spec that skips without a packaged build and a display | `docs/notes/P25/perf-ts.md` |
| D-16.840 | Similarity closest regions (P25-18) | — | `similarity --closest N --report` ranks regions by the sum of their runs' scores and writes `docs/internals/similarity-report.md` with an empty verdict per region; verdicts are the lead's or the owner's | `docs/notes/P25/similarity-closest.md` |
| D-16.841 | Built-artefact sweep (P25-19) | — | `tooling.checks artifacts --artifact PATH …` reads folders, ASAR archives (our own reader), `docker save` tars layer by layer (config and labels included) and zip-family installers; banned names and hosts, reference media hashes and the building machine's home folder fail; text members over 32 MB are path- and hash-checked only; `artifacts_allow.json` takes member globs with a dated reason | `docs/notes/P25/artefact-sweep.md` |
| D-16.842 | Parity runner (P25-05) | — | Terminal and stdio flows run through our transcript driver (`indus --local`, pinned `INDUS_TERMINAL_ID`); browser flows go through REA's browser capture and come back with `--import` (the runner holds no MCP client); the gateway flow drives the real `indus gateway` against a Bot API stand-in; one flow at a time, the runner stops only its own process groups; captures in the git-ignored `.cache/parity/`; desktop flows are the owner's lane (packaged app + REA connect mode) | `docs/notes/P25/parity-runner.md` |
| D-16.843 | Scripted model server (P25-06) | — | stdlib `http.server` speaking Chat Completions streaming; the scripts are code (`approve_or_decline` and `clarify` branch on the tool result) and the model id is the script name on both apps (a `/<script>/v1` prefix for clients that rewrite ids); pacing waits on a stop event, no sleeps | `docs/notes/P25/scripted-model.md` |
| D-16.844 | Parity scenarios (P25-07) | — | Twelve `parity/<flow>.indus.json` files; `data-testid` and `data-ui` both count as our hooks (new ones use `data-ui`); `wait_for_timeout` is refused; the approval flows run `git reset --hard HEAD` in a scratch project; nine desktop hooks are owed (`scenarios.OWED_HOOKS`, to add as `data-ui` now that P24 has landed) | `docs/notes/P25/parity-scenarios.md` |
| D-16.845 | Obligation ledger and comparison (P25-08, P25-09) | — | 18 obligations, one manifest binding each; `rules.json` starts empty and gets rules only from measured, classified divergences; our `ledger.py --ledger` report is the progress bar until REA's ledger build has both sides' Evidence; `compare.py` kinds `step-only-in-<side>`, `step-failed-in-<side>`, `role-only-in-<side>` (at `wait_for` steps only), `page-errors`, accessible names not compared | `docs/notes/P25/obligation-ledger.md`, `parity-compare.md` |
| D-16.846 | CI and release wiring for P25 (integration) | — | PR gate: `ledger.py --check` + `runner.py --all --dry-run` in `checks`; `pnpm --filter @indus/tui bench` in `ts`; `check-bundle.mjs` after the browser suite's production build in `web-e2e`; a new `artefacts` job (needs `web-e2e` + `desktop-package`, both upload their build) sweeps the web dist, the unpacked Linux desktop build, a strict docs-site build and the three installers; `docker.yml`'s build job sweeps each saved image (names, hosts, home folder; without the reference, so no media hashes: docker files never spell the reference's location). Release gate only: `acceptance traps --strict` after `uv sync --locked --all-extras` (it fails by design until the Review rows are resolved) | `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `docker.yml`, `release.yml` |

| D-16.849 | sherpa-onnx licences (integration, P24-20) | — | `sherpa-onnx` 1.13.8 is `Apache-2.0 AND LGPL-2.1-or-later` (its Linux wheels bundle alsa-lib's `libasound` as a shared library) under a dated exception with the LGPL obligation note extended; `sherpa-onnx-core` 1.13.8 is `Apache-2.0 AND MIT` (bundled ONNX Runtime); both manual rows; owner ruling joins the open licence items | `scripts/release/notices_data.json`, `tooling/checks/licenses_allow.json` |

| D-16.860 | Multiplex in `gateway run` (P24-01..04 wiring, WA) | one daemon with per-profile tables | With `gateway.multiplex_profiles: true` the launch profile's `GatewayDaemon` runs `decide_second_run` before its per-home instance lock (attach 0 / 75 supervised, transient 75, permanent 78 / 1), claims the host lock and record, and serves every live profile of the root at start (including `default` when launched from a named profile), each as an embedded per-profile `GatewayDaemon` (`parent` set; own store, session service, authorizer, adapters, control socket and instance lock) bound with `ScopeBinder`; one `AdapterTable`, `ProfileRouter.identify` picks the pipeline, replies go through the bot that received the chat; served profiles stop before the host's own adapters | `docs/notes/P24/multiplex-host.md` |
| D-16.861 | Served profiles' routes, HTTP and locks (WA) | — | Profile routes come from the launch profile's config only; a served profile's HTTP surfaces (API server, webhooks) need the launch profile's listener and are mounted at `/p/<profile>/` on it (`SharedIngress`); served profiles use the host's credential-lock directory; the root-level parts (task boards, group rooms: `ROOT_PART_FACTORIES`) run once, in the host | `docs/notes/P24/shared-ingress.md`, `multiplex-host.md` |
| D-16.862 | Room loop turn policy (P24-06 wiring, WA) | — | `rooms/loop.py`: lease → recover → expire turns → admit → start → member turn in the member's profile → settle / stop → prune; the mentioned member answers first, else the first member, then they alternate; at most 8 member turns per person message; silence, a failure, a stop directive or a newer person message ends the chain; a stop holds until the member is mentioned again; no replay after a restart; an unserved member is `member.unavailable`; each member turn runs in a fresh session with the recent transcript; approvals inside room turns have no answerer yet and room events are not pushed (`groups.log` only) | `docs/notes/P24/room-driver.md` |
| D-16.863 | Relay front in the daemon (P24-08/09 wiring, WA) | — | `RelayPart` builds the front and starts it with retry and backoff; one adapter per descriptor through `GatewayDaemon.attach_relayed` (an enabled native adapter keeps its platform; a relayed platform still needs its registry entry); the connector's interrupt stops the running turn; idle → `go_dormant`; fix: `RelayFront.hold` / `release` so events replayed right after the handshake are not acked and dropped before the adapters attach | `docs/notes/P24/relay-adapter.md` |
| D-16.864 | Idle exit with rooms and relays (WA) | — | A running room turn counts as busy for idle exit; a platform served only through a relay connector does not keep the daemon up (the front goes dormant instead); `DaemonPart.busy_reason` lets a part report its own reason | `docs/notes/P24/room-driver.md` |
| D-16.865 | LSP tier per process (P24-15/16 wiring, WB) | one language-server manager per session | `service/lsp_host.LspUpkeep`: one tier per process shared by the session services through a user count, started at the first attach, trusting each session's git work tree, stopped when the last user closes; servers get the starting profile's child env (no provider key reaches a server); tests run with no language servers (root conftest `no_language_servers`); release before a worktree is removed (P11) and `indus lsp restart` for a running daemon are follow-ups | `docs/notes/P24/lsp-client.md` |
| D-16.866 | Stop gates in the agent loop (P24-17..19 wiring, WB) | — | `core/loop._handle_answer` asks `SessionRuntime.stop_gates` after the intent check; `stop_gates_lsp.standard_gates` = LSP gate, verify-on-stop in the session's terminal env, pre-verify from `AgentOptions.pre_verify` (override with `AgentOptions.stop_gates`); subagents get the standard gates without pre-verify, the review fork none; a non-durable gate's continuation writes the interim answer and the nudge as scaffold rows; when the budget runs out the gated answer is the reply | `docs/notes/P24/lsp-diagnostics.md`, `verify.md` |
| D-16.867 | Bot and peer tools in the daemon (P24-07, 10 wiring, WB) | — | `gateway_tool_registry` registers `message_agent` and `a2a_call` beside the Feishu / Yuanbao tools (built-in tool modules may not import the gateway, R02.26; answers D-16.791's owed item); the chat service installs `LocalBotTransport` for its profile (undone at stop); daemon sessions get `delivery_origin` from the admission pipeline's origin source (cron and board tools deliver to the chat). Owed: canonical bot-chat turns with the `bots` toolset (until then `message_agent` is registered but no session offers it) | `docs/notes/P24/bots-and-peers.md`, `a2a.md` |
| D-16.868 | Preview automation over server requests (P24-25 wiring, WB) | — | `preview.act` / `preview.read` are server-request kinds (`PREVIEW_REQUEST_KINDS`, generated for TypeScript); the generic `server_requests` feature means the person-answered kinds only (`PERSON_REQUEST_KINDS`), preview kinds are named explicitly; the preview tools are offered only in sessions a desktop window created, which routes them to its preview pane; the dev-server port comes through the broker | `docs/notes/P24/preview-annotate.md` |
| D-16.869 | Suggestion offers from reviews; project join at create (P24-14, 45 wiring, WB) | — | `service/review_offers`: a background review offers suggestions only from blueprints of skills that review wrote; offers on a connected integration are owed (no "connected" event) and the gateway runs no background review yet; `SessionService.create` joins the project of the session's folder (the WebSocket's duplicate join removed) | `docs/notes/P24/suggestions-blueprints.md`, `projects.md` |
| D-16.870 | `voice.listen` (P24-20 wiring, WC) | the terminal records in its own process | A declared protocol method served by the TUI bridge and the daemon (`service/voice_rpc`): one take on the answering process's microphone (the one the wake word released), the REPL voice rules (moved unchanged to `tools/builtin/media/voice_take.py`), then the profile's speech-to-text; deadline 3,900 s; the TUI's `wake.detected` hands off to it, sends the transcript as the prompt and always resumes the wake word; `/wake on` waits 190 s (`Rpc.call` `timeoutMs`) | `docs/notes/P24/wake-word.md` |
| D-16.871 | Wake word default engine (P24-20, applies D-16.800) | openWakeWord | `wake_word.engine` defaults to `sherpa` (sherpa-onnx, the `wake-sherpa` extra; models supplied by the user, none shipped); `openwakeword` stays selectable where it installs; Porcupine not offered | `src/indus/foundation/config/schema.py` |

| D-16.874 | `indus dashboard --console`; canonical session in `profiles.list` (P24-27, 36 wiring, WC) | — | `--console` sets the started backend's `console_enabled` (a reused machine backend keeps its own setting and the command says so); profile rows carry `canonical_session`, read without creating a store (a broken store answers null) | `docs/notes/P24/admin-console.md`, `management-pages-2.md` |
| D-16.875 | Native-app OAuth broker not built (P24-29, WC) | — | The server-side RFC 8252 broker (`/auth/native/*`, `native_pkce` in `/api/status`) is a security surface of its own and is not built in the wiring wave; the desktop keeps the token-mode fallback (D-16.589 stands) | `docs/notes/P24/connections.md` |
| D-16.876 | `self-chat` holders in tests (integration) | — | Root cause of the order-dependent `test_a_cli_pass_can_deliver_to_the_own_bot_chat`: two `DeliveryRouter`s in `tests/unit/gateway/adapters/test_targets.py` were never closed, so their hold on the process-wide `self-chat` deliverer outlived them; they now close, and the root conftest's autouse `no_self_chat_holder_left` (`tests/self_chat_guard.py`) fails any test that leaves a router open and releases its holds; no product change (every product router is closed by its owner) | `tests/conftest.py` |
| D-16.877 | Attach for served profiles (integration; amends D-16.542) | attach through the host | `bind_daemon_scope(..., served=True)` accepts the routed scope of the daemon that serves exactly that profile inside a multiplex host (its home is the ticket book's profile, its secret store inside that home); `WsEndpoint(served=)` passes it and the served daemon sets it, so a served profile has its own attach endpoint and `indus` / the TUI attach through its control socket the normal way; any other routed scope is still refused | `docs/notes/P24/multiplex-host.md` |

| D-16.929 | Owner inputs to the gates (P25-21) | — | `INDUS_REF` (clean room, acceptance, traps, similarity) and `PARITY_REF` (the owner's reference captures, read as `${PARITY_REF:?…}`) come from the environment; a missing input fails its gate with a message, never a silent pass | `scripts/release/gates.txt` |

| D-16.931 | Gate run records (P25-21) | — | `<work>/gates.txt` holds one `<gate> exit=<n> <s>s` line per gate (also printed), `<work>/logs/<gate>.log` each gate's output, `<work>/run.txt` the repository, full commit, gates file, start, finish and failed count, `<work>/setup.log` the clone output | `docs/notes/P25/release-gates.md` |
| D-16.932 – D-16.934 | unused | | | |
| D-16.935 | Release-lane end-to-end tests (P25-22) | — | A `release` pytest marker: the test runs only when `INDUS_RELEASE_LANE=1` (release.yml's `ci` job sets it on its test step) and skips with that reason elsewhere, so the PR gate stays fast; POSIX only | `docs/notes/P25/rehearsal.md` |
| D-16.936 | Canary → stable rehearsal, source half (P25-22) | — | Two releases built from the checkout's own tracked source tree (canary and stable named by `scripts/release/versioning.py`), tagged in a temp bare `origin`; the canary installed with the real `install.sh` stages (`INDUS_INSTALL_REPO_URL`), the dependencies step done offline (`uv sync --frozen --no-dev`, `UV_OFFLINE=1`, the running interpreter as `UV_PYTHON`) and the saved extras set to `[]` because the lane's uv cache holds the base set only; the extras path stays with release.yml's `install-e2e` and `upgrade` lanes; data written through the canary's own CLI and read back by the stable | `tests/e2e/upgrade/test_canary_to_stable.py` |
| D-16.937 | Post-release smoke scripts (P25-23) | — | One self-contained script per OS; everything in one throwaway folder with its own HOME (Windows: its own Indus home; the user PATH entry and the per-user desktop install are removed again); the fake-model endpoint is the release's own `scripts/parity/scripted_model_server.py` from the installed checkout; one `<step> PASS\|FAIL\|SKIP <detail>` line per step, exit 1 on any FAIL, 2 on usage; `--dry-run` fetches nothing; a `--workdir` that holds files is refused (the folder is removed after a clean run) | `docs/notes/P25/smoke-rollback.md` |
| D-16.938 | Smoke "turn with an approval" (P25-23) | — | `indus -z` auto-approves by design, so the smoke uses a finite `chat -q` without a terminal: it passes when the risky command did not run and the run either refused it (`--local`, `approvals.single_query_mode`) or left the approval waiting for a terminal (attached to the daemon); the interactive approval is the person's check (`--interactive`) | `scripts/release/smoke/linux.sh` |
| D-16.939 | Smoke secrets and the keychain (P25-23) | — | The smoke sets `security.secret_store: file` so nothing reaches the machine's keychain; the keychain survival check is a manual row of the rehearsal log | `docs/internals/rehearsal-log.md` |
| D-16.940 | Smoke desktop checks (P25-23) | — | Licences: `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` inside the package (AppImage unpacked, DMG mounted read-only, Windows per-user install) — the app has no licences screen (follow-up); launch: alive after 20 s, the chat confirmed by a person with `--interactive`, otherwise SKIP; macOS `codesign` + `spctl` and Windows Authenticode are their own `signature` step (FAIL until P23-15/16 sign) | `docs/notes/P25/smoke-rollback.md` |
| D-16.941 | Rollback (P25-23) | — | Release back to draft, image aliases back to the previous receipt's digests, the channel record edited by hand (the controller refuses backward moves), fix forward with the next patch; no tag, version or digest is ever deleted or reused; source installs follow tags, so no switch reaches them and the sheet gives the per-person way back | `docs/internals/rollback.md` |
| D-16.942 | Smoke steps pinned before a release exists (P25-23) | — | A release-lane test sources the shell smoke script and runs its post-install steps on a locally built stable release installed in the installers' default layout; only `dashboard-login` may fail there (a release tree built in the test has no `apps/`, so no dashboard pages) | `tests/e2e/upgrade/test_smoke_steps.py` |

| D-16.950 | Windows `local` backend (P25-24) | — | Git Bash runs every command; same backend name and flags; the registry opens it when the OS family is `win32`; best effort (D-16.55) | `docs/notes/P25/windows-local.md` |
| D-16.951 | Git Bash lookup (P25-24) | — | `INDUS_GIT_BASH` (refused when missing) → installer portable Git `<home>/tools/git-*` (newest) → the Git of `git.exe` on PATH → `known_dirs.git_for_windows_roots` → `bash.exe` on PATH; System32/SysWOW64/Sysnative/WindowsApps launchers and MinGit never count | `docs/notes/P25/windows-local.md` |
| D-16.952 | Paths (P25-24) | — | cwd and Python paths native; scripts MSYS; `pwd -P` converted back; drive, UNC, `\\?\`, `/cygdrive/`, Git-root and `/tmp` rules; `shell_path` / `native_path` on the environment; MSYS argument conversion off unless the user set it | `docs/notes/P25/windows-local.md` |
| D-16.953 | Temp dir on Windows (P25-24) | — | `terminal.temp_dir` (existing) → `<home>/cache/terminal` with forward slashes (pruned once per process) → the system temp dir; never `TMP`/`TEMP`/`TMPDIR` | `docs/notes/P25/windows-local.md` |
| D-16.954 | Script delivery (P25-24) | — | each command's script is a 0600 file in the temp dir run as `bash [-l] <file>` (32,767-character command line, MSYS command-line globbing); removed once the process has exited, leftovers at cleanup | `docs/notes/P25/windows-local.md` |
| D-16.955 | Stopping (P25-24) | — | suspended start, own job object, resume; stop and force-stop both terminate the job at once (no grace on Windows); no kill-on-close, so a finished command's background jobs live on | `docs/notes/P25/windows-local.md` |
| D-16.956 | UTF-8 (P25-24) | — | `PYTHONUTF8`, `PYTHONIOENCODING`, `LANG` defaults; the command's hidden console set to code page 65001 when `chcp.com` exists; UTF-8 decoding with replacement | `docs/notes/P25/windows-local.md` |
| D-16.957 | Child PATH (P25-24) | — | Git Bash's `mingw64/bin`, `clangarm64/bin`, `mingw32/bin`, `usr/local/bin`, `usr/bin`, `bin` (those that exist) first, then Indus's own bin dir when missing, then the user's PATH without repeats (compared without case or trailing separators) | `docs/notes/P25/windows-local.md` |
| D-16.958 | Windows CI lane (P25-24) | — | `windows-local` job runs `tests/unit/backends/test_local_windows.py` on `windows-latest`, best effort (`continue-on-error`) until it has been green | `docs/notes/P25/windows-local.md` |

| D-16.964 | Matrix undecryptable-notice test waits on its condition (integration, test only) | — | Root cause of the full-gate failure of `test_an_undecryptable_event_gives_one_notice_per_session_and_no_event`: the parked event counts syncs, and each sync and the notice's send wait on the io pool's real-time worker thread, while the test gave a fixed four long polls with 5 ms real waits; under CPU load (reproduced 1 in 15 with eight busy processes) the third notice had not been sent yet. The test now polls until the notice count is reached (bounded, 40 polls); 0 in 25 under the same load; no product change | `tests/unit/gateway/adapters/matrix/test_crypto.py` |

| D-16.970 | One language-server tier per served profile (wave 45, S45, SEC-14) | — | `LspUpkeep` builds and joins the tier of its own profile (keyed by the profile home): its `LspManager` uses that profile's child env and `lsp` settings; checkers are installed per tier key and `diagnostics.active()` answers the tier of the edit's profile (the scope `LspToolHooks.before` binds from the call, else the running scope; with neither, the process-wide checker or the only one); a profile without a tier gets none, never another profile's; the stop gate and `indus lsp status` ask the scope's tier | `src/indus/service/lsp_host.py`, `src/indus/tools/lsp/diagnostics.py` |
| D-16.971 | Room transcripts framed as data (wave 45, S45, SEC-15) | — | in a member's turn prompt each member reply follows a `name (member):` line inside `core.untrusted`'s fence (new `untrusted.wrap`: same fence, any length), a person's further lines are indented, speaker names lose control characters and colons; the lead-in says how to read it | `src/indus/gateway/rooms/loop.py`, `src/indus/core/untrusted.py` |

The only non-browser credential gated mode takes, the service bearer on its opted-in routes (`INDUS_DASHBOARD_DRAIN_SECRET`), is decided by `BearerSeam` before the check; the session token stays refused in gated mode | `src/indus/web/auth/gate.py` |

| D-16.980 | `message_agent` in canonical bot-chat turns (P45, finishes D-16.867's owed item) | — | A turn in the profile's canonical chat (the row titled `Bot chat`, or the live tip its lineage leads to) runs with platform `bots` and the profile's selection for it: `platform_toolsets.bots` when saved (`indus tools`; the ACP-only `no_mcp` marker dropped), else `indus-bots`. Applied at attach, before the MCP and plugin steps, by `SessionService(bot_chats=service.bot_turns.BotChats(...).shape)`; the gateway daemon gives it to its chat service and the API server's service (a peer's `/v1/bots/dm`), never the webhook surface; hosts without a bot transport (CLI in-process, TUI bridge, ACP) do not register `message_agent`, so a local delivery to a profile with no running daemon gets no `message_agent` in the target's turn | `docs/notes/P24/bots-and-peers.md` |

The classic CLI's in-process tier is not asked on `/worktree prune` (its servers in a removed tree are reaped once the folder is gone) | `docs/notes/P24/lsp-client.md` |
| D-16.983 | `indus lsp restart` reaches the running daemon (P45, D-16.865 follow-up) | restart in the CLI's own process | Owner-only control verb `lsp-restart` (`service.lsp_control`, `lsp_host.restart_servers`), asked first; then this process's tier, if any. No protocol method: the plan names none and the control socket is the CLI's door to its daemon (`stop`, `maintenance-run`). Entry points only (`restart_servers` / `release_servers` in `lsp_host`); S45's per-profile tier changes their lookup in one place | `docs/notes/P24/lsp-client.md` |

| D-16.990 | Taint granularity (wave 46 a, SEC-4) | — | A tainted result is fenced whole: the entire `execute_code` / `delegate_task` result, not only the part a script printed from an RPC answer (over-fencing only labels text as data) | `docs/notes/P25/security-review.md` |
| D-16.991 | Kernel taint is sticky per owner (wave 46 a, SEC-4) | — | A session kernel that ran an untrusted tool keeps its owner (session, or a child's owner) tainted until `reset`, across that owner's kernels; with `kernel: off` each run stands alone | `docs/notes/P25/security-review.md` |
| D-16.992 | Short or failed untrusted runs count (wave 46 a, SEC-4) | — | `read_untrusted` is set by an untrusted tool that started even when its result was under `MIN_CHARS` (not fenced itself) or an error, so a child summary built on it is fenced | `docs/notes/P25/security-review.md` |

| D-16.994 | `exclude-newer` at the latest the age rule allows (wave 46 b, RM8) | — | `exclude-newer` = lock date − 14 days (2026-10-08 for the 2026-10-22 lock), `pins_meta.json` lock date 2026-10-22; a pull request reads `pins_meta.json` from its base, so this lands on main directly | `docs/notes/P25/deps-audit.md` |
| D-16.995 | pynacl override over discord.py's cap (wave 46 b, RM8) | — | `[tool.uv] override-dependencies = ["pynacl==1.6.2"]` over discord.py 2.7.1's `<1.6` (moderate advisory); one line plus `uv lock` to revert; dropped when discord.py allows 1.6 | `pyproject.toml`; `docs/notes/P25/deps-audit.md` |

| D-16.1024 | Room demo waits for the stalling request (integration, test only) | — | Root cause of `test_a_room_with_two_members_taking_turns` failing under load: a task reads `running` when the driver claims it, before the member's turn asks the model, so `groups.stop` could cancel alpha's third turn before its request; beta's later request then took the scripted 10,000 s stall. The test now also waits for the third model request (30/30 under load, was 4 failures in 15) | `tests/integration/gateway/multiplex/test_room_two_members.py` |
| D-16.1025 | Dual-host allow-list loses `cli.q.denies` (integration, test only) | — | Wave 46's F-5 fix (D-16.1010) makes an attached finite `chat -q` deny an approval like the in-process host, so the matrix's `cli.q.denies/outcome` entry (D-16.487) was stale and failed the matrix; removed from `tests/fixtures/service/dual_host_allowlist.yaml`. D-16.487 (the daemon-side single-query flag) stays the fuller follow-up; done in wave 47 (D-16.1051 – D-16.1053), and the matrix still needs no `cli.q` entry: both hosts now decide on the same `--local` route | `docs/notes/P17/dual-host-matrix.md` |
| D-16.1026 – D-16.1029 | unused (waves 45 + 46 integration) | | | |
| D-16.1030 | Desktop busy state follows the generation (wave 47 a, screenshot bug 1) | — | The composer's Stop/Steer state is keyed by the generation (turn ids seen in `turn.started`; the newest ended generation clears it), not by the admission's turn id; the admission id is only a fallback `turnId` until `turn.started` names the turn. No protocol change | `docs/notes/P20/busy-actions.md` |
| D-16.1031 | One permanent session listener per connection and session (wave 47 a) | — | `sharedActions` keeps one session-event listener for its lifetime per (connection, session), so sends made with no composer mounted still see their terminal events; the per-composer subscription stays only for actions passed as a prop (tests, embeds) | `docs/notes/P20/busy-actions.md` |
| D-16.1032 | Tool arguments on `HistoryMessage` (wave 47 a, screenshot bug 2) | — | Additive optional protocol field `HistoryMessage.arguments` (the call's parsed arguments, masked as `tool.started` masks them) rather than each surface re-deriving them; `"{}"` comes through as `{}` (no Arguments section, as live) | `docs/notes/P20/transcript.md` |
| D-16.1033 | REST messages mask tool-call arguments for every client (wave 47 a) | — | `/api/sessions/{id}/messages` now masks tool-call arguments (`redact_arguments`) for the web SPA too; before, it sent them as stored | `docs/notes/P20/transcript.md` |
| D-16.1034 | A session's own folder anchors host tools (wave 47 b, screenshot bug 3) | — | A session's `workdir` beats the profile's `terminal.cwd` for relative paths on the local backend only (`with_workdir`); a sandbox (docker, ssh, cloud) reads `terminal.cwd` inside it and keeps it | `docs/notes/P05/file-ops.md` |
| D-16.1035 | Stored folder on attach-again only for person surfaces (wave 47 b) | — | A stored session attached again gets its stored folder only when its source is `cli`, `tui`, `gui`, `acp` or `web` and the folder differs from the host's own; messaging chats keep the host's folder and `terminal.cwd` | `docs/notes/P05/file-ops.md` |
| D-16.1036 | A missing chat folder falls back silently (wave 47 b) | — | A folder that is not an existing absolute directory leaves the scope unchanged (tools use `terminal.cwd`, else the process folder); no refusal and no notice yet (follow-up) | `docs/notes/P05/file-ops.md` |
| D-16.1037 | Chat folders add no attack surface (wave 47 b) | — | `session.create` already accepted `cwd` from an authenticated client and file tools already took absolute paths; only the relative-path anchor moves; no threat-model row cites it | `docs/notes/P05/file-ops.md` |
| D-16.1038 | `session.list` over the daemon lists the whole profile (wave 47 c, screenshot bug 4) | — | An attached connection's `session.list` lists every stored conversation of the served profile (as `--local` and `-c/-r` do); only session-addressed calls are limited to attached sessions (B-DAEMON-5 evidence adds `tests/contract/gateway/test_ws_pickers.py`) | `docs/notes/P17/ws-endpoint.md` |
| D-16.1039 | Picker reads shared in `indus.service.picker_reads` (wave 47 c) | — | `complete.slash`, `model.options` and `session.list` are served by the daemon's WS table and the TUI bridge from one service module (the gateway may not import `tui_bridge`); D-16.549 partly closed | `docs/notes/P17/ws-endpoint.md` |
| D-16.1040 | Next daemon-served TUI reads (wave 47 c, follow-up) | — | Same pattern next: `complete.path` (needs the session's backend and cwd), `commands.catalog`, `config.get` (read-only; `bridge.settings` then narrows to `config.set`). Done in wave 48 b (D-16.1072 – D-16.1074): all three are served by the daemon's WS table from `indus.service` (`path_completion`, `client_settings`, `picker_reads.command_catalog`) with the same answers as `--local`; `bridge.settings` keeps only `config.set` | `docs/notes/P17/ws-endpoint.md` |
| D-16.1041 | Light/dark through the colour-scheme report protocol (wave 47 d, screenshot bug 5) | — | DSR 996 / report 997 and mode 2031 are the one channel for light/dark changes between the dashboard's xterm (answers from `data-theme`) and the TUI (`followTerminalScheme`); an environment-pinned theme is left alone; terminals that speak it natively behave the same | `docs/notes/P12/themes.md` |

| D-16.1045 | Cron scripts on Windows through Git Bash only (wave 47 e, RL26) | — | Shell scripts run in Git Bash (MSYS path, `git_bash_env`), every script in a job object; no Git Bash refuses the run (no PATH `bash`, which may be the WSL launcher) | `docs/notes/P25/windows-local.md` |
| D-16.1046 | `execute_code` on Windows: per-call scripts only (wave 47 e, RL26) | — | The kernel's fd passing has no Windows form; the launcher refuses `pass_fds` with the WSL2 message | `docs/notes/P25/windows-local.md` |
| D-16.1047 | Shared Windows helpers in `local_windows` (wave 47 e) | — | `git_bash_env` (PATH plus UTF-8/MSYS defaults, also used by `run_env`), `wsl2_refusal`, `start_windows_process(..., separate_stderr=)` | `docs/notes/P25/windows-local.md` |
| D-16.1048 | `indus update --finish` fetches pages for every non-developer checkout (wave 47 f, F-3) | — | Phase `web_pages` runs `python -m indus.web.bundle fetch` as a child (300 s, scope env; the CLI does not import `web`, D-16.246) whether or not a stamped tree exists, so a failed first fetch heals on the next update; off a release the child answers 3 after one local `git tag` (no network) | `docs/notes/P23/update-finish.md` |
| D-16.1049 | Pages stamped for another release are not served (wave 47 f) | — | `locate()` reports kind `stale` and the server answers "not built" until a fetch or build, rather than serving with a warning: those pages talk to an API they were not built for | `docs/notes/P18/build-serve.md` |

| D-16.1051 | Attached `-z` also sends `single_query` (wave 47 g, D-16.487) | — | `-z` sends `single_query` and `unattended` (its `--local` run is single-query with auto-approve), so attached it asks nobody too: a clarify answers "undelivered" instead of detaching with exit 3 | `docs/notes/P17/cli-attach.md` |
| D-16.1052 | A single-query turn asks nobody on the daemon (wave 47 g) | — | `asks_nobody` is the whole broker for that turn, not a filter on viewers: other surfaces watching the session are not asked while the one-shot turn runs (as `--local` passes no answerable kinds); their next turn is asked as usual | `docs/notes/P17/cli-attach.md` |

| D-16.1084 | Wire models build on first use (wave 48 e, RM25) | — | `ProtocolModel` sets `defer_build=True`: validators and serializers build when a model is first used; fields, descriptions and the generated schema unchanged (`codegen --check` clean) | `docs/notes/P25/perf-python.md` |

| D-16.1091 | Wire-model field docs from a generated twin (wave 48 g, RM25) | — | `ProtocolModel` drops `use_attribute_docstrings`; `__pydantic_init_subclass__` sets the same descriptions from `protocol/field_docs.json` (per-module sha256; a changed or missing module is parsed once); all 238 models' descriptions and `model_json_schema()` byte-identical (`python -m indus.protocol.field_docs`, `--check`) | `docs/notes/P25/perf-python.md` |
| D-16.1092 | One-shot runs skip Docker, cron and the content scanner imports (wave 48 g) | — | Liveness answers move to `backends/docker/liveness.py` (`reaper.set_liveness` re-exported), the in-process host imports `CronStore` / `cron_refs` in `start()`, `project_trust` imports `safety.content_scan` on first scan | `docs/notes/P25/perf-python.md` |

| D-16.1094 | Slash commands the daemon refuses (wave 48 h) | — | `slash.exec` / `command.dispatch` are served (`service/slash_commands.py` shared, `service/daemon_slash.py`: title / undo / retry / branch through `session.mutate`, `/compress` under the session lock when idle, `/stop` and `/steer` by generation); `/queue`, `/yolo`, `/reasoning`, `/fast` and quick `exec` commands are refused (catalogue `unavailable`) until `config.set`'s session keys and a daemon-side `shell.exec` come to the daemon. Since wave 50 only `/queue` is refused: `/yolo`, `/reasoning`, `/fast` run on the daemon (a, D-16.1155) and quick `exec` commands through `service/host_shell.py`, with the daemon's `shell.exec` (b, D-16.1157 – D-16.1160) | `docs/notes/P17/ws-endpoint.md` |

| D-16.1096 | Attached `/title` before the first reply answers "Title set" (wave 48 h) | — | The daemon's rename writes the row at once, so it answers "Title set: X" where the bridge says "(saved after the first reply)" | `docs/notes/P17/ws-endpoint.md` |
