| D-16.1111 | Our own codesign walk (wave 49 a) | — | Mach-O files found by magic, signed deepest first with the hardened runtime and our entitlements, the app last; no `@electron/osx-sign`, nothing new installed | `docs/notes/P23/sign-mac.md` |
| D-16.1112 | The sealed payload digests are the ASAR integrity hashes (wave 49 a) | — | `ElectronAsarIntegrity` in `Info.plist` is recomputed from the archives right before the outer signature and rewritten only when stale | `docs/notes/P23/sign-mac.md` |
| D-16.1113 | macOS signing is all or nothing (wave 49 a) | — | No secret set → unsigned with one line naming them; a partial set stops the build naming what is missing; the config's mac `identity` stays `null` unless `dist-mac.mjs` set `INDUS_MAC_SIGNING=1` and `CSC_LINK` | `docs/notes/P23/sign-mac.md` |
| D-16.1114 | Three entitlements (wave 49 a) | — | `allow-jit`, `allow-unsigned-executable-memory`, `device.audio-input`; one file for the app and its nested code; no camera, no `disable-library-validation` | `docs/notes/P23/sign-mac.md` |
| D-16.1115 | `dist-mac.mjs` is the mac release entry (wave 49 a) | — | `release.yml` `desktop-mac` runs it with plain node in the `release` environment; dependencies install in an earlier step without secrets; secrets reach only the build step under their own names | `docs/notes/P23/sign-mac.md` |
| D-16.1116 | The DMG is not notarised on its own (wave 49 a) | — | The app inside is notarised and stapled (Gatekeeper checks it offline); the update ZIP is made from the stapled app; DMG notarisation is a follow-up if the owner wants it | `docs/notes/P23/sign-mac.md` |
| D-16.1117 | Windows / Linux packaging hooks live in `apps/desktop/scripts/` (wave 49 b) | — | As D-16.1110: `sign-win.mjs`, `dist-win.mjs`, `checksums.mjs`, `channel-identity.cjs` with `.test.ts` tests | `docs/notes/P23/sign-win-linux.md` |
| D-16.1118 | One Windows signer, armed by `dist-win.mjs` (wave 49 b) | — | electron-builder's own resource edit and signing off (`signAndEditExecutable: false`); `armWindowsSigning` keeps the secret values and deletes them from the environment before electron-builder runs; a complete Azure Trusted Signing group wins over a PFX, a partial group is refused; signtool's child gets only the Azure credential names | `docs/notes/P23/sign-win-linux.md` |
| D-16.1119 | Every PE we ship is signed once (wave 49 b) | — | `.exe`, `.dll` and `.node` in the packed folder are signed once after the fuses (a ledger); vendor-signed Electron DLLs are re-signed with ours; the installer and uninstaller go through the same signer | `docs/notes/P23/sign-win-linux.md` |
| D-16.1120 | Per-arch Windows installers (wave 49 b) | — | One NSIS installer and one portable ZIP per arch (x64, arm64; `buildUniversalInstaller: false`); the ZIP stays (D-16.84) | `docs/notes/P23/sign-win-linux.md` |
| D-16.1121 | Canary desktop identity (wave 49 b) | — | From `INDUS_DESKTOP_CHANNEL` at config time: app id `ai.indus.desktop.canary`, product `Indus Canary` (install folder, userData, `Indus Canary.app`), Linux `indus-desktop-canary`, feed channel `canary`; an unlabelled build is a canary | `docs/notes/P23/sign-win-linux.md` |
| D-16.1122 | Desktop update feeds per channel on GitHub Releases (wave 49 b) | — | electron-builder's `github` provider for `RELEASE_REPOSITORY` read from `electron/releaseBase.ts`; nothing published from the build; Windows keeps `verifyUpdateCodeSignature: false` and the manual update owner until a signed build's publisher name is known. Since wave 50 e (D-16.1171 – D-16.1173) Windows update signature verification is on (`verifyUpdateCodeSignature: true`, `publisherName` from `packaging/msix/identity.json`; a signed build refuses the placeholder); the update owner stays `manual` until the first signed run | `docs/notes/P23/sign-win-linux.md` |
| D-16.1123 | The `.deb` maintainer is the RM12 placeholder (wave 49 b) | — | `Indus Agent maintainers <maintainers@indus.invalid>` until the owner names an address | `docs/notes/P23/sign-win-linux.md` |
| D-16.1124 | Azure Trusted Signing through signtool `/dlib` (wave 49 b) | — | The client package 1.0.53 is fetched by the Windows lane on every run (`continue-on-error`, no secret in that step); `INDUS_AZURE_SIGNING_DLIB` is set in the build step only when the Azure endpoint secret exists; to verify on the first signed run | `docs/notes/P23/sign-win-linux.md` |
| D-16.1125 | Windows build stamp in the version resource (wave 49 b) | — | `stampWindowsExecutable(context, stamp)` writes the version resource and icon into the pristine `electron.exe` in `afterExtract`, with `IndusBuildVersion`, `IndusBuildChannel`, `IndusBuildCommit` as on macOS (merged into `after-extract.mjs`, D-16.1144) | `docs/notes/P23/sign-win-linux.md` |
| D-16.1126 | Termux canary spelling (wave 49 c) | — | Our tag grammar (P23-21): `v1.2.4-canary.<stamp>` → Debian `1.2.4~canary.<stamp>-1`; `+canary` is refused; canaries sort between the previous stable and their own in dpkg order | `docs/notes/P24/termux.md` |
| D-16.1127 | Termux repository layout (wave 49 c) | — | Suites `indus-stable` / `indus-canary`, component `main`, architecture `aarch64`, package `indus-agent`; pool path built from the control fields, never the input file name | `docs/notes/P24/termux.md` |
| D-16.1128 | The Termux repository accumulates (wave 49 c) | — | An existing `Packages` in the suite is read back so stable and canary versions live side by side, sorted by package then dpkg version (our comparator) | `docs/notes/P24/termux.md` |
| D-16.1129 | `repo.py` / `build.sh` exit codes (wave 49 c) | — | 0 signed, 2 bad input or refused, 3 Release unsigned (the lane fails on 3: an unsigned channel is never kept) | `docs/notes/P24/termux.md` |
| D-16.1130 | Termux builder base digest placeholder (wave 49 c) | — | `builder.Dockerfile` carries `sha256:` + 64 zeros marked owner-to-fill; `build.sh` refuses it; the pins guard's new rule 6 (Dockerfile bases by digest outside `tests/`) warns on the marked placeholder instead of failing | `docs/notes/P24/termux.md` |
| D-16.1131 | Termux runtimes from Termux's own packages (wave 49 c) | — | The builder image installs bionic `python`, `nodejs-lts`, `uv`; their file lists are copied into the payload with a version manifest; wheels are built in the container and retagged `android_24_arm64_v8a` (ABI `cp314`) | `docs/notes/P24/termux.md` |
| D-16.1132 | The Termux lane is `termux.yml`, dispatch or call only (wave 49 c) | — | Never on push or pull request; the signing key by name from the `release` environment (`TERMUX_APT_SIGNING_KEY`, `TERMUX_APT_SIGNING_PASSPHRASE`); wired into `release.yml` as an optional lane at the integration (D-16.1143, D-16.1145) | `docs/notes/P24/termux.md` |
| D-16.1133 | The Termux `.deb` stamps `.install_method` = `apt` (wave 49 c) | — | `indus update` reads it as a package install and refuses with the package message; a Termux-specific message (`pkg upgrade indus-agent`) is a follow-up | `docs/notes/P24/termux.md` |
| D-16.1134 | MSIX version quads (wave 49 d) | — | Stable `X.Y.Z.0`; canary `X.Y.H.L` with `H.L` the seconds since 2026-01-01T00:00:00Z of the canary stamp split into two 16-bit halves | `docs/notes/P24/msix.md` |
| D-16.1135 | MSIX packed by our script (wave 49 d) | — | `scripts/release/msix.mjs` packs one `.msix` per arch with `makeappx` from electron-builder's unpacked apps, not electron-builder's `appx` target; the builder config is untouched | `docs/notes/P24/msix.md` |
| D-16.1136 | MSIX signing secret names (wave 49 d) | — | The Windows set shared with P23-16: `WIN_CERTIFICATE` + `WIN_CERTIFICATE_PASSWORD`, or `AZURE_TRUSTED_SIGNING_ENDPOINT` / `_ACCOUNT` / `_PROFILE` + `INDUS_AZURE_SIGNING_DLIB` (the dlib reads `AZURE_TENANT_ID`, `AZURE_CLIENT_ID`, `AZURE_CLIENT_SECRET`); per-arch packages signed before the bundle, the bundle after | `docs/notes/P24/msix.md` |
| D-16.1137 | No vendored MSIX schemas (wave 49 d) | — | The SDK's XSDs are not redistributable; the rendered manifest and feed are checked in code against a rule table | `docs/notes/P24/msix.md` |
| D-16.1138 | MSIX write virtualization off (wave 49 d) | — | `desktop6` with the restricted `unvirtualizedResources` capability, so the app and its daemon share `~/.indus` and `%LOCALAPPDATA%` with the CLI; the Store asks for a justification | `docs/notes/P24/msix.md` |
| D-16.1139 | MSIX release job behind an optional lane (wave 49 d) | — | The slice left `release.yml` alone; the integration added the opt-in `desktop-msix` lane (D-16.1143) | `docs/notes/P24/msix.md` |
| D-16.1140 | App Installer feed URLs (wave 49 d) | — | Stable `…/releases/latest/download/Indus.appinstaller`; canary `…/releases/download/channels/Indus-Canary.appinstaller` on the fixed `channels` release; the bundle URL names `v<version>`; `RELEASE_REPOSITORY` read from `versions.py` | `docs/notes/P24/msix.md` |
| D-16.1141 | MSIX canary keeps the `indus` link scheme (wave 49 d) | — | Both packages register `indus` (the app routes only that today); with both installed Windows asks which opens a link | `docs/notes/P24/msix.md` |
| D-16.1142 | Store bundles need 1.0.0 or later (wave 49 d) | — | Checked before packing; Store bundles are stable-only, unsigned (the Store signs) and never carry the canary identity | `docs/notes/P24/msix.md` |
| D-16.1143 | Optional release lanes (wave 49 integration) | — | `claims.Lane.optional`; `OPTIONAL_LANES` = `desktop-msix`, `termux`; `DEFAULT_LANES` = every other lane (what a claim gets without `--lanes`, so the canary and a plain release never run them); `LANE_REQUIRES` refuses `desktop-msix` without `desktop-win`; `JOB_LANES` gains both jobs; `release.yml` gains `desktop-msix` (packs `desktop-win`'s unpacked apps, kept only when the lane is on) and `termux` (calls `termux.yml`); `gate` needs both, `upload` needs `desktop-msix` | `tests/unit/release/test_publish.py::test_optional_lanes_are_off_by_default_and_gated_when_named`; `tests/unit/ci/test_release_workflow.py` |
| D-16.1144 | One `afterExtract` for macOS and Windows (wave 49 integration) | — | The config runs `after-extract.mjs` (`STAMPERS`: `darwin` from P23-15, `win32` from P23-16) and keeps `afterPack`, `afterSign`, `afterAllArtifactBuild`; one shared comment over the desktop lanes names both signing lanes' secrets | `apps/desktop/scripts/sign-win.test.ts`; `apps/desktop/scripts/builderConfig.test.ts` |
| D-16.1145 | `termux.yml` takes the admitted commit (wave 49 integration) | — | Optional `workflow_call` input `commit`: checked out instead of `refs/tags/<tag>`, then named with the tag locally (never pushed; a tag on another commit stops the build) | `docs/notes/P24/termux.md` |
| D-16.1146 | Checklist and gap text after wave 49 (integration) | — | Phase table P23 23/23 and P24 47/47 built; RM14 code done with the owner's secrets and first signed runs owed; §0 lists every secret name (incl. `APPLE_API_*` and `TERMUX_APT_*`); RL8 / RL9 and Known gaps S5 / S6 partial; R08.22 cites the Windows signing-order tests | `docs/internals/release-checklist.md` |
| D-16.1147 | Packaged-binary lookup follows the channel identity (wave 49 integration) | — | `scripts/fuses.mjs` `findPackagedBinaries` knows both channels' product and Linux executable names (from `channel-identity.cjs`: `Indus` / `Indus Canary`, `indus-desktop` / `indus-desktop-canary`), and the Electron E2E specs find the packaged Linux binary through `packagedLinuxBinary` in `e2e/fixtures.ts`; before, a local (unlabelled, so canary, D-16.1121) `--dir` package failed `verify-fuses` and the packaged smoke | `apps/desktop/scripts/fuses.test.ts`; `apps/desktop/e2e/core/packaged.electron.test.ts` |
| D-16.1148 – D-16.1149 | unused (wave 49 integration) | | | |
| D-16.1150 | One module for `config.set`'s checks and writes (wave 50 a) | — | `service/config_writes.py` holds the keys, validation, messages and profile writes; the bridge's `methods_config` delegates to it (its contract tests unchanged) and keeps what is per view (pending model, its viewer's tool-progress mode, its projector); the daemon's `config.set` uses the same module | `docs/notes/followups/w50-a.md` |
| D-16.1151 | Attached `verbose` is the connection's own mode (wave 50 a) | — | `config.set verbose` with a session on the daemon sets this connection's mode for that session (`Subscriptions.set_tool_mode`), read back by `config.get` on the same connection, dropped when it lets the session go, not saved; `session.info.tool_progress` stays the profile's (W48-h-6). The daemon does not yet trim tool events by that mode (follow-up: a per-viewer projection) | `docs/notes/followups/w50-a.md` |
| D-16.1152 | Attached model switch is `session.mutate`'s `model` (wave 50 a) | — | A switch while a turn runs is `deferred` and applies when the next turn starts (the bridge: as soon as the running turn ends); the mutation's context-window check answers `CONFIRM_REQUIRED` too, besides `tui.confirm_models`; a refusal answers `PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE` with the mutation's message | `docs/notes/followups/w50-a.md` |
| D-16.1153 | Attached session switches change the shared session (wave 50 a) | — | `yolo`, `fast`, `reasoning` change the session for every viewer; each applied switch puts a `session.info` into the session's events (every viewer's status bar); `reasoning` is applied under the session's mutation gate | `docs/notes/followups/w50-a.md` |
| D-16.1154 | A person's `/yolo` outlives an unattended turn's switch (wave 50 a) | — | While a `-z` turn holds the run-scoped auto-approve, the person's choice wins and the turn's end no longer turns it off (`DaemonSessionHost.set_auto_approve`); the row is written off the loop first; a session with no row yet keeps the switch live only, as in `--local` | `docs/notes/followups/w50-a.md` |
| D-16.1155 | `/yolo`, `/reasoning`, `/fast` are shared server commands; daemon `/status` counts the queue (wave 50 a) | — | `slash_commands.HANDLERS` run them through `SlashHost.set_switch`; `DAEMON_REFUSED` keeps `/queue` only and the daemon's catalogue lists the three `server`; `SlashHost.queued` is async and the daemon's `/status` shows `Queued messages: N` from the session's durable queue (every viewer's, the running input excluded), where the bridge counts its viewer's own queue | `docs/notes/followups/w50-a.md` |
| D-16.1156 | Attached `config.set` needs no protocol change (wave 50 a) | — | `config.set` was already declared (catalogue, generated TS, field docs); codegen and the twins are unchanged by this slice | `docs/notes/followups/w50-a.md` |
| D-16.1157 | One definition of the owner's shell command (wave 50 b) | — | `service/host_shell.py` holds the refusals, the child environment, the runner and the output clean-up for both hosts; the bridge's `methods_shell` delegates (no behaviour change; the gateway may not import `tui_bridge`); the `shell` exception in `security_allow.json` moved with the code | `docs/notes/followups/w50-b.md` |
| D-16.1158 | The daemon runs `shell.exec` for any ticketed connection, on the host (wave 50 b) | — | Every attached client passed the ticket check and is the owner (B-DAEMON-2); no rule is looser than `--local` (dangerous / hardline refused with `SHELL_REFUSED`, env from the builder, 30 s on the scope's clock, redaction before the tail cut); a named session must be one this connection attached (`SESSION_NOT_FOUND`); the command runs on the host, not through the profile's sandbox backend, as the bridge does (owner question below, §3.2) | `docs/notes/followups/w50-b.md` |
| D-16.1159 | Folder order for the daemon's shell commands (wave 50 b) | — | The session's folder, else the profile's `terminal.cwd`, else the daemon's own folder (the bridge's order; its last step is the bridge's folder) | `docs/notes/followups/w50-b.md` |
| D-16.1160 | `shell.exec` declares `SESSION_NOT_FOUND`; quick `exec` runs on the daemon (wave 50 b) | — | Protocol row gains `SESSION_NOT_FOUND` and the `session_id` doc (generated TS, schema, field docs and reference regenerated); `DAEMON_QUICK_EXEC` removed, quick `exec` commands are `server` in the daemon's catalogue and offered by `complete.slash` (W48-h-2's quick `exec` part closed) | `docs/notes/followups/w50-b.md` |
| D-16.1161 | Native Windows refuses a trailing `&` (wave 50 c) | — | On native Windows with a host-local terminal backend `plan(foreground_only=True)` refuses a trailing `&` with a foreground hint (WSL2 / Docker / SSH for servers) instead of turning it into a background job; POSIX and Windows sandbox backends unchanged | `docs/notes/followups/w50-c.md` |
| D-16.1162 | Native Windows caps an over-limit timeout (wave 50 c) | — | There a foreground timeout above `terminal.max_foreground_timeout` is capped at it and the result carries `timeout_capped`, instead of a promotion to a background job | `docs/notes/followups/w50-c.md` |
| D-16.1163 | Windows foreground guidance texts (wave 50 c) | — | There the foreground refusals (inline `&`, nohup / disown / setsid, servers and watchers) name WSL2 or a Docker / SSH backend instead of `background=true` (`WINDOWS_GUIDANCE`); `docs/user/getting-started/windows.md` gains "Foreground only" | `docs/notes/followups/w50-c.md` |
| D-16.1164 | The w47 starter test waits on a real signal (wave 50 c, tests only) | — | `test_windows_starter_boots_and_stops_the_tree` waits for the shell's and the child's pid lines (15 s deadline) instead of sleeping 0.5 s; the `GroupJobs` stand-in counts the pipe again before calling it closed (a second race); 20x clean, also under CPU load | `docs/notes/followups/w50-c.md` |
| D-16.1165 | Termux update message names `indus-agent` (wave 50 d) | — | `indus update` on an `apt` install refuses with `pkg upgrade indus-agent` (the package's real name, as the install guide says), not `pkg upgrade indus` | `docs/notes/followups/w50-d.md` |
| D-16.1166 | `apt` is the Termux install kind (wave 50 d) | — | The `.install_method` stamp `apt` (only `scripts/termux/build.sh` writes it, and it wins over `.git`) is its own install kind with a Termux / `pkg` message; exit 1 like every other managed kind | `docs/notes/followups/w50-d.md` |
| D-16.1167 | Termux build: `repo`, `validate`, `sign` stages (wave 50 d) | — | `repo` assembles unsigned, `validate` checks the tree with `repo.py validate`, `sign` signs (`repo.py sign`); `repo.py build --key-file` still does both in one call and validates before signing, so no path signs an unchecked tree; `build.sh` has 11 stages | `docs/notes/followups/w50-d.md` |
| D-16.1168 | `repo.py` exit 4 for an invalid repository (wave 50 d) | — | New exit 4 "the built repository is invalid" beside 0 / 2 / 3 (D-16.1129); problems are collected, not first-only | `docs/notes/followups/w50-d.md` |
| D-16.1169 | What `validate` checks (wave 50 d) | — | Release, index, pool and stanza fields; every listed package's pool file by size and digest; only the tag's own package opened for its payload layout. The in-container install / upgrade proof needs docker and aarch64 (follow-up for the first lane run) | `docs/notes/followups/w50-d.md` |
| D-16.1170 | Desktop runtime identity from the build stamp (wave 50 e) | — | `electron/identity.ts` derives the app name and the Linux launcher id from the stamp's channel (canary `Indus Canary`, `ai.indus.desktop.canary` with its own .desktop and icon; stable untouched); a test holds it equal to `channel-identity.cjs` for every channel; `desktopName` staged per channel | `docs/notes/followups/w50-e.md` |
| D-16.1171 | Windows update publisher from `identity.json` (wave 50 e) | — | `win.signtoolOptions.publisherName` is read from `apps/desktop/packaging/msix/identity.json` (`stable` / `canary` `publisher`), the one place the certificate subject is configured; `verifyUpdateCodeSignature: true` | `docs/notes/followups/w50-e.md` |
| D-16.1172 | A signed Windows build refuses the placeholder publisher (wave 50 e) | — | `dist-win.mjs` refuses a signed build while the publisher is `OWNER-TO-FILL` (as `msix.mjs` does); unsigned builds keep the placeholder, which fails closed | `docs/notes/followups/w50-e.md` |
| D-16.1173 | Windows update owner stays `manual` (wave 50 e) | — | The stamp's update owner on Windows stays `manual` until the first signed run (owner item); only the config half (publisher, verification on) lands | `docs/notes/followups/w50-e.md` |
| D-16.1174 | Config defaults twin (wave 50 f, RM25) | — | `foundation/config/defaults.json` keyed by sha256 of `schema.py` + `versions.py` (generator `python -m indus.foundation.config.defaults_twin`); section models `defer_build`; `validate()` checks only the top-level settings that differ from their defaults (per-setting adapters), the whole-model check kept as the oracle and for alias spellings; a missing or stale twin falls back to building the defaults. Any edit to `schema.py` (comments too) or `versions.py` needs a regeneration | `docs/notes/followups/w50-f.md` |
| D-16.1175 | Shipped-skill front-matter twin (wave 50 f, RM25) | — | `learning/skills/shipped_frontmatter.json` keyed by the sha256 of each front-matter block (not the path), so edited and user skills always parse; hits are deep copies; generator `python -m indus.learning.skills.frontmatter_twin` | `docs/notes/followups/w50-f.md` |
| D-16.1176 | Four twins in the `generated_twins` guard (wave 50 f) | — | Both new generators join `tooling/checks/generated_twins.py` (pricing, field docs, config defaults, skill front matter); the guard's test fixture copies `skills/`; `biome.json` skips both new JSON files (D-16.1102); perf budgets unchanged | `docs/notes/followups/w50-f.md` |
| D-16.1177 | Ledger text after wave 50 (integration) | — | D-16.549 closed (D-16.1150 – D-16.1156), D-16.1094 (only `/queue` refused now), D-16.1122 and W49-b-6 (Windows update signature verification on, D-16.1171), `docs/notes/P17/ws-endpoint.md` (`config.set` and `shell.exec` served), threat model B-DAEMON-5 (`config.set` session switches only for an attached session); each W50 label in its note carries its D-id | `docs/plan/phases/LEAD-NOTES.md` |
| D-16.1178 | Checklist text after wave 50 (integration) | — | RS30 / RS33 follow-ups closed (`config.set` served attached); RL26 (`plan()` foreground on native Windows done; left the `windows-local` real-child lane and a native background port); RL8 (Termux `indus update` message done); RM25 (the wave 50 f measurements); §0 Windows publisher also feeds the update check; ids and counts unchanged | `docs/internals/release-checklist.md` |
| D-16.1179 | A room watcher added during a read waits for the next poll (wave 50 integration) | — | Found by the gate (`test_watchers_get_every_new_room_event_in_order_once` failed 1 in 20 alone, on a tree no wave-50 slice touched): a connection that called `groups.watch` from an earlier cursor while a poll's read ran off the loop took that page's later events and its cursor skipped the earlier ones. `RoomWatch._poll_room` now gives a page read from `since` only to watchers whose cursor is at least `since`; the others get every event, in order, on the next poll (the watch's nudge makes it immediate) | `tests/unit/gateway/rooms/test_watch.py::test_a_watcher_added_while_a_read_runs_misses_no_event` |
| D-16.1180 – D-16.1189 | unused (wave 50 integration) | | | |
| D-16.1191 | Web look: tokens, presets, fonts (wave 51 a, W51-a-1..3) | — | Web tokens live in `apps/web` (`src/theme/`, `index.css`): two preset colours (canvas, ink) with every other colour mixed from them; presets Deep Teal (dark, default), Deep Teal Large, Clear Blue (light), Graphite, our names; open fonts only from `@fontsource` at exact pins (Archivo, EB Garamond, JetBrains Mono, Courier Prime; OFL-1.1 on the licence allow-list); `@indus/ui` unchanged | `docs/notes/ui-parity/web-foundation.md` |
| D-16.1192 | Web shell and Sessions as the worked example (wave 51 a, W51-a-4..6) | — | Sessions has an Overview (recent cards) and a History view (rows with actions, search, paging); the sidebar status block reads our daemon status and active sessions; the language button offers English only (D-16.51) | `docs/notes/ui-parity/web-foundation.md` |
| D-16.1193 | Sessions page (wave 52 a, W52-sc-1..3, -6) | — | No shared-metrics consent strip (no telemetry, D-16.49; the pressure banner keeps the strip look); prune runs its dry run by itself (on open and 300 ms after an edit); "Import sessions" shows disabled ("Not available yet", no import endpoint); "Recent sessions" shows the 5 newest of the filter (our `is_active` stays on after a CLI exits); with no sessions the page shows the empty state instead of the recent card | `docs/notes/ui-parity/web-sessions-chat.md` |

| D-16.1208 | Button labels in capitals (wave 53 web integration; reversed by D-16.1270) | — | The foundation `Button` sets its label uppercase for every tone but `ghost`, as the specs measure the reference's buttons; `caps={false}` for the mixed-case ones (Keys "Show advanced"); the source text and accessible names are unchanged, so tests that find buttons by name stay as they are | `docs/notes/ui-parity/web-foundation.md` |
| D-16.1209 | Shell tests outside the time budget; one test per route (wave 53 web integration) | — | Root cause of the App / ThemeMenu / Webhooks vitest timeouts (also on the base): the tests are CPU-bound, not waiting — each full-shell render costs 0.1–0.4 s idle (React development element checks, jsdom 29 computing styles for every role query) and the first visit to a lazily routed page evaluated its module graph inside the test (about 1.7 s for the whole table); with several suites on 4 cores the same work took 3–5 times as long and crossed the fixed 5 s test and 1 s `waitFor` budgets. `src/testing/renderApp.tsx` loads every routed page while the test file is imported (15–20 % less test time for the shell tests), and "resolves every route" is one test per route (19 mounts were one 2.5 s test); slice f's 20 s timeout on the docs test is removed; no timeout raised. e2e: the four waits for the "Recent sessions" heading (`auth.spec.ts` ×3, `auth-gates.spec.ts` ×1) wait for "No sessions yet" instead — on a fresh stack the card shows only while the list loads and then gives way to the empty state (D-16.1193), so they passed only when the browser caught the loading moment (`auth.spec.ts:76` failed 1 of 2 full runs, `:114` 3 of 3) | `apps/web/src/testing/renderApp.tsx` |
| D-16.1210 | Desktop parity tokens (wave 51 b, W51-b-1, W51-b-10) | Their own token set and colour values | Our `--desk-*` token names grouped by role (`bg-*`, `fg-*`, `stroke-*`, `row-*`, `accent*`, `status-*`, `code-*`, sizes), light and dark values measured from screenshots (never copied); `--indus-color-*` mapped onto them; the dark primary button uses dark text (#151617 on #4a84fe) as measured | `docs/notes/ui-parity/desktop-foundation.md` |
| D-16.1211 | Desktop fonts (wave 51 b, W51-b-2) | Their bundled UI and wordmark fonts | Inter (`@fontsource/inter@5.3.0`) for the UI and Abril Fatface (`@fontsource/abril-fatface@5.3.0`), both OFL-1.1 and bundled (no network fetch, CSP `self`), for an "INDUS AGENT" wordmark drawn narrower with `scaleX`; no reference artwork | `docs/notes/ui-parity/desktop-foundation.md` |

### 2.8 Not in 1.0

Parts of P0/P1 inventory rows that 1.0 ships without. The acceptance map lists these under
"Known gaps". A row built later moves to §2.7.

| ID | Feature | Indus 1.0 | Why not in 1.0 |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-16.41 | Reading or importing a reference home (store, exports, memory, skills) | Not supported; users start fresh | D-16.2; clean-room simplicity. Optional one-way importer later (owner) |
| D-16.45 | Curated plugin catalog and optional-MCP catalog | Plugins install from a git URL with a full 40-char SHA pin or a local dir; MCP servers are configured by hand or via `indus mcp add` | A catalog is a governance process (review, hosting), not code (07 Q14). Owner decides if we run one |
| D-16.46 | Out-of-process plugin host | Plugins run in-process behind allow-list, static scan and consent (same trust model the reference documents as "not a sandbox") | Security posture call for the owner (07 Q12); recommended post-1.0 |
| D-16.47 | Separate native setup installer app | The desktop app's in-app installer covers first run | Second app to build, sign and ship for one flow (08 Q10) |
| D-16.48 | Light (remote-only) desktop variant | One desktop variant that can connect local or remote | Packaging matrix cost (08 Q11) |
| D-16.49 | Telemetry and install receipt upload | No telemetry; local install receipt only | Privacy default; nothing to upload to |
| D-16.50 | Import from another agent product's home (OpenClaw migration) | Not built | Migration tool for a third-party product; low value for a new product |
| D-16.51 | Non-English locales (16 catalogues) for CLI, TUI, gateway, dashboard and desktop; docs translation | English only, with the catalogue mechanism and the key-parity check live on every surface | Translation must be written fresh; owner decides languages and workflow (10 Q13) |
| D-16.15 | Termux APT channel, MSIX/Store | Built in wave 49 (P24-21, P24-22) as optional release lanes `termux` and `desktop-msix`, off by default and never run (D-16.1143); no Store submission | P5 ("later"); signing keys, the builder digest, store accounts and a first green run are the owner's |
| D-16.12 | Vendor portal provider and its commands | Not built (P3) | Product decision |
| D-16.11 | Impersonating subscription OAuth | Not built (P2) | Legal/product decision |
| D-16.85 | Legacy single-query runner script with minimal flags (inventory O9) | Not built; `indus -z` and `indus chat -q` cover one-shot runs | A second entry point duplicates the CLI; no user need |
| D-16.86 | Home Assistant messaging adapter | Not in the bundled adapter set; can come later as an external catalogue plugin through the P13 plugin API | Integration with a home-automation server is outside the gateway core; better owned as a plugin |
| D-16.87 | Teams meeting pipeline (joining and transcribing meetings) | The Teams chat adapter ships (P16); the meeting pipeline does not | Separate media pipeline and vendor permissions; low priority for 1.0 |
| D-16.88 | Desktop "cloud" connection kind (a hosted portal connection) | Local and remote (SSH/URL) connections only | Tied to the dropped portal provider (D-16.12) |

## 3. Decisions

Date for every row below: 2026-10-18. "Lead" = the build lead; "owner" = the product owner.
Rows marked **open — owner** need the owner's answer; each carries our recommendation so the build
can proceed on it until answered (builders treat the recommendation as provisional and keep the
change cheap).

| D-16.40 | Trademark and licence review of "Indus Agent"; wording of the MIT credit in `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` | Keep the name; run a trademark search before public launch; credit the reference's copyright line verbatim in notices only | partly settled — owner chose **MIT** for Indus Agent (2026-10-18); trademark search still open |

| D-16.55 | Windows scope (04 Q10, R09.19) | Tier 1: WSL2 for CLI, TUI, gateway; native Windows for the desktop app and the PowerShell installer; native-Windows terminal backend (Git Bash) best-effort in P25 | **settled — owner, 2026-10-18** (as recommended) |
| D-16.56 | Versioning scheme (04 Q4) | SemVer starting at 0.x during the build, 1.0.0 at release; canary builds as `x.y.z-canary.<ts>` with channel order from the channel record (R10.26) | accepted — lead, 2026-10-18 (recommendation; owner may override) |
| D-16.57 | `update` scope in 1.0 (04 Q7) | Stable and canary channels, transactional update with rollback, restart of this profile's gateway; no fleet-wide restarts or one-off channels | accepted — lead, 2026-10-18 (recommendation; owner may override) |
| D-16.58 | Release artefact hosting (10 Q9) | GitHub Releases + GHCR for images in 1.0; object storage with channel records later | accepted — lead, 2026-10-18 (recommendation; owner may override) |
| D-16.59 | Trajectory format consumers (10 Q10) | Our own documented JSONL format, with think blocks applied consistently; no promise of byte-equality with the reference | accepted — lead, 2026-10-18 (recommendation; owner may override) |
| D-16.60 | Default tokenizer and summariser for trajectory compression (10 Q11) | A permissively licensed tokenizer pinned by revision, remote code off; summariser = the configured auxiliary model | accepted — lead, 2026-10-18 (recommendation; owner may override) |
| D-16.61 | REA registration (README Stage 1) | Approve, so the parity pass and the "needs REA" questions can be answered | **settled — owner approved, 2026-10-18**; rea-agents 6.7.0 registered, doctor healthy |
| D-16.62 | Default turn (iteration) cap (01 Q1) | Finite default of 150 iterations with the grace summary call; `0` means unlimited | accepted — lead, 2026-10-18 (recommendation; owner may override) |
| D-16.63 | Model-visible tool names (02 Q10) | Keep plain functional names (`terminal`, `read_file`, `patch`, …) since they describe behaviour and help models; rename every branded or vendor-prefixed tool | accepted — lead, 2026-10-18 (recommendation; owner may override) |
| D-16.64 | Docker default image (02 Q1) | Publish our own slim sandbox image (and a browser/desktop variant for browser and computer-use tools); never default to the reference's image | accepted — lead, 2026-10-18 (recommendation; owner may override) |
| D-16.690 | Where the desktop's in-app installer downloads `install.sh` (P20-09) | The builder guessed `https://github.com/indusagi/indus-agent/releases/download/v<version>/install.sh` (override `INDUS_DESKTOP_RELEASE_BASE`, dev override `INDUS_DESKTOP_INSTALL_SCRIPT`); the repository today is `varunisrani/Indus-agent`. Confirm the release org/repo before P23. Since P23 (D-16.740) the value is one constant, `RELEASE_REPOSITORY` in `src/indus/foundation/versions.py`: change it there, rerun `scripts/install/pins.py update` and `scripts/nix/pins.py`, and `tests/unit/packaging/test_release_base.py` names any copy left behind | open — owner |
| D-16.691 | How the desktop dials a remote Indus (P20-11) | Main cannot put the saved token into the WebSocket upgrade inside Electron (the subprotocol header is neither visible nor rewritable in `onBeforeSendHeaders`), and handing the token to the renderer breaks D-16.18–20. Recommendation: the serve mints a short-lived single-use dashboard ticket for a token-mode client too (`POST /api/auth/ws-ticket` with the session-token header, redeemed as `indus-dashboard-ticket.<t>`), main mints it per dial and the renderer offers it like the local ticket (a P18 + P20 change); alternative: main relays the remote socket itself. Until then remote chat is owed (D-16.685) | **built as recommended** — P24-29 (D-16.785): the serve mints a single-use ticket for a token-mode client, main mints one per dial; verified in a real Electron against a real `indus serve` (`apps/desktop/e2e/core/remote.electron.test.ts`, P24 integration, 2026-10-21); owner may still pick the relay instead |
| D-16.800 | The wake word's default engine (P24-20, D-16.52) | openWakeWord cannot be locked on Python 3.13 (its Linux path needs tflite-runtime, which has no 3.13 wheels), so there is no `wake` extra; `wake-sherpa` (sherpa-onnx, Apache-2.0) ships. Recommendation: make sherpa-onnx the default engine (models trained or supplied by the user, none shipped) and leave Porcupine out (vendor key and vendor terms; a §2.8 row if wanted) | **applied as recommended** — wave 42 (D-16.871): `wake_word.engine` defaults to `sherpa`; the owner may reverse it with one line |
| D-16.801 | Origin of `indus pm`'s ffmpeg (P24-23) | The catalogue fetches a third-party static ffmpeg build (a GPL binary, only ever run as a separate process). Recommendation: keep it as an optional package the person installs on request, or point at the distribution's ffmpeg instead | open — owner |
| D-16.802 | Lightpanda and agent-browser in `indus pm` (P24-39) | Not added to the catalogue at integration: Lightpanda is AGPL-3.0 (not on the allow-list even as a separate process for a managed download) and agent-browser ships through npm only. Recommendation: both stay found on PATH (install hints), no managed download | open — owner |
| D-16.1071 | TUI and web dashboard room views (wave 48 a, W48-a-2; RS3) | Not for 1.0: the desktop's room view (D-16.1070) and the messaging surfaces cover rooms; the TUI and the dashboard have no room view today, so each would be a new surface (the dashboard would also need `groups.*` and `groups.event` on its socket). Record after-1.0 unless the owner wants them | open — owner |
| D-16.1158 | Should an attached `!command` (and quick `exec` command) run through the profile's sandbox backend instead of on the host? (wave 50 b, W50-b-2) | Keep it on the host: it is the owner's own command, typed by the person who passed the ticket check (B-DAEMON-2, B-SHELL-1), with `--local`'s refusals, child environment, limit and redaction; `--local` and the reference run it on the host too. A profile that wants every command sandboxed can be a later opt-in setting | open — owner |
| D-16.1190 | Should the web dashboard and the desktop app look like the reference's, page by page and component by component? (owner, 2026-10-24: "the Indus UI must look like the reference's, same to same, every page and component, web + desktop") | Follow the reference for layout, structure, component set, spacing, density, type style and the light/dark colour scheme; keep the clean room for code and assets (no copied code, CSS, theme files or assets; our own "INDUS AGENT" wordmark in an open font; no reference names in product code or UI text; longer copy in our words); our protocol, APIs and behaviour stay. Overrides §2.5's palette and shape rows and R09.22 for look and layout | **decided — owner, 2026-10-24** (waves 51–53) |

### 3.3 Open — lead (proposed, build proceeds on the proposal)

| ID | Question (source) | Proposal | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-16.26 | API server key on loopback (06 Q13) | Always require a key | accepted — lead, 2026-10-18 |
| D-16.36 / D-16.54 | "every Nd at HH:MM" (07 Q1, R07.10) | Accept as daily/N-daily at that wall-clock time in the configured zone | accepted — lead, 2026-10-18 |
| D-16.52 | Pinning policy (10 Q7, R10.11) | Exact pins for direct deps, bounded ranges only where a library demands it; uv `exclude-newer` 14 days at lock time; full-SHA git sources; CI check enforces exactly this text | accepted — lead, 2026-10-18 (enforced by the `pins` check, P00-09) |
| D-16.65 | Persona and project file names (01 Q5) | `PERSONA.md` in the profile home; `INDUS.md` or `.indus.md` in projects; also read `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, cursor rules | accepted — lead, 2026-10-18 |
| D-16.66 | Prompt guidance blocks and model-family gating (01 Q3) | Keep the idea of per-family guidance, write our own text and our own family lists; golden prompt tests per surface | proposed — lead |
| D-16.67 | MCP serve `events_wait`: push or long-poll (09 Q11) | Long-poll in 1.0 driven by a store change counter; MCP notifications when client support is common | proposed — lead |
| D-16.68 | Dashboard pages in 1.0 (09 Q7) | All pages, since the gateway exists by P18; git/audio/local-model routes ship with the desktop in P20 | proposed — lead |
| D-16.69 | Cross-process turn lease timing (01 Q11) | Built in P17 with the daemon; before that, a simple per-session file lock prevents two in-process surfaces from running one session | accepted — lead, 2026-10-18 |
| D-16.90 | Session-key namespace for the default profile (P15-07, P24-03) | Keys of the default profile use the literal namespace `@default`; named profiles use their profile id | accepted — lead, 2026-10-18 |
| D-16.91 | Per-platform policy variables (06, P15-08) | Product policy env vars follow `INDUS_<PLATFORM>_ALLOWED_USERS` (and siblings), while vendor credentials keep vendor names (D-16.22) | accepted — lead, 2026-10-18 |
| D-16.92 | Loop completion token (P10-25) | Keep the generic `LOOP_COMPLETE` token the model emits to end a `/loop`; it is a plain word, not a brand | accepted — lead, 2026-10-18 |

> Not legal advice. Before shipping under the brand, have counsel review the trademark and licence
> points (D-16.40).
